- **What:** an "Add to calendar" button on each upcoming appointment in the patient app. API: `GET /api/v1/bookings/<id>/calendar` returns a standard `.ics` file (service and clinic, place, start and end in UTC, `CONFIRMED` or `TENTATIVE`).
- **Rules we added:** it needs the same `read:booking` right as viewing the booking (the patient who owns it, or the clinic's staff); a cancelled booking has no file (404); the file holds no patient name, id or message text, and no calendar alarm (the system's own reminder does that job).
- **Limits:** it is a one-time download, not a live calendar feed, so it does not update if the appointment is moved (the patient downloads it again; the same booking id as UID lets most calendars replace the old entry); no doctor name in the file (names are not stored, DEV-024); the file is only checked by tests, not opened in every calendar app.

### DEV-056: download the Reports page as CSV (ADD)

- **What:** a "Download CSV" button on Reports saves the numbers on the page (period, bookings, attendance, busiest services, referrals, waiting lists, messages, response times, bookings per day) as a spreadsheet file `zoiko-report-<from>-to-<to>.csv`, in three columns: section, measure, value.
- **Rules we added:** the file is built in the browser from the numbers already loaded, so no new server call and no new data leaves the system: it holds counts only, exactly as the page does; a service name that starts with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` gets a leading `'` so a spreadsheet can never run it as a formula; an empty response time stays empty, never 0; a byte-order mark is added so Excel reads it as UTF-8.
- **Limits:** the file reflects the period chosen on the page; there is no scheduled or emailed report; only the file-building logic was checked (a script of 11 checks), not an automated test in the repo, because the staff app has no test runner yet.

### DEV-057: daily "running late" email to clinic operations staff (ADD)

- **What:** once an hour the worker checks every active customer. If an organization has bookings waiting for confirmation, or patient messages unopened, for longer than `RESPONSE_TARGET_HOURS` (DEV-054), it queues ONE email per recipient for that day, and the usual mail sender delivers it. The email holds counts and a link to the staff app only: no patient name, message text or booking detail.
- **Assumption (open question, see DECISIONS-NEEDED):** recipients are people holding **R04 (clinic operations)** at that organization whose email address is VERIFIED (they clicked our link). Doctors, patients and unverified addresses never receive it. This was chosen because R04 is the role that confirms bookings and reads messages, not because anyone decided it.
- **Rules we added:** at most one digest per organization per UTC day (the day is in the subject; a queued email with the same subject means it was sent); nothing is queued when nothing is late or nobody has a verified address; another customer's or organization's late work is never counted (per-organization counts).
- **Limits:** needs the worker running (`docker compose restart worker` after this change); it can arrive at any hour (no quiet hours, no per-person opt-out, no digest of anything else); the once-a-day check relies on the queued row, which is kept after sending; if a recipient is added later the same day they get nothing until tomorrow; the opened-message clock is the newest patient message (see DEV-054).

### DEV-058: staff Providers page (ADD)

- **What:** the staff **Providers** page (it was marked "soon") lists the clinic's doctors (people holding R03 at the organization) with, for the next 14 days: confirmed appointments, free times, and when the next appointment starts. API: `GET /api/v1/staff/providers`; it needs the same `analytics` right as Reports (clinic operations).
- **Rules we added:** only confirmed appointments count as appointments; a time with a waiting or confirmed booking is not "free", and a cancelled booking gives the time back; numbers and times only: no patient id or name appears (a test checks this); another customer's doctors and bookings are never shown. Doctors are shown by login until names are stored (DEV-024).
- **Limits:** the page does NOT show qualifications, licences, specialities or facility privileges (not stored, B-phase trust work); the 14-day window is fixed; no way yet to add or remove a doctor from this page (use the Team page); free times are counted per doctor across all services of the clinic.

### DEV-059: read-only staff Settings page (ADD)

- **What:** the staff **Settings** page (it was marked "soon") shows the timing rules the service runs by (response target, appointment reminder lead time, waiting-list offer time) and how many clinic operations people would receive the daily late-work email, with a warning when that is nobody. API: `GET /api/v1/staff/settings`, with the same `analytics` right as Reports.
- **Rules we added:** it is read only: the values come from the deployment's settings (`RESPONSE_TARGET_HOURS`, `REMINDER_LEAD_HOURS`, `WAITLIST_OFFER_HOURS`), and only a NUMBER of recipients is shown, never an address.
- **Limits:** nothing can be changed here. Per-clinic or per-service values, and anyone changing them from the screen, are not built because they need a decision first (DECISIONS-NEEDED 22 and 23) and a place to store them (a new table).

### DEV-060: working search box in all four top bars (ADD)

- **What:** the search box in the staff, patient, doctor and platform apps (it said "Search is not built yet") now works as a quick jump, from one shared component (`frontend/shared/src/QuickSearch.tsx`). In the patient app the last choice is "Find care ...", which opens Find Healthcare with the text already searched. In the doctor app it is "Find my patient ...", which opens My patients with the name already searched; My patients also got its own search box, which filters only the patients the server already lets that doctor see. Typing shows the pages whose name starts with or contains the text (only pages that are built), and "Find patient ..." which opens the Patients page with that name already searched. Enter opens the best page match, or the patient search when no page matches. Escape clears it.
- **Rules we added:** it adds no new server call and no new data: pages are matched against the menu, and patient names are matched by the Patients page on the list it already loads (so it shows only what that page shows to this login); the search text is never sent anywhere or stored; choosing a page from the side menu clears an earlier patient search.
- **Limits:** it searches page names and patient NAMES only (no ids, bookings, messages or referrals); no keyboard arrow selection in the result list yet; in the platform app the last choice is "Find customer ...", which opens Customers filtered by that name (the Customers card also got its own search box; names only, numbers are not searched); only the page matching was checked by a script (8 checks on the shared function), plus type checks and builds of both apps, not an automated test in the repo, and the screen was not opened in a browser.

### DEV-061: staff work queue, the first slice of the workflow module (ADD)

- **What:** the staff **Work queue** page (the menu item "Workflows", which said "soon") lists what the clinic still has to do: appointments to confirm, referrals to decide, patient messages to answer. Each item has a due time (the response target, DEV-054), shows when it is overdue, and can be TAKEN by one staff member so colleagues see who is on it, and given back. New module table `workflow_workitem` (migration `workflow/0001`, row-level security on). API: `GET /api/v1/staff/work` (`?mine=1`, `?unassigned=1`), `POST /staff/work/<id>/claim` and `/release`; the same `analytics` right as Reports.
- **How items open and close:** from the events of the other modules, never by hand: a requested booking, an issued referral or a patient message opens one; confirming or cancelling a booking, deciding a referral, or a clinic reply closes it. A second event for work already open changes nothing. Every ten minutes a worker job (`workflow.sync`) also opens an item for anything waiting that has none (work from before the queue existed, or a lost event); it only opens, closing stays with the events.
- **Rules we added:** an item holds a kind and the id of the booking, referral or thread and NOTHING about the patient (a test checks this); an item can be taken by one person at a time: taking one a colleague holds, or giving back one you do not hold, is refused (409); taking and giving back are written to the audit trail; one open item per piece of work (database constraint); another customer, and another site of the same customer, see and take nothing.
- **Deviation:** unlike the booking, referral and message writes, taking an item does not need a policy receipt (it changes no patient record, only who is on a task): it is guarded by the staff-only check and the `analytics` right, and audited. Say if you want a receipt required here too.
- **Limits:** this is only the work queue, NOT the documented workflow engine (definitions, states and transitions, timers, exception cases, partner handoffs, closure snapshots are not built); waiting-list offers and family requests are not in the queue yet; items cannot be assigned TO someone else, only taken; a message item is timed from the newest patient message on the first sync; the worker must be restarted after this change (done locally).

### DEV-062: download my data (ADD)

- **What:** on Privacy & Consent the patient (or guardian) can **Download my data**: one JSON file with their appointments, referrals, care team, access list, profile details, family links, insurance, waiting list, notifications, and every conversation WITH its message text. API: `GET /api/v1/my/export`.
- **Rules we added:** the file is assembled from the patient's own screens (the same code and the same rights checks), so it holds only what that login can already see, never more; only patient and guardian accounts may ask (staff and doctors get 403, because the same screens would otherwise return the clinic's records for them); reading the messages for the file does NOT mark them read (a dedicated read-only function); every download is written to the audit trail (`data.export`).
- **Limits:** this is the right to a COPY only. Deleting or correcting data, restricting its use, and a request workflow with deadlines are NOT built and need the retention and legal decisions (DECISIONS-NEEDED 2 and the retention item); the file is plain JSON, not encrypted or password protected (the page says so); there is no limit on how often it can be downloaded; it is built in one request, so a person with a very large record could be slow; file contents of a section the person is not allowed to read show `{"available": false}`.

### DEV-063: clinic phone numbers and the patient Help & Support page (ADD)

- **What:** a site (location) can now have a phone number (new column `catalog_location.phone`, migration `catalog/0002`). Staff set it when they add a location, or later with **Edit contact** on the Services page (API: `POST /api/v1/locations/<id>/contact`, which also changes the address). The patient app's **Help & Support** page (it said "not built yet") shows the sites the patient has booked, with address, a tap-to-call phone link, and a button to send a message; the phone also appears in the booking data.
- **Rules we added:** a phone is digits with an optional leading `+` or `(`, spaces, brackets and dashes, at least 5 digits and at most 24 characters; anything else is refused (422), and empty clears it; only clinic staff with the catalog-write right at that site can change it (patients, doctors, other sites and other customers cannot); changes are audited (`location.contact`); a clinic is listed only if the patient has an appointment there.
- **Emergency wording (needs review):** the page says "Zoiko Access is not an emergency service... call your local emergency number straight away". We wrote this text ourselves. It is generic safety wording, but it has NOT been reviewed by a clinical-safety owner and is not localised to any country's number: add it to the clinical-safety review (DECISIONS-NEEDED section 4).
- **Limits:** no opening hours, email or support-desk contact; there is no Zoiko-level support contact yet (the page only lists clinics); a patient with no appointments sees no clinic phone; the phone number is not checked against any real phone format by country; the clinic list logic was checked by a script (5 checks), not an automated test in the repo.

### DEV-064: change my password while signed in (ADD)

- **What:** a signed-in person can change their own password: **My Profile** in the patient app, **Settings** in the staff app (both use one shared form, `frontend/shared/src/ChangePassword.tsx`). Before this, a password could only be changed by the "forgot password" email link. API: `POST /api/v1/auth/change-password` with `current_password` and `new_password`; the answer is a fresh session token.
- **Rules we added:** the CURRENT password must be right, so a stolen open session alone cannot lock the owner out; wrong guesses count towards the same lockout as sign-in (5 wrong, then 15 minutes locked, and even the right password is refused meanwhile); a wrong current password is a 422, not a 401, so the app does not think the session ended; the new password must pass the usual strength rules and differ from the current one; success ends EVERY other session and signs this one in again with a fresh token; a verified email address gets a notice ("your password was changed"), with no password in it; each attempt, allowed or denied, is audited without any password text.
- **Doctor and platform apps:** both now have a **My account** page (a shared component, `frontend/shared/src/Account.tsx`) showing who is signed in, their organization and roles, and the same change-password form; the platform one works even if the account is not allowed into the console. The error wording is shared too (`friendlyPasswordError`).
- **Limits:** the account pages show only what the sign-in returned (no name or email yet); a development link (`?as=`) has no password, so the form says so; the notice email does not say from which device; there is no "sign out other devices but keep my password" button (the existing sign-out signs out everywhere); the form shows one generic message for a wrong current password or a weak new one (the server does not say which, by design); the form logic was checked by type checks, a build and a live run against the stack, not by an automated test in the repo.

### DEV-065: set or change my email address while signed in (ADD)

- **What:** a signed-in person can add or change their own email address. A **My email address** card sits next to change-password in the patient app (My Profile), the staff app (Settings), and the doctor and platform apps (My account), with "Send the confirmation link again". API: `POST /api/v1/auth/email` (`email`, `current_password`). Before this, an address could only be set by the person who invited an account, so staff and doctors often had none, and with no verified address they could not use "forgot password" or receive the daily late-work email (DEV-057).
- **Rules we added:** the CURRENT password is required, so a stolen open session cannot redirect password-reset mail to an attacker; wrong guesses share the sign-in lockout (one shared check for this and change-password); the new address starts UNVERIFIED and gets a verification link (older unused verification links are cancelled); if the OLD address had been verified it receives a notice naming the new address in masked form; setting the same address again changes nothing; a verification link sent too soon (one a minute) does not stop the address being saved ("sent: false" is returned and the card says to wait); every attempt is audited without any password.
- **Links now open the right app:** verification and password-reset emails used to open the patient app for everyone. They now open the patient app for patients and guardians, the doctor app for doctors, and the staff console for everyone else (`app_url_for`). Zoiko's platform staff are sent to the platform console (new setting `PLATFORM_APP_URL`, default http://localhost:5175), which now has its own link page. The link page itself is one shared component (`frontend/shared/src/LinkActions.tsx`) that the staff, doctor and platform apps each wrap with their own text and server calls, replacing two near-identical copies.
- **Limits:** one address per login (no second or backup address); no check that the address really exists beyond its shape, and none that it is not already used by someone else (logins are unique, emails are not); the account stays signed in after the change (a new address does not end sessions); the card shows the address masked, never in full; the card forms were checked by type checks, builds and a live run against the stack (including the dev inbox), not by repo tests.

### DEV-066: display names for staff, doctors and platform users (ADD)

- **What:** until now every staff member, doctor and platform user appeared as their login (an email address) everywhere: on a patient's appointment ("with doc"), in the work queue, in messages, in the Providers and Team lists. A person can now choose the name they are shown by (**Settings** in the staff app, **My account** in the doctor and platform apps; API `POST` or `DELETE /api/v1/auth/display-name`, and `display_name` in `GET /me`). New table `trust_actorname` (migration `trust/0007`, row-level security on). The shared label function (`actor_labels`) uses the name when there is one, so every screen that showed a login now shows the name.
- **Rules we added:** the name is stored ENCRYPTED and bound to the person (a name copied to another person's row does not decrypt, and that person is shown by login); only the person can set or remove their own name, nobody else can (not even an administrator); 2 to 80 characters, spaces tidied, no control characters, and no `@` (so an email address cannot be used as a name); patients and guardians cannot have one (their name is a patient detail in My Profile; refused with 403); changes are audited without the name; a name that cannot be decrypted is skipped, never an error. The Team page shows the name with the login underneath.
- **NOT VERIFIED (important):** the name is typed by the person and nobody checks it against an identity, a licence or a registration. A patient could be shown "Dr. Somebody" for someone who is not a doctor. The card says so, but patients are not told on their own screens. Verified professional names and titles need the practitioner-credential work (docs `catalog.practitioner`, `trust.professional_qualification`) and a decision on who verifies (DECISIONS-NEEDED, clinical safety). Until then treat this as a convenience, not as proof.
- **Limits:** an administrator cannot set or correct someone else's name; no title or speciality field; the top bar still shows the login (only lists and appointments use the name); the card was checked by type checks, builds and tests of the server side, not by a screen test.
- **Lesson recorded:** a new migration must be applied to the local database (`manage.py migrate`) as soon as it is created; I forgot, and every page that lists people returned a 500 until I did.

### DEV-067: reasons for cancelling an appointment (ADD)

- **What:** when an appointment is cancelled the person can say why (optional). The patient app and the staff app show a "Why?" list next to "Yes, cancel". The staff Appointments list shows the reason under "Cancelled", the Reports page has a **Why bookings were cancelled** card, and the CSV download has the same breakdown. New column `scheduling_booking.cancel_reason` (migration `scheduling/0007`). API: `POST /bookings/<id>/cancel` takes an optional `reason`.
- **Rules we added:** the reasons are FIXED CODES, never free text, so nothing personal can be typed into one: patients and guardians choose from four (cannot make that time, no longer needed, booked elsewhere, other) and clinic staff from four (clinic unavailable, doctor unavailable, service changed, other); a code from the wrong side, or an unknown code, is refused (422) and the booking stays as it was; the FIRST reason is kept if a cancelled booking is cancelled again; when CLINIC staff cancel, the patient's notice says why in plain words ("The clinic said: the doctor is unavailable then"), but a patient's own reason is not repeated back to them; "other" adds nothing to the notice; the report counts reasons for bookings made in the chosen period, with "No reason given" for none.
- **Limits:** no free-text explanation (deliberate, it keeps personal data out); a move (reschedule) cancels the old booking internally and does NOT record a reason; the reason is not shown to the doctor; older cancellations have no reason (counted as "No reason given"); no per-doctor or per-service breakdown.

### DEV-068: optional short emails to patients about their care (ADD)

- **What:** a patient can turn on short emails ("Email me about changes" on the Notifications page; API `GET` or `POST /api/v1/my/email-notices`). Until now every notice lived only inside the app, so a patient learned of a confirmation, a cancellation or a reply only by signing in. New table `comms_emailpreference` (migration `comms/0002`, row-level security on). The mail goes through the normal mail queue and the worker sends it.
- **What an email may say:** ONLY that something changed, never what: "An appointment was confirmed / cancelled / moved", "You have an appointment coming up", or "You have a new message from your clinic", plus a link to the Notifications page. It names no clinic, service, doctor, time, reason or message text (tests check this), so a forwarded or glanced-at email reveals almost nothing.
- **Rules we added:** OFF by default and opt-in (the patient turns it on); sent only to an address the patient has CONFIRMED (a switch on with no confirmed address sends nothing, and the page says so); only to the patient's OWN login: a guardian's login is never emailed about the person they look after, and one patient's choice never affects another; not for a booking request, an attendance record, a referral, the waiting list or a family request (only confirm, cancel, move, reminder and a clinic message); staff, doctors and platform users have no such switch (403); turning it on or off is audited.
- **Limits:** no per-kind choices and no quiet hours (all or nothing); no daily cap per patient (a patient with a lot of activity gets one email per event); no SMS or push; the email says which kind of change but links to the Notifications page, not the item; whether an email reached the inbox is not shown anywhere except the platform's failed-mail count; the guardian case has no email option at all (a decision on consent for emailing about a child is needed first, see DECISIONS-NEEDED 2).

### DEV-069: "Confirm all waiting" on the staff Appointments page (ADD)

- **What:** when two or more bookings are waiting for confirmation, the staff Appointments page shows **Confirm all waiting (N)**. It asks "Confirm N appointments?" and then confirms them one after another, saying afterwards how many were confirmed and how many could not be.
- **Rules we added:** there is NO new server action: each booking is confirmed by the same call as the single Confirm button, so every one passes the same permission check, is audited on its own, sends its own patient notice, and closes its own work-queue item; only waiting bookings whose time has NOT passed are included (an old waiting booking must be looked at one by one); a booking that cannot be confirmed (for example someone changed it a moment earlier) is counted as failed and stays waiting, and the others still go ahead; nothing happens without the second click.
- **Limits:** it confirms everything that is waiting, not a chosen set (no checkboxes yet), and it does not look at the filter; a long list is confirmed one by one, so it can take a few seconds; the page cannot undo it (a confirmed booking can only be moved or cancelled); this is screen logic checked by type check and build, not by a repo test, and it was not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-070: create a whole run of days of free times at once (ADD)

- **What:** on the staff Services page, "Create times" can now repeat the same times for several days in one go: **Repeat for N days** (1 to 31) and **Skip Saturday and Sunday**. A clinic that opens 09:00 to 13:00 Monday to Friday can make a month of availability with one click, instead of one day at a time. The button says how many times it will make ("Create 160 times"). API: `POST /services/<id>/slots` takes optional `days` and `weekdays_only` (the old request still works and means one day).
- **Rules we added:** the same wall-clock times are made on every day, in the location's own time zone (no drift across a clock change; a time that does not exist or happens twice is still refused); with "skip weekends", Saturdays and Sundays are skipped and do NOT use up one of the N days (5 weekdays starting on a Friday ends on the next Thursday); at most 31 days and at most 400 times in one request (more is refused, nothing is made); ALL OR NOTHING: if any one time clashes with an existing time, or any start is in the past, nothing at all is created; only staff with the catalog-write right at that clinic can do it (403 for patients and doctors); one audit entry for the run.
- **Limits:** weekends are the only days that can be skipped (no public holidays, no lunch break, no different hours on different days); a run can be undone by removing those days' free times (see DEV-071); the same doctor and location apply to every day; the screen was checked by type check and build, and the server rules by 21 tests, but the form was not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-071: take free times off the calendar (ADD)

- **What:** until now a clinic could create free times but never remove them: a public holiday, a doctor away, or a mistake in a repeated run (DEV-070) left unwanted times for patients to book. The staff Services page now has **Remove free times**: pick a first and last day (both included), optionally one doctor, press **Count them** to see how many, then **Yes, remove them**. API: `POST /services/<id>/slots/withdraw` (`first_day`, `last_day`, optional `provider`, optional `dry_run`).
- **Rules we added:** only FREE, FUTURE times are removed; a time that has a booking (waiting or confirmed) is NEVER touched, so no patient loses an appointment this way (those are cancelled or moved one by one, with a reason); times in the past are left alone; the days are the clinic's own calendar days at each time's location; at most 62 days at once and the range must not run backwards (else refused, nothing changes); a dry run only counts; the removed times are marked "withdrawn", not deleted, so the history stays; creating the SAME time again (same service, place, doctor and start) brings the withdrawn one back instead of clashing with it; one audit entry per run; only staff with the catalog-write right at that clinic can do it.
- **Limits:** no way to remove a single chosen time (a range of days only); patients who were looking at a time when it was removed see it vanish the next time they load (a booking attempt on it is refused); no notice is sent to anyone; a time with a booking can only be dealt with by cancelling or moving that booking; the form was checked by type check and build, and the server rules by 18 tests, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-072: patient dashboard shows real referrals (FIX)

- **What:** the patient Dashboard still marked "My Referrals" and "Referral Status" as "not built yet", although referrals have been live for a while. The tile now opens the Referrals page, and the card lists the patient's three latest referrals (clinic, who referred, urgent or not) with a plain status: sent and waiting, accepted, clinic needs more information, declined. No new data is fetched: it uses the referrals the app already loads.
- **Limits:** only the three latest are shown (the full list is one click away); no backend change, so no new tests; checked by type check and build only, not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-073: "Today" card on the staff dashboard (ADD)

- **What:** the staff Dashboard now shows how today looks at the clinic: how many appointments are confirmed today, how many requests for today are still waiting for staff, and how many of those have not started yet. API: `today` in `GET /staff/dashboard` (`confirmed`, `waiting`, `still_to_come`). Numbers only, no patient names.
- **Rules we added:** "today" is the clinic's own calendar day at each appointment's location (not UTC, so a 02:00 appointment is counted on the right day); cancelled appointments are not counted; only the signed-in staff member's own organization is counted; a login with no organization sees zeros.
- **Limits:** no list of today's appointments yet (the Appointments page has it); the "still to come" number compares with the server's clock; checked by 8 tests, with each rule broken on purpose to make sure a test fails, plus type check and build, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-074: refused checks by person and by day on the Compliance page (ADD)

- **What:** the staff Compliance page now starts with two small tables for the last 7 days: **who was refused most** (how many refused checks, out of how many checks that person made) and **refused checks per day** (every day listed, quiet days show zero). It helps a compliance officer notice someone repeatedly trying something they may not do. API: `refused` in `GET /staff/compliance`; numbers only, no patient and no reason text.
- **Rules we added:** only the signed-in organization's own checks are counted; days are UTC days with today as the last row; the 7-day window starts at midnight UTC six days ago; the list shows at most 10 people and only people who were refused at least once; people appear by their login (as everywhere else for staff). Only staff who may read evidence can see it (a patient is refused).
- **Limits:** the window is fixed at 7 days on the screen (the server accepts 1 to 31); no click-through from a person to their checks; the existing 50-row decision table below is unchanged; checked by 15 tests with every rule broken on purpose to make sure a test fails, plus type check and build, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-075: remove one single free time (ADD)

- **What:** DEV-071 could only remove free times by a range of days. On the staff Services page, pressing **Add times** on a service now also lists its **next 15 free times**, each with a **Remove** button, for a single cancelled slot or a doctor who cannot do one hour. API: `POST /slots/<id>/withdraw`.
- **Rules we added:** a time that has a booking (waiting or confirmed), that was already removed, or that has already started is refused with a conflict and nothing changes; a time whose booking was cancelled counts as free again and can be removed; the removed time can be put back by creating it again (DEV-071); one audit entry per removal (refusals leave none); only staff with the catalog-write right at that clinic can do it, and another customer's staff get "not found".
- **Limits:** only the next 15 times are listed (use the range form for more); nobody is told when a time they were looking at disappears; checked by 13 tests (each rule broken on purpose; the customer-scope filter in the service cannot be tested separately because the database's row-level security already blocks it), type check and build, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-076: missed visits by doctor and busiest days of the week in Reports (ADD)

- **What:** the staff Reports page has two new tables. **Missed visits by doctor:** for each doctor, visits that already happened and were recorded as attended or missed, how many are not recorded yet, and the share missed. **Busiest days of the week:** appointments in the period, Monday to Sunday, by the clinic's own day. Both are in the CSV download too. API: `by_doctor` and `by_weekday` inside `bookings` of `GET /staff/reports`.
- **Rules we added:** only confirmed visits whose time has passed count as visits (waiting, cancelled and future ones do not); the missed share is missed divided by (attended + missed), so visits nobody recorded yet do not make a doctor look better or worse, and it is empty, not 0%, when nothing was recorded; doctors are listed busiest first, shown by login name; the weekday table counts waiting and confirmed appointments whose time falls in the period (by the appointment date, not by when it was booked) on each location's own clock, and leaves cancelled ones out; other clinics are never counted.
- **Limits:** one doctor with very few visits can show a dramatic share, so read it together with the counts; the weekday table counts at most 5,000 appointments; no filter by doctor or service yet; checked by 13 tests (each rule broken on purpose to make sure a test fails), type check and build, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-077: platform Releases log (ADD)

- **What:** the platform app's **Releases** page was a placeholder. It is now a hand-written log of what went live and when: version, date, one-line summary and optional details. Zoiko support staff can add an entry; support and safety staff can read the log. API: `GET` and `POST /platform/releases`. New table `trust_releasenote` (migration `trust.0008`, with row-level security).
- **Rules we added:** it is a record only and deploys nothing; entries are add-only (a wrong entry is answered by a new one, never edited or deleted, and the app database role is not granted UPDATE or DELETE); a version can be recorded once per platform (stray spaces are trimmed first, so "1.0" and " 1.0 " clash); version up to 40 characters, summary up to 200, details up to 2,000; control characters are refused, and line breaks are allowed in the details only; the date may be today or earlier, with one day of slack for time zones; newest release date first, then newest entry; reading needs `read:platform` (support R11 and safety R12), adding needs `write:platform` (support only); a customer's own staff, even with a support role, are refused because the account must be in the platform tenant; each entry writes one audit entry (refusals write none) and each read leaves its normal access-decision record.
- **Limits:** this is "release evidence" in the weakest sense: nobody checks the entries against what was really deployed, and there is no rollout control, approval or rollback (the menu text said "a later phase" and that is still true for those); the list shows the newest 100; no search; the platform Incidents and Settings pages are still placeholders; checked by 34 tests (each rule broken on purpose to make sure a test fails), type check, build and a migration drift check, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-078: platform Incidents log (ADD)

- **What:** the platform app's **Incidents** page was a placeholder. It is now a hand-written log of what went wrong and how it ended: title, severity (low, medium, high), the date it happened and optional details. An incident starts **open** and can be **closed once** with a note on how it ended. API: `GET` and `POST /platform/incidents`, `POST /platform/incidents/<id>/resolve`. New table `trust_incident` (migration `trust.0009`, with row-level security).
- **Rules we added:** it is a record only: it raises no alarm and fixes nothing; the three severity labels are **provisional** because the real scale is undecided (DEV-014), and they can be replaced later without losing entries; an entry is never deleted and is changed only by being closed, which sets the status, the note, the closing date and who closed it; the closing date is the server's today, never typed; a closed incident cannot be closed again (the first note stays); the title and severity cannot be changed; title up to 120 characters, details up to 2,000, closing note up to 1,000; control characters are refused and line breaks are allowed in details and notes only; the date may be today or earlier with one day of slack for time zones; open incidents are listed first, then the newest date, then the newest entry; reading needs `read:platform` (support R11 and safety R12), adding and closing need `write:platform` (support only); a customer's own staff, even with a support role, are refused; each record and each closing writes one audit entry (refusals write none).
- **Limits:** the text is free and **cannot be checked for patient details**; the screen warns against it, but it relies on the person writing it; no link to the audit log entry, no owner or follow-up tasks, no reopening, no notification to anyone, and no link with the real severity scale or any reporting duty (decision 14 and the legal questions stay open); the list shows the newest 100; no search; checked by 51 tests (each rule broken on purpose to make sure a test fails), type check, build and a migration drift check, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-079: platform Settings: open or close a customer's patient sign-up (ADD)

- **What:** the platform app's **Settings** page was a placeholder. It now lists every customer with whether patient self-sign-up is **open** or **closed**, and support staff can switch it, with a "Yes, open it" or "Yes, close it" confirmation. Until now only the development console could do this, with no access check. API: `POST /platform/customers/<id>/sign-up` with `{"enabled": true|false}`.
- **Rules we added:** needs the right to write the platform (support R11); safety officers and other platform roles can look but not change; a customer's own staff are refused even with a support role; `enabled` must be a real true or false (the words "true", "yes", 1 and so on are refused, nothing changes); the platform's own tenant has no patient sign-up and is refused; an unknown customer is "not found"; setting the value it already has changes nothing and writes no audit entry; a real change is written to the audit chain of the customer (who made it) and of the platform (which customer, and open or closed); closing sign-up does not remove or lock accounts that already exist; new customers still start closed (DEV-040).
- **Limits:** this is the only setting on the page; suspending a customer is still not built and should need two people's approval; changing the sign-up setting needs one person's confirmation click, not two; there is no per-customer limit on how many accounts open sign-up can create beyond the existing sign-up rate limit; checked by 26 tests (each of 12 rules broken on purpose to make sure a test fails; one weak test was strengthened), type check, build and a migration drift check, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-080: two-person approval to suspend or reinstate a customer (ADD)

- **What:** the docs say tenant-level actions should take two people's approval (DEV-040 and the platform console notes). The platform Settings page now has **Suspend or reinstate a customer**: one support person asks, with a reason; a DIFFERENT support person approves, which applies it at once, or rejects it with an optional note; the person who asked can only take their own request back. A suspended customer cannot sign in and its open sessions stop working (the sign-in and token checks already required an active customer), and reinstating restores the same accounts. API: `GET /platform/suspensions`, `POST /platform/customers/<id>/suspension`, `POST /platform/suspensions/<id>/approve|reject|cancel`. New table `trust_suspension` (migration `trust.0010`, row-level security, never deleted).
- **Rules we added:** only support (R11) may ask, approve, reject or cancel; the safety officer (R12) can read the list; nobody can decide their own request; one request can wait for a customer at a time; suspending needs an active customer and reinstating a suspended one, checked when asking AND again when approving (a request that no longer fits stays waiting and can be rejected); the platform's own tenant can never be suspended and an ended customer cannot be touched; the reason is required (up to 500 characters, one line); the note is optional (up to 300); every step writes one audit entry in the platform's chain, and an applied change also writes one in the customer's chain naming who approved it; refusals write nothing.
- **Limits:** "different person" means a different login, not a different employee; the approver must hold the support role, so a safety officer cannot approve (open question 26); nothing notifies the customer or the other support person; a suspension request has no expiry; the status change and the customer's own audit entry are two separate database steps, so a failure between them would leave the status changed without the customer-side entry (the platform-side entry is written together with the status); suspended customers' data is not hidden from Zoiko staff; checked by 50 tests (each of 27 rules broken on purpose to make sure a test fails; two weak tests were strengthened), type check, build and a migration drift check, but not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-081: booking history, the design's `booking_event` (ADD)

- **What:** the design (docs/schema 03, section B) lists `scheduling.booking_event` as a first-phase table: an append-only record of every step of a booking. Until now we only had the booking's current state plus the general audit log. Every step is now written to a new table `scheduling_bookingevent` (migration `scheduling.0008`, row-level security) in the SAME transaction as the step itself: **requested, confirmed, cancelled** (with the reason code), **moved** (written on both the old and the new booking), **attended / did not attend** (a correction is a new step) and **reminder sent**. Each step carries the digest of the one before it, so a changed, missing or swapped step is detected. Patients get a **History** button on each appointment and staff a **History** button in the Appointments table; both call `GET /bookings/<id>/history`.
- **Rules we added:** the database refuses UPDATE and DELETE on the table, even for the table owner (a trigger, the same approach as the audit log), and the application role is only granted SELECT and INSERT; a booking can have only one step in each place of its chain (two writers racing get a conflict, not a fork); a step that changes nothing (confirming twice, cancelling twice, same attendance twice) writes nothing; a refused action writes nothing; a patient sees the steps as "You or your family", "The clinic" or "Automatic" and never which staff member acted, while staff see which login acted on the clinic side but only "The patient or family" for the patient side, never a name; reading needs the same right as reading the booking, so a doctor with no link to the patient, or any other customer, is refused; the answer says whether the chain still checks out, and the screens warn when it does not.
- **Where we differ from the design (for review):** the design's row (standard column set B) has a tenant-wide chain, an event id, a schema version, an actor authority reference, a policy decision reference, an evidence reference, a receipt type and a causation id. We chain per booking (so two bookings never wait for each other), keep the actor, the side (`source`, our version of "authoritative source"), the reason code and the related booking, and leave the rest out. The design's separate booking intent, partner booking, hold and operation tables are still not built: a booking is one row. The design's 16 internal booking states are still our three (requested, confirmed, cancelled).
- **Limits:** bookings made before today have **no history** and say so (nothing was invented for them); the chain detects a changed, removed or swapped step but cannot detect the newest steps being cut off, because nothing outside the chain records how many there should be (the general audit log still has them); the digest and the chain-link check each back up the other, so removing just one of them changes nothing; the history is about this booking only (a move is two linked histories, shown separately); nothing is exported or searchable across bookings; checked by 25 tests (41 rules broken on purpose to make sure a test fails; two real gaps found and closed, plus four pairs of checks that cover for each other), 193 related tests, type check, build and a migration drift check, but the screens were not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-082: work queue history, the design's `workflow_transition` (ADD)

- **What:** the design (docs/schema 03, section D) lists `workflow.workflow_transition` as a first-phase table: the permanent record of every move of a task. Our work queue (DEV-064) kept only an item's current state. Every step of a work item is now written to the new table `workflow_workitemevent` (migration `workflow.0002`, row-level security) in the same transaction as the change: **opened** (work arrived), **taken** by a named person, **given back**, **done** and **no longer needed**. The staff Work queue has a **History** button on each row showing who did what and when, oldest first. API: `GET /staff/work/<id>/history`. The chaining logic is now a small shared helper (`foundation/chain.py`), tested on its own.
- **Rules we added:** the database refuses UPDATE and DELETE on the table, even for the table owner (a trigger, as for the audit log and the booking history), and the application role is only granted SELECT and INSERT; each step carries the digest of the one before it, so a changed, missing or swapped step is detected, and the screen warns when the check fails; a step that changes nothing (taking an item you already hold, opening or closing the same work twice, an event delivered twice) writes nothing, and a refused action writes nothing; closing by an event is recorded as "Automatic" (no person closed it), while taking and giving back name the staff login; the history holds only references and kinds of work, never the patient, the booking or the thread (tested); only staff of the clinic that owns the item may read it, with the same right as the work queue itself, and the read leaves its normal decision record.
- **Limits:** items opened before today have no history and say so (nothing was invented); the chain detects a changed, removed or swapped step but cannot detect the newest steps being cut off; the screen only reaches items that are still open (the API also serves closed ones by id); who closed the work in the real world (the person who confirmed the booking, for instance) is not recorded, because the event that closes an item does not carry the actor; the booking history (DEV-081) still has its own copy of the chaining code and should move onto the shared helper; there is no history for referrals or message threads themselves; checked by 25 tests (34 rules broken on purpose to make sure a test fails, with 4 gaps closed and 2 pairs of checks that cover for each other), 144 related tests, type check, build and a migration drift check, but the screen was not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-083: the app refuses to start on a database login that skips row-level security (ADD)

- **What:** DEV-018 made PostgreSQL row-level security the second fence between customers, and recorded the risk that if the app were ever deployed on a superuser or owner login, "RLS silently does nothing", with a test that only documents it. Nothing stopped that deployment. Now the web server and the worker check, as they start, which database login they are running as. If it is a **superuser** or has the **BYPASSRLS** attribute, then with `DEBUG` off they **refuse to start** with a plain message saying what to fix; in development (`DEBUG` on, which is how the Docker setup runs, on one superuser login) they only **log a warning**. New file `foundation/dbrole.py`; called from `config/wsgi.py`, `config/asgi.py` and the `relay_outbox` worker.
- **Rules we added:** the check uses the login in force (`current_user`), so a role switched to with `SET ROLE` is judged as that role; migrations are NOT guarded (they run as a separate owner role, as DEV-018 says); the only way to turn the refusal off is the setting `REQUIRE_RESTRICTED_DB_ROLE`, which is `True` in the base settings and `False` only in the test settings (the test database user is a superuser); there is no environment variable or flag to bypass it in a real deployment; if the database is not reachable when the worker starts, it retries (as it already did) and asks again, instead of treating the outage as a refusal.
- **Checked against the real stack:** started with the base settings (`DEBUG` off) against the real superuser login, both the web entry point and the worker refused with the message; in the Docker development setup the backend and the worker both start and log the warning once.
- **Limits:** it checks the two attributes that bypass row-level security, not whether the login also owns the tables (owners are covered by the `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` that every table gets, DEV-018); it runs once at start, so changing the role's attributes while the server is up is not noticed; the deployment setup (creating the two roles and the connection settings for each) is still not written, so a real deployment today would simply refuse to start until someone does it; checked by 19 tests (15 rules broken on purpose, all caught), type check and lint.

### DEV-084: the application login's rights, in one list, with a setup script and guide (ADD)

- **What:** DEV-083 makes the app refuse to start on a login that skips row-level security, but nothing said what the restricted login should be allowed to do, how to create it, or how to prove it has exactly those rights. Now: **`foundation/dbgrants.py`** is the single list (audit, booking-history, work-history and release-log tables: read and add only; the delivery plumbing: also delete; every other table: read, add, change; Django's own tables: none; plus the outbox sequence). **`manage.py db_grants`** prints the SQL, applies it (`--apply`, as the owner) or compares it with what the login really has (`--check`, exit code 1 if anything differs). **`deploy/create_db_roles.sql`** creates the owner and app logins (neither superuser, neither `BYPASSRLS`). **`docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`** has the first-time steps and the every-release routine. The test setup now grants its restricted role from the same list, so the tests run with exactly the rights production gets.
- **Rules we added:** applying always starts from a clean slate (everything revoked, then the list granted, in one transaction), so a right added by hand is taken away and a failed apply changes nothing; a table added by a release has no rights for the app until `db_grants --apply` is run after `migrate` (fail closed: the app errors on it instead of quietly using it); role and table names are restricted to lower-case words before they go into SQL; the list is checked against the database itself: the tables that carry an "append-only" trigger must be exactly the add-only list, and every table with a `tenant_id` must be covered.
- **Found and fixed on the way:** the release log (DEV-077) was add-only only because the app login was never given UPDATE or DELETE; unlike the audit log, booking history and work history it had no database trigger, so an owner login could still change it. Migration `trust.0011` adds the trigger, with a test.
- **Rehearsed end to end** on a scratch PostgreSQL 16 database (then removed): script, migrate as owner, `--check` (105 rights missing) then `--apply` then `--check` (exactly as listed); as the app login the web entry point and the worker started, `migrate` was refused, two customers could each see only their own rows, nothing was visible with no customer selected, and editing audit rows, deleting, dropping or creating tables and truncating were refused.
- **Limits:** not tried on a managed cloud database (no true superuser there); passwords are shown on a command line in the guide for clarity; rotating passwords, backups and server moves are not written; an unexpected table the app needs but that is not one of our models would have no rights; checked by 31 tests (21 rules broken on purpose, all caught), type check, lint and the rehearsal above.

### DEV-085: the platform Overview shows the platform team's own open work (ADD)

- **What:** the platform Overview only looked at customers and system health. It now also shows what the platform team itself has open: how many **incidents** are still open (and how many of those are **high** severity, DEV-078), how many **suspension or reinstatement requests** are waiting for a second person (DEV-080), and the **latest release** (DEV-077). They appear in the "Needs attention" list (a high-severity open incident is "urgent", the rest "check"), each with an **Open** button to the right page, and as a "Latest release" card. API: a `work` block in `GET /platform/overview`.
- **Rules we added:** counts and a version only: no incident title or details, no suspension reason or customer, and no release summary or notes ever appear on the Overview (tested); the numbers are read from the signed-in login's own platform tenant, so one platform tenant never sees another's; they use the same permission as the rest of the Overview (support and safety staff can read; other roles and signed-out visitors cannot); closed incidents and finished (approved, rejected or taken-back) requests are not counted.
- **Limits:** the counts come from the newest 100 incidents and 100 requests (a platform with more than 100 open at once would under-count; far beyond today's use); a suspension request waiting for a second person is not marked as yours or someone else's here (the Settings page does that); no history or trend; checked by 10 tests (9 rules broken on purpose, one weak test strengthened and re-checked), type check, build and lint, but the screen was not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-086: booking history moves onto the shared chain helper, with a digest version (CHANGE)

- **What:** DEV-081 (booking history) had its own copy of the chaining code, and DEV-082 (work history) used the shared helper in `foundation/chain.py`. Two copies of security code can drift apart. The booking history now uses the shared helper for every NEW row. Because the two layouts produce different digests, each booking-history row now records `digest_version`: rows written before this change are **version 1** (the first layout, kept only for checking them), new rows are **version 2** (the shared helper). The check reads each row's version and uses the matching layout, so a chain may mix both. Migration `scheduling.0009` adds the column; existing rows get version 1 without being rewritten (adding a column with a default does not trip the table's append-only trigger).
- **Rules we added:** the version has no default in the model, so a row written without one is refused instead of guessed; flipping a row's version label to make it pass fails the check (the digest no longer matches); an unknown version (0, 3, 99) fails; both digest layouts are pinned to fixed known values in tests, and the tests say that a change there needs a NEW version number, never an edit; `verify_chain` takes either the usual parts or a function that says what each row's digest must be (exactly one of the two).
- **Facts:** the local development database held no booking-history rows at all (every booking in it predates DEV-081), so nothing local was at risk; the version protects any other copy of the database that has used the feature. The real migration was run in a test against a row written the old way: it became version 1 and still checked out (rolled back afterwards).
- **Limits:** the check still cannot detect the newest steps being cut off; the work history has only one layout so far and no version column (it can get one the same way if its layout ever changes); old rows stay on the old layout for good, so the old digest function must be kept as long as any such row may exist; checked by 16 new tests (13 rules broken on purpose, all caught) and 158 related tests, type check and lint, but the screens were not re-clicked in a browser.

### DEV-087: the work history says who closed the work in the real world (CHANGE)

- **What:** DEV-082 recorded who TOOK and GAVE BACK a work item, but when work closed (a booking confirmed, a referral decided, a message answered, a booking cancelled) the history said "Automatic", because the event that closes it carried no actor. The booking, referral and message events now say who acted, and the work history shows it: a clinic login's name, "The patient or family" when the patient side did it (for example by cancelling), and "Automatic" only when nothing human did.
- **Rules we added:** a clinic login's public id travels in the event; a **patient's id never does**: the event carries only the side ("patient"), and the history stores the word "patient", not an id (tested: the patient's person id and booking id never appear in a work history, and a patient-side event has no actor id); an event that names nobody (events queued before this change, or work closed by the system) still closes as "Automatic"; only references go in events, as before; the booking history, work history and event payload rules are unchanged otherwise.
- **Limits:** the work-queue screen (not the history) still lists only open items, so a closed item's history is reachable only by its id through the API; "the clinic" acting as a whole (a staff member acting on a patient's behalf, such as booking for them) is recorded as clinic side with that login, not as the patient; the opening of work stays "Automatic" ("Work arrived") because a patient's action opens it and we do not name them; checked by 34 work-history tests (22 rules broken on purpose, one gap closed and re-checked), 225 related tests, type check and lint, but the screen was not re-clicked in a browser.

### DEV-088: a release check refuses development-only settings in production (ADD)

- **What:** the development setup deliberately runs with an unsigned sign-in, short passwords, placeholder secrets, only-local host names and a catch-all development inbox. Nothing stopped those reaching a real server (open question 15). New `foundation/release_check.py` holds one list of them. `manage.py check --deploy` reports each as a numbered error (`zoiko.E001`...). With `DEBUG` off, the web servers (`wsgi.py`, `asgi.py`) and the worker (`relay_outbox`) call it as they start and **refuse to start** while any remains, in one message that lists all of them. The worker runs it before the database-login check (DEV-083).
- **What it checks:** `DEBUG`; `DEV_AUTH_STUB`; `PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH` under 10; the secret key, database password and field-encryption keys containing a placeholder word (`dev-only`, `replace-me`, `replace-with`, `change-me`, `changeme`, `test-only`) or being empty; a secret key under 40 characters; `ALLOWED_HOSTS` empty, `*` or only local addresses; `ZOIKO_CELL_ID` starting `DEV-` or `TEST-`; email going to `localhost`, `mail`, `mailpit`, `mailhog` or port 1025. The placeholder words are the ones in the committed `.env.example`, so copying that file to a server unedited is caught (tested against its real values).
- **Rules we added:** nothing is checked with `DEBUG` on (development is expected to break all of them); the only switch is `REQUIRE_PRODUCTION_SETTINGS`, `True` in the base settings and `False` only in the test settings (which are placeholders on purpose); a missing database password counts as a placeholder (found while testing: it was being read as the text "None"); comparisons ignore upper and lower case.
- **Checked against the real environment:** with production-like settings on the current development environment, the web entry point, the worker and `check --deploy` each refused and listed six problems; a clean set of values reported none; the Docker development setup still starts normally.
- **Limits:** it checks values, not whether they are good: a long random-looking key that was pasted into a chat would pass; the field-encryption key cannot be recognised as a "development" key unless it contains a placeholder word (the developer's own local key does not, so the same key reused on a server would pass: decision 13 on key management is still open); the mail check treats any `localhost` mail server as development; Django's own deploy checks (HTTPS redirect, secure cookies) are not configured or required, because the apps use bearer tokens and TLS is decided at hosting (DEV-016); checked by 73 tests (41 rules broken on purpose: 40 caught, and the one left is harmless because an empty host list is reported by the "only local addresses" rule anyway; two weaknesses found and fixed: a missing database password was read as the text "None", and the placeholder-word test took its list from the code it was testing), type check and lint.

### DEV-089: the Work queue can show recently closed work (ADD)

- **What:** DEV-082 and DEV-087 noted that closed work disappeared from the Work queue, so its history could not be reached from the screen. The Work queue now has a choice "closed in the last 14 days": the work the clinic closed, newest first, with when it closed and the result (Done, or No longer needed), and a **History** button on each row. API: `GET /staff/work?closed=<days>` (1 to 30). The dashboard's "items are running late" alert also gained an **Open the work queue** button.
- **Rules we added:** the closed list carries exactly the fields the open list does (the booking, referral or thread is only the pointer "subject" that has always been there; a test pins the field set so a new field that could carry patient information would fail), plus the status and the closing time; the days must be a whole number from 1 to 30, anything else is refused with nothing returned; the service itself keeps the number in range (0 or negative acts as 1 day, more than 30 acts as 30); only the signed-in clinic's own work is listed; the same staff-only right as the open list, with a decision record for each read; `mine` and `unassigned` are ignored when asking for closed work; a new open item for the same thing (the patient writes again) is open while the old one stays in the closed list.
- **Limits:** the screen looks back a fixed 14 days (the API takes up to 30); at most 200 items are listed (the newest); the list does not say who closed each item (open the history for that); no search or paging; checked by 34 tests (25 rules broken on purpose: 23 caught, and two harmless: open work has no closing time so it was already excluded, and a second tenant filter that the organization filter already covers; two real gaps in the day limits were found and closed), 107 related tests, type check, lint and a build, but the screen was not clicked through in a browser.

### DEV-090: "Download my data" includes each appointment's steps (FIX)

- **What:** the patient export says "everything here is information you can already see in the app", but the History button on each appointment (DEV-081) was not in it. The export now has an `appointment_history` section: one entry per appointment, in the same order as the appointments, each with its steps (requested, confirmed, cancelled with the reason, moved, attended or missed, reminded) and whether the record still checks out. It is built by the same code as the History button, so a patient sees the same wording ("You or your family", "The clinic", "Automatic") and never which staff member acted; the export and the button are tested to give identical content.
- **Rules we added:** appointments made before steps were recorded have an entry with an empty list (nothing is invented); an appointment whose steps cannot be read is listed as `available: false` instead of being left out or stopping the export; if the whole appointments section is unavailable, so is its history; the file format name stays `zoiko-access-export-1` (a section was added, nothing changed or removed, so existing readers of the file are not affected).
- **Also:** the Privacy page's description of the download now mentions the appointment steps. While doing this the developer checked the privacy promises written on the screens against the code: appointments and patients listed by staff are recorded per patient in the patient's own access list; insurance numbers, names, birth dates, phones and messages are stored encrypted; every profile save is audited; staff screens never show a patient's email. All held; nothing else needed changing.
- **Limits:** only the patient's own appointments (and those of people they may act for) are exported, as before; the work-queue histories are clinic records and are not in a patient's export; the file still holds only what the app shows, so internal digests and staff logins are not included; checked by 10 new export tests (9 rules broken on purpose: 7 caught at once, the 2 "cannot be read" branches caught after two direct tests were added), type check and lint, but not re-run in a browser.

### DEV-091: the Reports page says its days are UTC days (FIX, wording only)

- **What:** an audit of the staff Reports page against how each number is computed found one mismatch between what a reader would assume and what happens. "Bookings made each day" and the report period count days from midnight to midnight **UTC**, while "Busiest days of the week" uses each clinic's own clock. For a clinic east of UTC (for example India, UTC+5:30) a booking made at 02:00 local time is counted on the previous day's bar. The page and the CSV now say so: the heading note, the card note and a "Days run" row in the CSV.
- **Why wording and not the counting:** counting by the clinic's own day needs a clinic time zone per customer (a customer can have sites in several zones) and a window that follows it; done carelessly it would drop or move bookings at the edges of the period, so the totals would no longer add up. That is a bigger change with its own tests and is left as a known limit.
- **Audited and found correct:** the attendance rate is attended divided by attended plus missed and says "of N recorded visits"; "Bookings made" equals the sum of the three statuses; "Time to confirm" is the middle value and says so; the stated length limits (messages 2,000 characters, names 2 to 80, the platform forms) and the "5 failed sign-ins in 24 hours" warning all match the code.
- **Limits:** the daily bars and the period still follow UTC days; counting by clinic day is not built; no behaviour changed, so no new tests (the staff app builds; lint and types unaffected).

### DEV-092: browser tests (robots) that click through the real apps (ADD, development only)

- **What:** `frontend/e2e` holds Playwright tests that open the real apps in a headless browser and act like a person: Robot 1 a patient books a free time and sees "Waiting for the clinic"; Robot 2 clinic staff confirm it and the patient then sees "Confirmed"; Robot 3 the doctor sees a booking only after the clinic confirms it; Robot 4 a patient moves an appointment, reads its History, then cancels with a reason; Robot 5 one support person asks to suspend the test clinic, a different one approves, the clinic's staff are refused, and the same two-person rule reinstates it (it also repairs a clinic left suspended by a crashed run).
- **Robot 6 (every page loads):** signs in to each app and opens every menu page (about 45 pages in all); fails on the "Something went wrong" screen, a browser error or a server 5xx. It checks that pages load, not that the numbers on them are right, so it does not replace a human click-through (backlog A8). Checked by making one patient page throw: the robot failed; restored, it passed.
- **Setup:** the stack and the four apps must be running (ports 5173 to 5176); `python scripts/seed_e2e.py` creates a separate "E2E Clinic" so the demo data is never touched; each robot adds its own free times on a random far-future day and cancels what it booked, so runs can repeat (cancelled cards from earlier runs stay in the patient's list; the robots allow for that). Run: `cd frontend/e2e && npx playwright test`.
- **Checked by breaking it:** changing the staff word "Confirmed" made Robot 2 fail; skipping the clinic's confirmation made Robot 3 fail; restoring each made them pass. Robot 5 was not broken on purpose.
- **Limits:** needs a running stack, so it is not part of the backend test run and nothing runs it automatically yet (no CI); the robots use screen words, so a wording change means updating them; Chromium only.

### DEV-093: AI governance foundation, switched off by default (ADD)

- **What:** the `ai` module (`backend/modules/ai`) builds the rules the docs set for AI (file 05 section G), not any real AI model. A feature runs only with an unexpired AUTHORIZED permit (a SHADOW permit allows a dry run that acts on nothing); with none it is refused. A permit moves along the docs' path (PROPOSED to AUTHORIZED, then SUSPENDED, CHANGE_REVIEW, RETIRED) and steps cannot be skipped. Authorizing needs two people other than the creator, and an expiry of at most a year. Tools are whitelist-only; `order_medication` and `release_critical_result` can never be on a list. A write the AI proposes waits for a named human who is not the proposer (two people where the permit says so). Prompt and answer text is never stored, only fingerprints; the request, answer-evidence and permit-history tables are append-only in the database; every step is audited and tenant-scoped (row-level security).
- **The one "agent":** `message.reply_draft` (lane A, admin assist) returns a fixed template that never repeats the input and goes to human review. No outside model, no network.
- **Choices I made where the docs are silent (please confirm):** a change review leads to validation or, if approved as not material, back to authorized (corrected in DEV-104: GHE-23 states this); features at level A3 or lanes C and D cannot be authorized at all (no regulatory qualification exists here); a clinical-content feature needs a named clinical owner; two sign-offs rather than the four named teams (Legal, Clinical, QA, SRE) because roles for them do not exist yet.
- **Not built, needs the lead:** who may create and authorize permits (no role or screen exists, so nothing can call this over the web yet); any real model provider (cost, contract, region, privacy); evaluation, drift monitoring and safety-event tables; the executor that carries out an approved write. Until then AI stays off.
- **Found in review and fixed:** an approval could still be given after its permit was suspended or had expired; approving now needs a live permit (refusing is always allowed). One more test covers it.
- **Also fixed:** two permits could be authorized for one feature at once, so suspending the newer left an older one live. Authorizing now needs the feature to have no other authorized permit (retire or suspend the old one first).
- **Checked:** 31 tests (one pins the forbidden list); six rules broken one at a time, five caught at once, and one weak test and one unreachable condition found and fixed; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; grants test corrected for append-only tables.

### DEV-094: staff screen for AI permits, with a temporary access rule (ADD)

- **What:** the staff console has an "AI permits" page (administrators only) and four staff endpoints (`staff/ai/permits`, `.../sign`, `.../advance`). An administrator proposes a permit (model and prompt are kept only as fingerprints), moves it along the approved path, and retires or suspends it with a written reason. To authorize, two administrators other than the proposer must sign it off first; then the authorize step sets how many days it is valid (1 to 365). A new rule in the module (`sign_off`) records each signature once, only while the permit is in release review.
- **Temporary rule (please confirm or replace):** only an organization administrator (R13) may use it. Note it is tenant-wide: permits belong to the whole customer, so an administrator of any one site can manage them for all sites (found in review, DEV-102). Permits belong to one customer's own account, so Zoiko support staff (who sit in Zoiko's own account) cannot act on them; the choice offered earlier ("only Zoiko support approves") is not possible without a way for support to act inside a customer, which has privacy consequences and needs the lead's decision (backlog B14).
- **Still switched off:** nothing can run until a permit is authorized, and the only feature that exists is the fixed reply template. The screen has no way to approve an AI-proposed action (no executor exists), and no way to run anything.
- **Checked:** 14 new endpoint tests plus the 31 module tests pass; five rules were broken one at a time and four were caught at once (the fifth, a duplicate limit on the days field, is also enforced inside the module, so removing the early check changes nothing); ruff, mypy, import rules clean; the staff app type-checks and builds; browser robot 7 passes and robot 6 now also opens the new page. **Full backend run on 2026-11-05 before this screen: 1370 passed, 1 skipped.**
- **Limits:** the page lists at most 200 permits; wording is first-draft; no screen for the AI's request log yet.

### DEV-095: verified doctor names, with a temporary rule (ADD)

- **What:** a doctor chooses their own display name and nobody had checked it. A clinic administrator can now record that they checked a doctor's current name, say on the licence register, with a short note of what they saw (`trust.NameCheck`, migration trust 0012, row-level security on). The Providers page shows each doctor's state (Not checked, Verified, Name changed: check again, Check withdrawn). Patients see a small "Verified" mark next to the doctor on free times, in the booking confirmation and in their appointments.
- **A check belongs to one name:** the check stores a fingerprint of the name it covered. If the doctor changes or removes their name, the mark disappears at once and stays gone until an administrator checks the new name. Withdrawing a check needs a written reason, and a fresh check after a withdrawal is allowed. Every check and withdrawal is in the audit log.
- **Temporary rule (please confirm or replace):** one organization administrator (R13) may check any doctor at their own organization, but never themselves (and never a doctor of another site). Open question 25 is still the real decision: who verifies, whether two people are needed (like suspensions), and what proof counts.
- **The doctor sees their own state (added the same day):** the doctor app's My account page has an "Is my name checked?" card (Not checked, Verified, Check ended after a name change, Check withdrawn with the reason). The administrator's private note is never sent to the doctor. One more test covers it; making the note leak made it fail.
- **Not built:** a public directory of doctors, qualifications or licence numbers as fields (only a free-text note, which must not hold patient details), and expiry dates for checks.
- **Checked:** 21 new tests; seven rules were broken one at a time and six were caught at once (the seventh showed a missing test for a doctor of another site, which I added and then confirmed catches it); two older tests that compared a doctor's details exactly were updated for the new `verified` field; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the staff and patient apps type-check and build; browser robot 8 passes, and hiding the patient mark made it fail.
- **Limits:** the Verified mark means "an administrator says they checked", not that Zoiko verified it independently; the note is free text.

### DEV-096: the patient decides whether clinic staff see their insurance (ADD, a behaviour change)

- **What:** a patient (or someone acting for them) can choose "Let the clinic's staff see my insurance details" on their Insurance page. The choice is stored per patient (`coverage.InsuranceSharing`, coverage migration 0002, row-level security on) and every change is audited. **The default is NOT shared.**
- **Behaviour change you should know about:** before this, the front desk could read any patient's insurance. Now they get an empty list and a notice ("this patient has not chosen to share ... ") until the patient shares. The patient and anyone acting for them always see their own. The front desk can still RECORD insurance a patient hands over (and remove it using the id from the answer), but cannot read it back unless it is shared. One older test was changed to match (the front desk test now has the patient share first).
- **Why this default:** it is the cautious one. Your lead may decide the opposite (share by default, patient can switch off), or that staff need it for billing; billing is not built (B3), so nothing breaks today.
- **Not built:** a general consent record for other data (appointment history, messages, referrals), consent for guardians' own views, a "break glass" for emergencies, expiry of a choice, and any per-staff-role setting (it is all staff of the clinic).
- **Found by the tests and fixed:** the front desk could set the patient's sharing choice (it may write insurance for patients, and the same permission covered the choice), which would let staff grant themselves access. Now only the patient, or someone acting for them, can choose.
- **Checked:** 14 new tests; seven rules broken one at a time and all seven caught; one older coverage test changed (the patient shares first) and one older providers test updated for the doctor name-check field (found by the full run); ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; both apps type-check and build; 164 surrounding tests pass; browser robot 9 passes and hiding the staff notice made it fail. **Full backend run on 2026-11-06 (before the sharing code was added): 1404 passed, 1 skipped, 1 failed (the providers test above, now fixed).**

### DEV-097: "the next 7 days" on the staff Reports page (ADD)

- **What:** a card on Reports showing, for each of the next seven days, the appointments booked so far and what that weekday usually has (a plain average of the last up to eight weeks, by the clinic's own day, empty days counted as zero). A day with at least three bookings and 25% more than usual is marked "busier than usual". Counts only: no patient, name or message appears.
- **Free times left (added after):** each day also shows how many free times are still open (a day with none is marked "none left"), so staff can see which days are nearly full. Checked by one run of the forecast tests (5 passed).
- **What it is NOT:** not a trained model and not machine learning. It is a simple average, so it ignores holidays, seasons and one-off events, and the page says so. With under two weeks of history it shows only the booked counts and says it cannot yet say what a usual day looks like. It needs no AI permit because nothing learns and nothing is stored.
- **Limits:** the "usual" figure counts requested and confirmed appointments (cancelled ones are left out); history is read from at most 20,000 bookings; a clinic with several sites in different time zones is counted by each appointment's own zone.
