- **Checked (kept small, per the request not to rerun tests):** 5 new tests run once, and the reports tests (24 passed in that run); one rule broken (average replaced by a sum) and caught; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the staff app type-checks and builds. The whole robot set and the full backend suite were NOT rerun for this change.

### DEV-098: automated accessibility scan, and the contrast fixes it found (ADD + FIX)

- **Why:** the Product Orientation document (section 12, item 06, and the definition of done in section 13) says the first deliverable's user screens must be tested for accessibility, with WCAG 2.2 AA as the target. We had no accessibility test at all.
- **What:** browser robot 10 opens every page of all four apps (and the four sign-in pages) and runs the axe-core rules for WCAG 2.0, 2.1 and 2.2 level A and AA. It fails on any serious or critical finding and names the exact elements.
- **What it found and what was fixed:** (1) text contrast was just under the 4.5:1 minimum on every page of every app: muted grey text, green "ok" labels, the "soon" label on the active menu item, the red unread-count badge and the faded "not built yet" cards (changed `--muted` from #62718d to #5a6985, `--ok` from #138a4d to #0f7a43, the badge to the existing red `--bad`, the "soon" label on the active item to a darker backing, and the faded cards now use a dashed border instead of reduced opacity); (2) the patient app's "unread" dot had a label but no role (added `role="img"`). After the fixes all five scans pass.
- **What this does NOT prove:** automated rules catch only part of the problems (roughly a third). It scans each page in its first, default state: not open forms, error messages, pop-ups, the dark colour scheme, zoom or narrow windows, keyboard-only use, or a screen reader. Those still need a person (added to `docs/CLICK-THROUGH.md`). Dark-mode colours were not changed or scanned.
- **Checked:** the scan failed on the real problems before the fixes (so it can fail), and passes after; the patient app type-checks. Other robots were not rerun for the colour changes.

### DEV-099: the five product prohibitions as named tests (ADD)

- **Why:** the Product Orientation document (section 12) lists five things the product must never do and asks for them "in test form". `backend/tests/test_prohibitions.py` has one named test for each, written against the real web interface and rules.
- **The five:** (1) an employer never sees a health record because of employment or funding (an employer login is refused every page that could show a booking, referral, patient or insurance, and cannot confirm a booking); (2) a slot is never confirmed unless the provider confirms it (the patient, an employer and a doctor are all refused, the patient's own list says "requested" until the clinic confirms); (3) a critical result is never marked reviewed from a message or an opened screen; (4) protected information does not cross cells or customers just because someone has an account (a different cell is refused, and another customer's staff see none of this customer's data); (5) clinical AI cannot be switched on by a general setting (no AI runs without an authorized permit whether debug is on or off, and a clinical-decision feature cannot be authorized at all).
- **Prohibition 3 is only a tripwire:** results are not built (backlog B1), so there is nothing to mark reviewed. The test fails the day any route mentioning "result" or "critical" appears, so it must then be replaced by the real rule.
- **Checked (small, as asked):** the five tests ran once and passed. Two bad changes were tried: letting a patient confirm their own booking (caught), and granting employers access while keeping the ban (still refused, as it should be) versus granting access and removing the ban (caught). Removing the ban alone changes nothing, because employers are also refused by default; that is defence in depth, and the policy tests cover the ban itself. Ruff is clean.
- **Not covered by these tests:** nothing about real regions or hosting (there is one cell); employers' aggregate billing view is allowed by design and is not tested here.

### DEV-100: backup-and-restore rehearsal with evidence (ADD)

- **Why:** the Product Orientation document (section 12, item 08, and the "Reliability" line of the definition of done) asks for recovery evidence. We had never restored a backup.
- **What:** `python scripts/recovery_drill.py` (development only; Docker must be up) takes a backup of the live database, restores it into a fresh scratch database, and checks: nothing changed during the backup; the restore had no errors; every table came back with the same number of rows; each customer's audit chain still verifies in the restored copy; and encrypted display names still decrypt with our keys. It writes `docs/evidence/recovery-drill-<date>.md`, removes the scratch database and the dump file, prints no passwords or settings, and exits non-zero on any failure.
- **First result (2026-11-06):** passed. 44 tables, 48,992 rows, 11 customers' audit chains (24,151 entries), backup 5.2 MB, backup 1.8 s, restore 3.3 s.
- **Checked by breaking it:** one audit row in the live development database was changed on purpose (with the append-only trigger switched off). The drill reported FAILED ("row content was changed") and exited 1. The row was then put back exactly as it was and the drill passed again (a bad restore cannot slip through).
- **What it does NOT prove:** a regional failover, a restore on different hardware or at production size, that backups run on a schedule, are kept somewhere safe and are themselves encrypted, or how long a real recovery would take. Those need the hosting decisions (DEV-013, DEV-016, backlog "production setup"). The word "recovery" in a release dossier should point to a drill at production size, not this one.
- **Limits:** the drill needs the database quiet for the few seconds it takes (it says so if rows changed); the audit chains are checked, but the booking and work-item history chains are covered only by the row counts.

### DEV-101: one launcher page with every local link and one-click demo sign-in (ADD, development only)

- **What:** `python scripts/dev_links.py` writes `dev-links.html` (ignored by git) and opens it: the four apps, the mail inbox, the developer console and the API check, each with a live green or red dot, and an "Open signed in" button for each demo account.
- **How the buttons work:** each sign-in page reads `?dev_org=&dev_login=&dev_pw=`, signs in once through the real sign-in, and removes the password from the address bar at once (`frontend/shared/src/devLogin.ts`). This only exists when an app runs through the Vite dev server: `import.meta.env.DEV` is false in a production build, and a production build of the staff and patient apps was checked to contain no such code.
- **Risks, stated plainly:** the launcher file holds the DEMO passwords from `docs/RUNNING-LOCALLY.md` and the E2E accounts. It is ignored by git and must never be shared. The links must never be used with a real person's password: the address, even for a second, can reach browser history, screen shares and logs. It signs in through the normal login, so a wrong or locked account still fails.
- **Checked (kept small):** browser robot 11 opens all 10 direct links and checks each lands signed in with the password gone from the address bar (11 passed in one run); the four apps type-check.
- **Limits:** the staff app's older `?tenant=&as=` shortcut still exists beside this one (it needs the unsigned dev token and is not used by the launcher).

### DEV-102: fixes from a read-through of the day's code (FIX)

A review of the day's changes, by reading only, found these. Fixed:
- **Removing an insurance leaked its details (privacy, DEV-096):** the "remove insurance" answer returned the insurer, plan, dates and last four digits to staff even when the patient had not shared it. Staff now get only the id and status; the patient, and staff for a shared insurance, still get the full answer. Three new tests.
- **Every "me" call decrypted every display name in the customer:** one person's name is now read on its own (`display_name_of`, and the name-check state for one person). Cheaper, same answers.
- **The forecast could call a day "usual" from almost no data:** a usual day now needs at least 10 past bookings as well as 14 days of history (`enough_history`, one unit test).
- **The direct-login link left the password in the address bar if someone was already signed in:** the apps now remove those parameters when a session exists (development only).
- **The recovery drill left its scratch database behind if it stopped halfway:** it now always cleans up.
Not fixed, by decision: **AI permits and name checks are managed by an administrator of ANY site and apply to the whole customer** (needs the lead: DEV-094, DEV-095; the register entries now say so); the doctor name-check note is free text and nothing can reliably tell whether it holds patient details.
- **Checked (once):** the 6 affected backend test files (111 passed), ruff and mypy clean, all four apps type-check, robots 9 and 11 run once. The full suite was not rerun.

### DEV-103: the hazard register, seeded from the specification (ADD)

- **Why:** the schema (file 05, `safety.hazard`) and its own note say safety tables are needed before any clinical capability is switched on, even in the first phases. We had an incident log but no hazard register, and the safety-officer login had nothing to decide.
- **What:** a new `safety` module (`backend/modules/safety`, migration safety 0001, row-level security on). The register is seeded, on first use, with the 20 product hazards H01 to H20 and the 16 AI hazards HAI-01 to HAI-16 from the schema, in two separate libraries. The platform app has a **Hazards** page. A safety officer **assesses** a hazard (severity, probability, how it was judged, the remaining risk). A **different** safety officer **verifies or rejects** it (a rejection needs a reason). Only a verified hazard can have its remaining risk **accepted**, by someone other than the assessor, and **never when the remaining risk is high**. Assessing again clears an earlier review. Retiring needs a reason and is final. Every step is in an append-only history (the database refuses edits and deletes) and in the audit log.
- **Not in the documents, so assumed (please confirm, INFERRED):** the scales (severity and probability 1 to 5; remaining risk low, medium or high); the rule that high remaining risk cannot be accepted; that all actions are done by safety officers (R12) only, with support (R11) able to read; and that "independent" means a different person (two safety officers are needed; `rao@zoiko.example` was added to the demo logins).
- **Not built from the same schema section:** `safety.control` (the controls that reduce each hazard), `intended_purpose_statement`, `qualification_dossier` and the other safety tables; linking a hazard to the code or tests that control it; review dates and expiry; a register per customer (this one is Zoiko's own, in the platform tenant).
- **Checked (kept small):** 20 new tests (51 with the grants tests) run once and passed; two rules broken once each (assessor reviews own work; high risk accepted) and both caught; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the four apps type-check and the platform app builds; browser robot 12 passed twice (it is safe to repeat; it leaves H20 open with a browser-test note). The full suite and the other robots were not rerun.
- **Limit:** the seeded titles are short names from the schema; the longer descriptions, exposure paths and affected groups are for the safety officers to fill in. Nothing here is a clinical safety case, and no clinical person has reviewed these scales.

### DEV-104: conformance audit against the original documents, and what it fixed (FIX + CORRECTION)

- **What was done:** four independent read-only reviews of the code against the 27 original Word documents, then my own check of the main claims. The full findings, with spec references and who must decide, are in `docs/CONFORMANCE-AUDIT.md`. Nothing was run for the audit; it is a reading audit, not a security or clinical review.
- **Corrections to my own earlier entries:** (1) **DEV-093 was wrong** to say the documents are silent on where a change review leads: GHE-23 says CHANGE_REVIEW goes to VALIDATION if the change is material, or to AUTHORIZED if approved as not material. The code now follows that, old sign-offs are cleared when a change review starts, and fresh sign-offs are needed to approve it (2 new tests, plus the earlier AI tests all pass). (2) **DEV-014 was wrong** to imply none of the incident severity scales has definitions: GHE-20 defines SEV-0 to SEV-3 (still open: we use low, medium, high). (3) **DEV-095/DEV-096 wording:** the "Verified" mark and the "In force" label overclaimed; they now read "Name checked" and "Dates current" (the API field names are unchanged).
- **Fixed (spec clear, no decision needed):** AI model and prompt fingerprints are now compared with the permit (1 new test); roles R05, R06, R07 lose their blanket read of every patient; support loses read of message content (hard prohibition); referral acceptance, booking request, insurance, "recommended", compliance, messages and referral wording made honest; skip link, visible focus, reduced-motion and forced-colours rules in all four apps; stale comments corrected.
- **Not fixed and why:** seven privacy and safety gaps (staff see all patients of a customer; care links not tied to a site; guardian access has no scope; reschedule and cancel skip the spec's pending states; referral has no named owner; purpose is cosmetic; roles are not checked against customer type) each need a business decision or a data-model change. They are in the audit document with size and owner, and in the lead note.
- **Checked (kept small):** AI, policy, platform, referral and prohibition tests run once (the policy consistency test caught a contradiction I introduced and I removed it; then 64 passed); ruff and mypy clean; all four apps type-check and the staff and patient apps build; robots 1, 8 and the accessibility scan (7 tests) passed with the new wording. The full backend suite and the other robots were not rerun for this.

### DEV-105: employer and payer roles fit only employer and payer logins (TEMPORARY, from audit item 7)

- **What was done:** `assign_role` now refuses (policy denied) to give R08 (employer sponsor) to anyone but an employer login, R07 (payer) to anyone but a payer login, and any other role to an employer or payer login. This backs product prohibition 1 (nothing individual reaches an employer) without needing a table of roles per customer type.
- **Why this smaller rule:** the full table (which roles fit a clinic, payer, employer, partner or platform customer) is the lead's decision, and the existing tests and the developer console put such roles inside clinic-type customers on purpose, so a strict table would be a guess. This rule needs no guess: the actor kinds already exist.
- **Still open:** the customer-type table (audit item 7). Replace this check with it when the lead answers.
- **Checked:** one new test (clinic login refused R07 and R08; employer and payer logins refused clinic roles; the right pairs accepted); trust, auth, devconsole, team and onboarding tests run once (101 passed); lint and types clean. Note for running tests in the container: its environment forces the dev settings, so pass `--ds=config.settings.test`.

### DEV-106: a doctor's care link is tied to the clinic site (TEMPORARY, from audit item 2)

- **What was done:** a care link now records the site (organization) where the visit happened (`CareRelationship.organization_ref`, migration `0013`, written by hand: one added column with an empty default). A link with a site counts only while the doctor still holds a care role (R03 or R09) at that site; when the role ends or the doctor moves, the patients disappear at once, even inside the 30 days. This applies everywhere links are read: the access rule, "my patients" and the care team. Links made before this change have no site and keep working as before.
- **Kept as it was, still the lead's decision:** the 30-day tail after the visit (our own number; GHE-06 AT-20 gives none). It is now one named constant, `CARE_LINK_AFTER_VISIT`.
- **Checked:** with Docker on, the new test, the hand-written migration (`makemigrations --check` finds nothing to add), lint and types pass. Clinician, people, policy, referral and prohibition tests ran once: 65 passed, and the 5 failures were fixed. One new test was wrong (a doctor with no care role is refused with 403, not shown an empty list). Four policy tests failed because of DEV-105, which I had not run them against: their helper now makes employer and payer logins of the right kind, and builds the deliberate mixed-role case directly so the policy engine is still tested for it.

### DEV-107: clinic staff reach only patients connected to their own site (TEMPORARY, from audit item 1)

- **What was done:** a new table (`trust.PersonSite`, migration `0014`, with row-level security) records which patient is connected to which clinic site. A connection is made when the patient books, is referred there, joins its waiting list, writes to it, a family-member request goes to it, or a doctor there gets a care link. When a decision is about staff (an organization rule) and names a patient, the policy engine now also requires that connection at the staff member's own site; otherwise it answers "relationship not verified" (403). Migration `0015` connected every existing patient to the sites they already deal with, so nothing disappears for today's data.
- **The definition is my proposal, not the documents':** GHE-06 and the roles matrix say "minimal" without defining it. I chose "has a booking, referral, waiting-list entry, message, family request or care link at this site". Replace it with the lead's definition when it arrives (audit item 1). A patient who has only signed up, or only added insurance, is invisible to staff until they do one of those things on purpose.
- **Effects to know:** a closed record (for example a rejected family request) can no longer be reached by staff at all, so a second decision on it now answers 403 instead of 409; one messaging test needed the patient linked to the other site to keep testing the domain's own check.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests (`test_site_patients.py`) and a break-check (rule switched off: the first test fails; restored). Policy, people, clinician, staff API, scheduling, referral, waiting list, messaging, coverage sharing, reports, prohibitions, platform, grants, row-level security and family tests ran once (after fixing 28 tests that assumed staff could reach an unconnected patient; the fixes are in setup, never in what the rule allows). Robots 01, 02 and 09 passed. Lint, types and import rules are clean. The remaining test files were not run.

### DEV-108: a patient's cancel or move of a confirmed appointment waits for the clinic (TEMPORARY, from audit item 4)

- **What was done:** GHE-14 WF-13 and tests T-26 and T-27 say a confirmed appointment must not be released until the source acknowledges the cancel, and a failed reschedule must keep the original. The documents describe outside booking systems; here the clinic is the source. So: a patient (or guardian) who cancels a CONFIRMED appointment only asks (`pending_change = "cancel"`); it stays confirmed, on the doctor's schedule, with its time held, until clinic staff confirm the cancellation (staff use the same cancel action; the patient's reason is kept). A patient who moves a confirmed appointment makes a new request and the original stays (`pending_change = "rebook"`); when the clinic confirms the new one the original is let go and its time freed; if the clinic cancels the new request, or the patient withdraws it, the original just stops waiting. Moving a waiting request again still replaces it and keeps pointing at the original. New history steps: `cancel_requested`, `change_requested`, `change_withdrawn`.
- **Unchanged on purpose:** cancelling a request that was never confirmed is immediate; so is anything the clinic does itself (cancel, or its own move of a confirmed appointment); so is a cancel of an appointment that has already started.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** the clinic answers a cancel request by using the ordinary cancel action (no separate "acknowledge" step or deadline); a patient cannot take back a cancel request; only one change can wait at a time (a second one answers 409); there is no automatic expiry if the clinic never answers, and the original simply stays; no separate notice yet tells staff that a cancel was requested (it shows on their appointments list as "Patient asked to cancel").
- **Checked:** 12 new tests (`test_pending_changes.py`) and 12 existing tests updated where they assumed an immediate patient cancel or move; booking, reschedule, history, reminders, providers, waiting list, work queue and history, calendar, today, reports, clinician, staff API, scheduling, API and e-mail notice tests ran once and pass. Robots 01 to 04 pass (the cleanup helper now also lets the clinic answer). Lint, types and both app type-checks are clean. The patient and staff screens show the waiting state; the old line "your current time is released as soon as you choose a new one" is gone.

### DEV-109: referrals have a named owner and fallback, and acceptance is a doctor's (TEMPORARY, from audit item 5)

- **What was done:** GHE-14 WF-16/17 and tests T-38 to T-41 say: receipt is not acceptance; a qualified receiving clinician decides; with no answer the referral escalates to a named clinical owner and is never closed automatically; a decline leaves the obligation with the sender. Built: each clinic site has a **referral inbox** (`ReferralInbox`, migration `0002`, with row-level security) naming an **owner** and an optional **fallback**, both doctors (R03) at that site, set only by the clinic administrator (R13). Receiving staff (R04, R13) can only **mark a referral received** (`acknowledged_at`; the state stays "sent"). Only the named owner or the fallback can **accept, ask for more or decline**; reception staff and the administrator can no longer. The referral gives the owner and fallback a care link of the "referral" kind, tied to the site, so they can read the patient; it ends when the referral is declined, and for everyone but the acceptor when it is accepted. A decline leaves responsibility with the sender; an accept moves it to the accepting doctor. A referral unanswered for 24 hours is flagged **escalated** (shown to staff, the doctor and the fallback) and stays open. Changing the owner moves waiting referrals to the new one. Screens: staff "Who answers referrals" and "Mark received"; the doctor app gets a "Referrals for you to answer" list with Accept, Ask for more, Decline.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** one owner and at most one fallback per site; the 24 hours (our own number, like the work queue's); escalation only flags and shows, it does not message anyone or change who may answer (the fallback may answer at any time); with no owner named nobody can accept (strict, so a new clinic must name one first); the care link has no end date other than the decision. Not built: the inbox "needs clarification" thread and attachments, receiving-clinician signature evidence, a separate partner SLA policy, e-mail or notice to the fallback.
- **Effects to know:** a closed referral can no longer be reached by the doctor (a second decision answers 403, not 409); the dev "Demo Clinic" has no owner until its administrator names one on the Referrals page.
- **Checked:** 14 new tests (`test_referral_inbox.py`), and the referral, attention, work queue, work history and reports tests updated where a receptionist used to decide (now the named doctor does): 111 passed in one run. The tests found one real bug (the new "responsible" field was not saved on a decision), fixed. Lint, types, import rules, both app type-checks and robots 03 and 06 are clean.

### DEV-110: a guardian's access has scopes, and records stay closed (TEMPORARY, from audit item 3)

- **What was done:** GHE-06 section 06, screen W05 and test AT-18 say a proxy's scope is the minimum necessary, chosen per grant (appointments, messages, billing, records "BLOCK"), and a request outside the scope is denied field by field. Before, every approved guardian could read everything the guardian role lists. Now each grant carries `rights` (migration `0016`). Clinic staff **choose the scopes when they approve** (at least one; the screen starts with only "appointments" ticked; medical records are not on the list). The policy engine passes the kind of data being asked for and the trust module checks the grant lists it: booking and workflow need "appointments", messages need "messages", referrals need "referrals", insurance needs "insurance", and **diagnostics, medication and billing need "records", which cannot be granted at all yet**. The dependent's own name needs no scope. A guardian's own record is not affected. The guardian sees in words what they were allowed; staff see the scopes on the request.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** the four scopes and what each covers; "records" blocked for everyone until the lead defines market and evidence rules; the default tick of "appointments" only; existing grants were given every scope except records so nothing breaks today; the internal primitive `approve_proxy_grant` defaults to all four scopes for trusted callers (the screen never does). Not built: changing the scopes of an approved grant (revoke and ask again), field-level hiding inside a scope, age or capacity triggers (AT-16), proof evidence for the legal relationship (AT-15), and a guardian's view that previews what they can see.
- **Checked:** 7 new tests (`test_guardian_scope.py`); family, people and policy tests updated for the new approve input and run once: 75 passed. A break-check (scope passing switched off) made 3 of the new tests fail, then was restored. Lint, types, import rules, both app type-checks and robot 06 are clean.

### DEV-111: a person can state why they are acting, and "continuity of care" is read only (TEMPORARY, from audit item 6)

- **What was done:** GHE-06 asks every request to carry a purpose ("why") that is checked against the role. Before, the purpose came only from the role (a doctor was always "direct care"), nobody stated a reason, and "continuity of care" was never used. Now a request may carry `X-Zoiko-Purpose`. It is only a claim: the policy engine still refuses it unless a role the person holds may act under it (a patient claiming direct care, or reception staff claiming it, see nothing; a doctor claiming "operations" sees nothing). A malformed value is ignored. The purpose is recorded in every access decision and shows to the patient in words in "who looked at me" ("to keep your care going"). **"Continuity of care" is read only** (`READ_ONLY_PURPOSES`): under it a doctor can read earlier care but cannot refer, book or decide. The doctor app has a selector at the top ("To treat my patients" or "To follow up earlier care (read only)"); the choice is sent with every request and kept for the visit only.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** the two purposes a doctor can choose; that continuity is read only (the documents name the purposes but do not say what each allows); the default stays "direct care" when nothing is stated; the choice is per visit, not per patient; no free-text reason and no extra step-up for a record the doctor has never treated (GHE-06 "lawful authority"). Not built: a stated reason per patient opened, review of unusual access, and purposes for staff beyond the single one their role allows.
- **Checked:** 7 new tests (`test_purpose.py`); policy, prohibition, clinician and referral tests ran once: 62 passed. Lint, types, the doctor app type-check and robots 03 and 06 are clean.

### DEV-112: sign-in pages follow the owner's Zoiko Access design (DESIGN)

- **What was done:** the four sign-in pages (patient, staff, doctor, platform) share one frame, built after the owner's design image for Zoiko Access (an earlier image that was for a different product, "Zoikorum", was used first and then replaced; no Zoikorum wording remains). Deep indigo and purple brand panel with the text "ZOIKO ACCESS" wordmark, a headline with a highlighted middle ("Your gateway to a healthier, smarter tomorrow."), a subtitle, four ringed rows with a title and a line, three small cards (Care, Appointments, Health) and a bottom strip; a white card with Login, Register and Reset password tabs (patient app only, the one with those flows), the wordmark, "Welcome back!", the four outside sign-in buttons, email and password with Show, "Remember me" and "Forgot password?" on one row, "Log In", "New to Zoiko Access? Register", and a footer. The purple belongs to these pages only. The form area is the page's `main` landmark. The button now reads "Log In" and the robots' helper was updated.
- **Not copied from the image, or changed (it would mislead or invent):** the doctor photograph and the phone and laptop pictures (image files that were not supplied; they can be dropped in as assets); working Microsoft, Google, Apple and company sign-in (none exists: the four buttons are shown switched off with "Single sign-in is not set up yet"; the Apple and company icons are simple stand-ins, not the real logos); **"Remember me for 30 days" is shown switched off** (today a login lasts 8 hours and is forgotten when the tab closes; a 30-day login needs a decision on lifetime and storage); Privacy, Terms, Accessibility and Help are shown as plain text, not links (those pages do not exist yet); the line "© 2026 Zoiko Tech Inc." is the owner's. Two of the owner's feature lines were softened because nothing backs them yet: "Trusted and compliant" became "Private by design" (no compliance review has been done) and "Better outcomes, insights for healthier lives" became "Clear insights: see your care and who has looked at it" (nothing measures outcomes).
- **Open for the product owner:** (the signed-in screens now follow the purple: DEV-124). Supply the photo and device pictures if wanted. Decide the "remember me" lifetime.
- **Checked:** type-check of all four apps; screenshots of the patient and staff pages against the design; an accessibility scan of all four pages and the patient's Register and Reset tabs found nothing (colour contrast included); robots 01, 02, 03, 05, 10 and 11 (21 tests) pass.

### DEV-113: message states: who opened a message, unread per person, and a fingerprint of each email (FIX, from the audit's messages item)

- **What was done:** GHE-16 keeps delivery, secure viewing and clinical review as separate states, treats an open as optional best-effort evidence that is never a clinician's acknowledgment, and says provider acceptance is not delivery. Built for our in-app messages: a record (`MessageView`, migration `0003`, row-level security, **append-only by database trigger**) that a signed-in person opened a message written by the other side, one row per login and message. A message now says, to the side that wrote it, when the other side first opened it ("Opened by the clinic 14:05. That means a person opened it, not that it has been answered", or "Sent. The clinic has not opened it yet"); nobody is told which person opened it. **Unread is per person** (`unread_for_me`): one staff member's reply or opening no longer clears it for another. The clinic-wide waiting count that drives the work queue and reports is unchanged: it still clears when the clinic answers. For email, the queue now keeps a SHA-256 fingerprint of subject and body (`content_digest`, foundation migration `0003`) after the body, which can hold a one-time link, is wiped; and "sent" is documented as "accepted by the mail server", not delivered or read.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** an opening is recorded only when someone opens the thread in the app (not on a reply, an export or a list); the replier's own messages never count as seen by themselves; a guardian and the patient count as the same side. Not built: separate delivery-attempt and receipt tables for email (only the attempt counter and last error class exist), provider receipts, patient-visible "delivered" for each email, quiet hours and channel preferences, and the message "quarantine" and escalation steps.
- **Checked:** 10 new tests (`test_message_states.py`); lint, types, import rules, and both app type-checks are clean. **Not run:** the existing messaging, export, grants, row-level-security, mail, work-queue and attention tests, because a wider run was stopped; the append-only list in `foundation/dbgrants.py` gained `messaging_messageview`, so the grants and isolation tests are the ones to run first.

### DEV-114: the audit record says who, why, which rules and which build, inside the hash (FIX, from the audit's audit-record item)

- **What was done:** the specification's audit event (GHE-10) lists actor kind, purpose, session, source service, build and policy fingerprints; we stored only actor, action, object, outcome, reason and request id. Now each audit row also carries: `actor_kind` (patient, clinician, facility user...), `purpose_code` (the reason the person stated or their role's default, DEV-111), `session_ref` (a 16-character fingerprint of the sign-in token, never the token), `source_service`, `build_digest` (from the new `ZOIKO_BUILD` setting), and `policy_digest` (a fingerprint of the access rules in force, written on every access decision so a reviewer can tell exactly which rules decided it). The person's details are attached only to events that person caused; an event written for someone else, or by a system job, carries none. **They are inside the tamper-evident hash**: rows carry a `hash_version` (1 = the original layout, which older rows keep and still verify; 2 = also covers the six new fields), so changing any of them is detected like any other edit. Production now refuses to start while `ZOIKO_BUILD` is empty or "dev".
- **Not built (still the audit's gap list):** organization on each row, a separate "step-up" result (the three outcomes remain allowed, denied, failed), signed checkpoints over ranges, opening an incident automatically on a broken chain, and a policy digest on non-access events (it is empty there on purpose).
- **Checked:** 10 new tests (`test_audit_who.py`) with the existing audit tests (25 passed), and the release-check tests (74 passed, 1 skipped as before); lint, types and import rules are clean. The wider suite was not rerun.

### DEV-115: a service reaches patients only after request, second-person review and publishing (FIX, from the audit's catalog item)

- **What was done:** GHE-14 WF-04 gives a listing the path DRAFT, EVIDENCE_PENDING, REVIEWED, PUBLISHED, says a provider "may request listing but cannot self-award verified status", and says a regulated listing needs a second person; an unpublished or withdrawn listing must not take new bookings or referrals (WF-02, T-60). Before, any service was bookable the moment staff made it. Now a service made in the app **starts as a draft** (`Service.listing_state`, migration `0003`; the clinic's own on/off switch `status` stays separate). Reception or an administrator **asks for a listing** with an evidence reference (5 to 200 characters, a reference such as a registration number, never patient details); a clinic administrator (R13) **who did not ask** reviews it or sends it back with a reason; a clinic administrator who did not ask **publishes** it. An administrator can **withdraw** a listing with a reason (new bookings and referrals stop; existing bookings stay) and a withdrawn service can **start again** from draft. Patients see, book, join a waiting list for, and are referred to only services that are **active and published**: the patient list, the category menu, booking, referral issue and waiting-list join all check it. Every step is audited. Services that already existed were set to published by the migration, so nothing disappears today. The staff Services page shows each service's state with the next step for that person.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** "regulated" is treated as always true, so a second person is always needed (a clinic with one administrator cannot publish a service alone; they ask another administrator or Zoiko to add one); the independent reviewer is another administrator of the same clinic (platform staff cannot read a customer's data, so they cannot be the verifier); evidence is a text reference only (no file upload or registry check); the states UNVERIFIED and REVIEW REQUIRED, scheduled re-verification and expiry are not built (a withdrawn listing is "suspended"); the development console and test fixtures create services already published.
- **Checked:** 12 new tests (`test_listing_states.py`); the 56 existing tests whose setup makes a service through the app (waitlist, referral, referral inbox, work queue, work history, attention, reports, profile, directory) and one API test were fixed with a set-up helper that publishes (`publish_service` in `tests/conftest.py`) and the whole group, with API, reschedule, team, late, my-data and scheduling tests, then ran once: 263 passed; the API file reran after its last fix: 17 passed. Lint, types, import rules and the staff app type-check are clean; robots 01 and 06 pass. The full suite was not rerun.

### DEV-116: three events the specification names are now emitted (FIX, from the audit's events item)

- **What was done:** GHE-06's event list names `delegation.revoked.v1` ("grant ceased to authorize further use"), `professional.privilege.revoked.v1` ("credential or affiliation no longer valid") and `access.denied.v1` ("policy refused a requested action; no patient-identifying event"). None was emitted. Now: ending a guardian's or caregiver's access emits `delegation.revoked.v1` once (grant and delegate references only; repeating the revoke emits nothing); ending a doctor's or care partner's role at a site (R03, R09) emits `professional.privilege.revoked.v1` once (assignment, login, role code and site references); and a refusal that a person actually hit, the explicit authorization of a request, emits `access.denied.v1` carrying only the operation, the purpose and the reason, never who asked or about whom. The quiet checks that filter a list (a "no" means "leave that one out") do not emit, so a list page does not flood the queue; they are still recorded as access decisions as before.
- **Not built:** nothing consumes these events yet (the specification says downstream consumers re-check on delivery: for example, a referral inbox whose named doctor loses the role should be flagged for a fallback, a screen could show the denial); `patient.link.*` and `identity.subject.proofed.v1` events need patient matching and identity proofing, which do not exist.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests (`test_spec_events.py`); the policy, family, people, trust, prohibition, outbox and team tests ran once: 134 passed. Lint, types and import rules are clean.

### DEV-117: the referral inbox reacts when its named doctor leaves the site (FIX, uses DEV-116)

- **What was done:** GHE-06 says that when a professional privilege is revoked, service actions are revoked and a clinical fallback is required. The referral inbox (DEV-109) now consumes `professional.privilege.revoked.v1`. If the doctor who lost the doctor role at a site was that site's named owner, the fallback becomes the owner; if there was no fallback, nobody is named (the staff Referrals page already says "Nobody is named yet" and no referral can be accepted until someone is). If it was the fallback, the fallback is cleared. Referrals already waiting move to the new owner, the leaver's access given by those referrals ends, and the change is audited as the system's own act (`referral.inbox_changed`, reason `role_ended`). A doctor who has the role again by the time the event arrives is left alone (the event is out of date), and a doctor who was never named changes nothing.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** the fallback is promoted automatically (rather than leaving the inbox unnamed for an administrator to decide); nobody is told by message that the inbox changed (the page shows it and the audit records it); only the doctor role (R03) counts, not home-care partners.
- **Checked:** 8 new tests (`test_referral_privilege.py`); the referral, referral inbox, dispatch, events, team and trust tests ran once: 89 passed. Lint, types and import rules are clean.

### DEV-118: incidents use GHE-20's SEV-0 to SEV-3 scale (TEMPORARY, from the audit's incident item and DEV-014)

- **What was done:** the documents give three different severity scales (GHE-09, GHE-18 and GHE-20) and our log used the provisional words low, medium and high. GHE-20 is the only document that says what each level means: SEV-0 immediate patient safety, a large unlawful disclosure or a systemic compromise; SEV-1 a major regional loss or a critical workflow failure; SEV-2 a partial service degradation; SEV-3 a minor operational issue (proposed internal classes that do not replace jurisdiction-specific notifiable-incident definitions). The incident log now uses those four levels (`sev0` to `sev3`), the form shows each with its meaning, and the platform overview counts open SEV-0 and SEV-1 incidents as the serious ones (the key keeps its old name `high_incidents`). The migration maps existing incidents: high becomes SEV-1, medium SEV-2, low SEV-3; none becomes SEV-0, because nothing old was recorded as patient safety or a disclosure (it can be raised by hand); the old words are now refused.
- **Still open (DEV-014):** which scale the owners choose, an on-call or notification process, and the legal notifiable-incident rules. The incident log is still a hand-written note that raises no alarm.
- **Checked:** the incident, platform-work and platform tests ran once (75 passed, labels and one SEV-0 case updated); lint, types, import rules and the platform app type-check are clean; robot 06 passes.

### DEV-119: guardian links use the specification's eight statuses (FIX, from the audit's status-words item)

- **What was done:** the specification's relationship-grant status list is PROPOSED, PROOF_REQUIRED, APPROVAL_PENDING, ACTIVE, REJECTED, SUSPENDED, REVOKED, EXPIRED (GHE-06, enums); we had proposed, active and revoked, and a refused request was stored as "revoked". Now: staff can **ask for proof** (a new request becomes PROOF_REQUIRED) and **mark it received** (APPROVAL_PENDING, ready to decide); approving works from any waiting state, so the steps are optional; a refused request is **REJECTED**, not revoked; staff can **pause** an active link with a reason (SUSPENDED; it authorizes nothing, the guardian is told it is paused, and it can be resumed unless it has run out); an active link past its end date reads as **EXPIRED** (worked out when read, not stored). All three waiting states give no access and count as "waiting" everywhere (the staff queue, the patient's list, the per-person limit). The staff page shows each request's state with its next step, plus a card of approved links with Pause and Resume; the patient's page explains each state in words. Pausing announces `delegation.revoked.v1` once (the grant ceased to authorize); resuming announces nothing. This also fixed a mistake in DEV-116: a request that was never active (refused or withdrawn) used to announce a revoked grant; now only a link that had been in use does.
- **My choices where the documents are silent (please confirm):** the new waiting steps carry no note or document (proof is checked at the desk, as before); a suspension needs a reason of 3 to 200 characters, kept in the audit; no second person is needed to approve, pause or resume; expiry is not announced as an event and nothing sweeps expired links (they simply stop working).
- **Checked:** 10 new tests (`test_grant_states.py`); the family, guardian scope, people, events and policy tests ran once with them: 90 passed. Lint, types, import rules, both app type-checks and robot 06 are clean.

### DEV-120: each clinic sets its own response target (FIX, from the audit's "our own 24 hours" note)

- **What was done:** the flat 24-hour target was one service-wide setting (`RESPONSE_TARGET_HOURS`) used in six places, and the specification says there is no universal timeout. Now each clinic has its own target (`Organization.response_target_hours`, migration `0019`; empty means the service default). Its administrator (R13) sets it on the Settings page, 1 to 168 hours, or goes back to the standard; the change is audited. It now decides what "late" means for that clinic everywhere: the late flag on bookings, the dashboard late counts, the daily late-work email, the due time of new work items (confirm a booking, answer a message, decide a referral) and when a waiting referral is flagged for escalation. The reminder lead time and the waiting-list offer time stay service-wide.
- **Not built:** a different target per kind of work, per service or per urgency (a referral marked urgent still waits the same time); a target per working day or opening hours; changing the due time of work that was already opened (only new work and the periodic sync use the new value).
- **Checked:** 15 new tests (`test_response_target.py`); the digest, late, settings, work queue, referral inbox and today tests ran once: 65 passed. Lint, types, import rules and the staff app type-check are clean. The page itself has not been opened in a browser.

### DEV-121: every email send attempt is kept, never overwritten (FIX, from the audit's messages item)

- **What was done:** GHE-16's DeliveryAttempt is an "immutable attempt history"; the mail queue kept only an attempts counter and the last error class, so an email that failed twice and then went through left no trace of the failures. Now each try writes one row (`EmailAttempt`, foundation migration `0004`, **append-only by database trigger**): which email, the attempt number, "accepted" or "failed", and the failure's class name. It holds no address, no text and no error message, and a repeat of the same attempt number (a crashed worker reporting twice) is ignored. "Accepted" means the mail server took it, not that anyone received or read it. The platform overview now shows how many send tries failed in the last 24 hours (and the stats count accepted tries too).
- **Not built:** delivery receipts (delivered, bounced, opened) because our mail server gives none; per-recipient or per-tenant counts (the table is cell-wide, like the queue); retry policy per kind of email; a screen listing the attempts of one email.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests added to `test_email.py` (history kept across failures, nothing personal stored, cannot be changed or removed, repeats ignored, 24-hour counts) and 2 existing assertions updated for the new stats keys; the mail, grants and platform tests ran once. Lint, types, import rules and the platform app type-check are clean.

### DEV-122: a command moves every encrypted value onto the current key (FIX, from the audit's encryption item)

- **What was done:** the encryption notes said a key can be rotated by adding a new one, but nothing re-encrypted old values, so an old key could never be retired. Now `python manage.py rotate_field_keys` (with `--dry-run` to count first and `--batch` for size) re-encrypts every stored value that still uses an old key: patient details (name, birth date, phone), staff display names, message subjects and bodies, and insurance member numbers, for every tenant. Each value keeps the same context binding (tenant, table, field, row), so a value that is in the wrong place or damaged cannot be read and is **counted and left exactly as it was**; nothing is deleted or guessed. It is safe to run twice or stop part-way (a value already on the current key is skipped; each tenant's tables change in one transaction). The command prints counts only, never a value. The steps are in `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md`. Each module registers its own tables with `foundation/rotation.py`, so a new encrypted column only needs a few lines.
- **Not built (still open):** where the master key lives (a managed key service: DEV-016), automatic or scheduled rotation, a periodic report of how many values are still on an old key, and encrypting the login and email columns (they are stored in the clear).
- **Checked:** 8 new tests (`test_key_rotation.py`: one value, wrong place refused, dry run changes nothing, every kind of value moves and the old key can then be retired with reads still working, a second run finds nothing, every tenant is rotated, a damaged value is left alone while the rest move, no readable text in the output); the crypto and release-check tests ran with them: 89 passed, 1 skipped as before. Lint, types and import rules are clean.

### DEV-123: the staff patient list no longer shows an identity mark nothing sets (FIX, from the audit)

- **What was done:** the staff patient list had an "Identity" column that read "unverified" for everyone, because no process ever sets a person's identity status. That suggested a check that does not exist. The column is removed from the screen; the stored field and the API value stay (it is always "unverified"), and the existing tests that read it still pass. GHE-06 says identity assurance is a per-market decision from an approved proofing provider and separate from the login, so building a "verified" state now would invent one. A desk check by clinic staff (like the doctor name check) is possible later, as a recorded fact and not an assurance level, once the owners choose.
- **Checked:** the staff app type-checks. No backend change.

### DEV-124: the signed-in screens use the sign-in design's indigo and purple (DESIGN)

- **What was done:** the owner's Zoiko Access design is indigo and purple (DEV-112 applied it to the sign-in pages only and left the question open). The four apps' brand colours now match: the active-menu and button colour, the soft highlight, the side menu and the logo tile move from blue to purple, in light and dark mode; neutrals (page, card, text, lines) and the green, amber and red status colours are unchanged. One existing dark-mode problem was fixed on the way: the patient app's notification badge was white on light red (contrast 2.2:1); it now uses the same deep red as the menu counters.
- **Checked:** type-check of all four apps; screenshots of the signed-in staff screen; the accessibility robot over every page of all four apps in light mode (11 robot tests with the booking, confirm and page-load robots, all pass); and a colour-contrast scan of the staff, patient and doctor dashboards in dark mode (the badge fault was found and fixed; the platform dashboard was not scanned in dark mode). The robots' API address is now set with `E2E_API` (default port 8000).

### DEV-125: an AI permit's tool lists are pinned by a fingerprint and checked on every run (FIX, from the audit's AI gate item)

- **What was done:** GHE-23 treats the model, prompt and tool policy as immutable versions and says a permit carries a tool-policy hash (INV-06, AIUsePermit). Our permit's notes said it "pins the tool list by fingerprint" but stored only plain lists, so a change to them would go unnoticed. Now a permit stores a SHA-256 fingerprint of its three tool lists (allowed, write, two-person; order does not matter) when it is made, and every run recomputes it from the stored lists: if they no longer match, the run is **rejected** with the flag `tool_policy_changed` and nothing is called. A real model may also declare the tool-policy fingerprint it runs (`register_provider(..., tool_digest=...)`) and is refused as `digest_mismatch` if it differs from the permit, like the model and prompt already were. Each run's evidence now records the tool fingerprint it ran under. Permits made before this got their fingerprint from their lists by the migration.
- **Still not built (the audit's AI list):** a step that removes or minimises personal details before the model is called (the spec has no list of what to remove, and a text scrubber would be a guess); a check that a permit's lane and level agree (the specification gives lanes as examples and levels separately, with no pairing table); and the use of "quarantined" for an empty answer (the spec gives it no such meaning).
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_ai.py` (fingerprint recorded and order-independent, tampered lists stop the run, a provider with the wrong tool fingerprint is refused and only the right one is called, the evidence records it); the AI and AI-staff tests ran once: 53 passed. Lint, types and import rules are clean.

### DEV-126: patients can file formal data-rights requests; the clinic tracks them and an administrator closes them (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-06 lists `policy.rights_case` (access, correction, erasure, portability, restriction, objection, consent withdrawal), identity verification and a closing authority, but gave no screens, states or deadlines. New module `modules/rights`: a patient files one request (note up to 500 characters, sealed with the field key and included in key rotation); the clinic's staff (R04, R13) start it and record the identity check; only a clinic administrator (R13) completes or declines it. Completing needs the identity check first; declining needs a reason of 3 to 300 characters that the patient sees. The patient can withdraw an open request. One open request per kind, at most 5 open at once. Filing links the patient to the clinic's site. Every step is audited without the wording of the request. New patient card (Privacy page) and staff page ("Data requests").
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the five states (received, in progress, completed, declined, withdrawn), the limits, and the 3 to 300 reason length. No deadline is built in because it differs by country; one now comes from a signed country pack when the customer's country is set (DEV-133, open question for Legal, with DEV-013). Guardians cannot file for a dependant (it needs proof of authority we do not hold). Completing a request does not delete anything by itself; the actual erasure or export is still done by a person.
- **Also:** the staff dashboard's "waiting for you" list now counts open data requests (a number only; nothing is read) and links to the Data requests page; `test_attention.py` covers it (5 passed).
- **Checked:** 25 new tests in `test_rights.py` plus 65 neighbouring tests (policy, prohibitions, key rotation, database grants), 90 passed; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; both web apps type-check.

### DEV-127: outbound signed webhooks for a clinic's partners (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-13 (sections 07-08, WF-09, WF-10) describes signed webhooks, at-least-once delivery, retries with jitter, a dead-letter list, and subscriptions that go live only after the address is verified. It gives no numbers and no screens. New module `modules/integration`: a clinic administrator (R13 only) adds a public https address and picks event families (appointments, messages, waiting list, free times, family requests, family access, doctor status). The address is verified by a signed challenge it must echo. Each event then becomes a delivery of a small reference-only message, signed with HMAC-SHA256 over `time.delivery id.body` with that address's own sealed secret (the delivery id was added to what is signed in DEV-131, as GHE-13 requires) (`Zoiko-Signature: t=,kid=,v1=` and `Zoiko-Delivery-Id` headers). Redirects are never followed and an address that resolves to a private network is refused at send time. The administrator can pause, resume (only if verified), revoke (final), rotate the secret (shown once; the old one stops working) and send a parked delivery again. A new worker command `deliver_webhooks` (service `webhooks` in `docker-compose.yml`) sends what is due. New staff page "Integrations".
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** at most 10 live addresses and 10 event families each; 8 attempts, waits of 30 s doubling to 1 h with 0.5x-1.5x jitter; a 400-class answer (except 408, 425, 429) is final at once; 10 parked deliveries in a row pause the address; a 5 second timeout; only https, port 443. The event families are the ones the system already emits (access-denied events are never offered). Nothing is exactly once, as the spec says: a receiver must ignore a delivery id it has seen.
- **Not built:** inbound signed callbacks (WF-08), partner client credentials and rate limits, the OpenAPI/AsyncAPI documents and sample SDKs, certification, and any clinical payload (the spec says to send an event plus a retrieval reference, so only references are sent). The address check reads DNS just before sending; a hostile name server could still answer differently to the connection itself (a known limit of this simple guard).
- **Checked:** 37 new tests in `test_webhooks.py` (registering, who may use it, bad addresses and topics, the challenge, signature, only references sent, one delivery per event, retry with the same id, final refusals, giving up and sending again, pausing, revoking, secret rotation including the field-key move, separation between customers, audit without the address or secret), plus 65 neighbouring tests, 102 passed; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on it.

### DEV-128: country packs are recorded and reviewed, never made live (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-04 defines a country capability pack as a signed, enforceable "authorization envelope" with a lifecycle DRAFT, VALIDATED, EXPERT_REVIEW, SIGNED, STAGED_IN_CELL, PREFLIGHT and CANARY, a local go-live gate, ACTIVE, then SUSPENDED or RETIRED, with independent approvals bound to the exact content, and it says a dashboard status is never evidence of a licence. New module `modules/jurisdiction` and a platform page "Country packs". A pack (country, service, revision) carries a typed manifest: data-request deadline in days, emergency number, default language, time zone, which capabilities it switches on, an evidence status per kind (legal, privacy assessment, security review, clinical; the documents themselves stay in the country's own vault) and a required review-by date. Unknown fields are refused. Its fingerprint (SHA-256 of the canonical content) changes with any edit. Only a draft can be edited. "Checked" needs nothing required to be missing. Two different people then approve the exact content (a support officer R11 for legal, a safety officer R12 for clinical; neither the author) and the second approval signs it. Sending it back clears the approvals. Either kind may suspend (with a reason); a suspended pack returns only as a new draft revision. Every step is audited. To let safety officers attest, R12 now has `attest` on `platform` and R11 has it too (nothing else changed in the rule table).
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** stand-ins for approvers (the spec names country legal counsel and a clinical lead; no such roles exist), the "approved on record" wording for SIGNED, the manifest fields and limits (days 1 to 365), the required review-by date, and that diagnostics, clinical AI and record exchange can only be off because they are not built.
- **Not built, on purpose:** everything from STAGED_IN_CELL onward. There is no way to make a pack live, and `active` is not even a status: staging in a cell, preflight, canary and the local go-live gate do not exist. There are no digital signatures or revocation epochs (the approvals are rows in our own database, not signatures by a certificate), no jurisdiction resolver that applies packs to requests at run time, and no link from a customer to a country. The data-request deadline is now applied to new data requests (DEV-133); nothing else in the product reads a pack to switch a feature on or off.
- **Checked:** 41 new tests in `test_packs.py` (content rules and refusals, the fingerprint, who may do what, order of steps, the two approvals and the author rule, send-back clearing approvals, suspend, new revision, retire, expiry display, no way to go live, audit trail) plus 157 neighbouring policy, prohibition, platform, release, incident and grant tests; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the platform app type-checks and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on it.

### DEV-129: "Remember me for 30 days" for patients only (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** the sign-in screens showed a disabled "Remember me for 30 days" box because the length and the rules were never decided. A patient who ticks it now gets a 30-day session instead of the 8-hour one; the backend honours it only for a patient and only for a literal `true` (anything else, such as "yes" or 1, is treated as not asking). Staff, doctors and Zoiko's own staff always get the 8-hour session whatever they send, and their box stays switched off. The patient app keeps a remembered session in this device's long-term storage until it expires (and removes it once expired); an ordinary session stays in per-tab storage as before. A remembered login is recorded in the audit trail with the reason "remembered".
- **Why it is safe enough to build before a decision:** the session token already carries a counter that "Sign out" (which signs the patient out everywhere), a password change and a password reset all raise, so any remembered session stops working at once; nothing new had to be invented for ending it. The box says "use only your own device".
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the 30-day length (the sign-in design shows it, the specification gives no number), patients only, and that "Sign out" ends every remembered device rather than just this one.
- **Not built:** a list of the patient's signed-in devices, asking for the password again before a sensitive action while on a remembered session, a shorter life on shared or public devices, and a refresh scheme (a remembered session simply ends at 30 days).
- **Checked:** 18 new tests in `test_remember.py` (30 days for a patient who asks, 8 hours otherwise, never for staff or doctors, only a plain `true` counts, expiry on the 30th day, sign-out, password change and audit entry end or record it, a wrong password with the box ticked is the same refusal) plus the 38 existing auth tests, all passing; ruff and mypy clean; all four apps type-check; new browser robot 14 (a ticked patient stays signed in on a device that kept only long-term storage and "Sign out" ends it, an unticked patient does not, staff cannot tick) and the accessibility scan of the sign-in pages pass.

### DEV-130: request measurements, two service-level indicators and a metrics endpoint (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-18 (section 05, WF-19) wants SLIs measured on what people experience, not "the pod is up", with a safe aggregate that may leave a region, and it PROPOSES 99.9% monthly availability and p95 under 750 ms. New `foundation/metrics.py` and a middleware that counts and times every request by route (the URL pattern, for example `api/v1/bookings/<str:booking_id>/confirm`, never the real address; no tenant, person or query string; odd characters are replaced; at most 200 distinct routes, the rest are "other"). Two indicators: availability (share of requests that did not fail with a server error; a 4xx is a valid answer) and speed (share answered within 750 ms, target 95%), each with a burn rate (how many times faster than allowed the error budget is being used) over 5 and 60 minutes. Health probes and the metrics endpoint are not counted. `GET /metrics` gives the cumulative numbers in Prometheus text format; it is **off** unless `ZOIKO_METRICS_TOKEN` (24+ characters) is set, needs the exact bearer token, and answers 404 otherwise. Platform staff (R11, R12) also get `platform/slo` and a "Service levels" page showing the windows, the busiest routes and what the numbers do NOT tell you.
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** counting 4xx as good, the 750 ms "within" reading of p95 (a share rather than a percentile estimate), 5- and 60-minute windows, a 25-route summary, and a 24-character minimum for the token.
- **Not built, stated plainly:** the numbers live in the memory of one web process (several workers each report their own; a restart resets them), the longest window is one hour, so the 30-day budget, error-budget decisions, burn alerts and paging do not exist; there are no journey SLIs (a booking or handoff actually being correct), no clinical safety sentinel, no traces, no per-cell or per-partner split, no dependency map and no runbooks. A green number here says requests succeeded and were quick, nothing more.
- **Also fixed (found by the accessibility scan on the new page):** the main content area of all four apps could scroll without keyboard access when a page had nothing focusable in it; it is now a tab stop.
- **Checked:** 19 new tests in `test_metrics.py` (the two indicators and their burn rates, client errors not counted as failures, no verdict with no traffic, windows and the one-hour cap, health probes excluded, labels carry no references or odd characters, the route cap, buckets only grow, the endpoint is off without a token and needs the exact one, nothing personal in it, a real request is counted, who may see the platform view) plus 133 neighbouring health, error, settings, release-check and database-role tests (1 skipped as before); ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the platform app type-checks; robots 6, 10, 13 and 14 pass on all four apps.

### DEV-131: partners can send us signed messages, which are checked and recorded only (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-13 WF-08 (inbound partner webhook verification) and WF-04 (partner credentials). `POST /api/v1/partner-events` takes a partner's message with no sign-in: the signature is the credential. It is checked in a fixed order and stops at the first failure: body over 4 KB (413); malformed headers, a timestamp more than 5 minutes from now, a key that names no live partner of a live customer, or a bad signature (all the same 401, so nothing is learned about which part failed; the signature is checked in constant time over `time.delivery id.exact body`); a partner over its per-minute budget (429 with Retry-After); a repeated delivery id (the same content is accepted again without storing it twice, different content under the same id is refused 409); only then is the body parsed, and one of the wrong shape or an event kind the partner is not allowed to send is HELD (422, only its fingerprint and the reason are kept, not the content). An accepted message is stored (202). A clinic administrator adds partners (a secret shown once and stored sealed; a key id that names the customer, `pc.<customer id>.<random>`, so the signature can be checked inside that customer only), says which kinds each may send (they must start with `partner.`), and can suspend (refused at once, evidence kept), resume, revoke (final) or rotate the secret. New cards on the Integrations page list partners and what arrived.
- **Also changed (DEV-127):** the spec says the signature covers the timestamp AND the delivery id with the body. The outbound webhook signature left the delivery id out; it is now `time.delivery id.body` on both sides, and the tests were updated. Nothing had been deployed.
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the 4 KB body limit, the 5-minute skew, 60 messages a minute per partner (fixed, not yet editable), at most 10 live partners and 10 allowed kinds each, the `partner.` prefix for inbound kinds, and rotation without an overlap window.
- **Not built, stated plainly:** the message does nothing: the spec sends a verified message on to the workflow engine for semantic checks, and that does not exist, so no booking, patient or clinical record is ever changed by an inbound message. No client certificates or proof of key possession, no sandbox/production split, no certification or dual approval, no dual-key overlap during rotation, no schema or profile versions per partner, no patient or order matching, no per-partner alerting for failed signatures (failures are not stored, to avoid a flood), no foreign-region check (there is one region). A valid signature proves who sent the bytes, not that a named licensed person attested anything.
- **Checked:** 36 new tests in `test_partner_events.py` (credential rules, sealing and the move to a new encryption key, who may manage, a good message kept, eleven different identity failures all the same 401 and nothing stored, the body not read before the signature is proved, oversize, repeats and a reused id, held messages for each wrong shape and for a disallowed kind, per-partner budgets, suspend, resume, revoke, rotate, separation between customers, audit without the secret) plus the 37 webhook tests and the neighbouring key-rotation, grant and policy tests, 96 passed with key rotation, grants and policy; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on it.

### DEV-132: a workflow engine kernel that moves only on evidence (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-12 describes a durable, cell-local orchestration service: versioned definitions, instances with an accountable owner, transitions that need evidence, an independent health overlay, timers and escalations, and one tamper-evident history. New module `modules/orchestration` (separate from `modules/workflow`, the staff work queue, which it does not touch) and a staff page "Workflow engine".
  - **Definitions** are versioned and frozen once published; a change is a new version and running instances stay on theirs (WF-12). A path is the universal states in order; only the clinical-review and handoff states may be left out, everything else is mandatory (section 04). A timer policy must state its own minutes: there is no default (section 06). Only a clinic administrator (R13) writes them. A definition that traverses a clinical-review or handoff state cannot be published until `WORKFLOW_CLINICAL_ENABLED` is turned on (WFL-06: clinical pathways stay off).
  - **Instances** start `REQUESTED` with the request's origin as evidence, a published definition, a named real owner and a subject reference; one subject has one live workflow. A step moves only to the NEXT state of the definition, with evidence of the kind that edge needs (validation, acceptance attestation, work start, output reference, review attestation, handoff acceptance, closure evidence). Transport receipts, e-mail sent, read receipts, downloads, elapsed time, payment received, a silent partner and "notification delivered" are refused by name (the spec's "disallowed shortcuts"). Every move names the version it expects (compare-and-swap, a stale one is a conflict, never retried) and carries an idempotency key (the same request again returns the first answer). Only the accountable owner or an administrator may move it (DEV-135: but a review or a handoff only the owner).
  - **Health overlay** (clear, at risk, exception, reconciling) is separate and never moves the state; changing it needs a reason, and it cannot be cleared while an escalation still waits for a person.
  - **Block, cancel, close:** blocking needs a reason, evidence and a named incident owner and freezes the instance until that owner or an administrator lifts it with resolution evidence; cancelling needs a reason and authorisation evidence and is final. Closing needs the closure predicate (last step reached, no open exception or reconciling overlay, no escalation waiting) and stores a digest of the whole history. "Closed" is documented everywhere as not meaning recovered or treated.
  - **Timers and escalation:** entering a state starts the definition's timers and leaving it stops them. When one runs out (a worker job every 30 s, `orchestration.timers`) the engine checks the step is still the one watched, marks the instance at risk and opens an escalation for the named owner. Only a person recording that a human was reached and took ownership closes it; an unsuccessful try is counted and may hand it to a fallback person. Nothing automatic ever says anyone was contacted (WF-08).
  - **History:** every step (move, overlay, owner change, timer) is one chained, append-only row (the database refuses UPDATE and DELETE) with the actor, role, purpose, evidence, definition version, the policy digest and the access decision in force, written in the same transaction as the change. The page shows each history and whether the chain still checks out. Staff see a workbench of what needs a person.
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the evidence kind names and the `ref` shape, owner-or-administrator as "the authoritative owner", the three timer kinds, the 30-second timer check, limits (6 timers, 8 evidence items, 300-character notes), clearing health needs a reason, and a block being lifted by its incident owner or an administrator.
- **Not built, stated plainly:** no durable engine vendor, so "durable" here means state, history and timers in our database with a worker (crash recovery works because everything is in the database, but there is no replay engine or worker versioning). No partner handoff with the four separate acknowledgements, no result review or amendment handling (WF-06), no outbox-driven external commands and no PENDING_RECONCILIATION against a source, no compensation (WF-11), no retry budgets or dead-letter handling for external calls (WF-10), no definition migration between versions (WF-12 option b), no per-country timer or deadline rules from a pack, no patient-facing status screen (WF-18), no operator console beyond viewing, recording an escalation and the API, and nothing yet starts a workflow from an inbound partner message (DEV-131) or from a booking. No clinical journey is enabled.
- **Checked:** 94 new tests in `test_orchestration.py` (definition rules, freezing and versions, retiring, who may write, bad paths and timer policies, starting, one live workflow per subject, the full path to a closure digest, every refused shortcut, evidence shape, no skipping or going back, stale versions, idempotency, owner-only moves, clinical paths, health overlay, closure blockers, block and resume, cancel, timers starting and stopping, a timer firing into an escalation for the owner, stale timers doing nothing, escalation outcomes, reassigning, the workbench, filters, the history refusing UPDATE and DELETE, what each step records, audit, separation between customers) plus 150 neighbouring grant, key-rotation, policy and work-queue tests; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on it twice.

### DEV-133: a customer's country, and the data-request deadline a signed pack gives it (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** DEV-126 left data requests with no due date and DEV-128 left packs applying to nothing. This joins them. Zoiko support (R11) records which country a customer operates in (a two-letter ISO code, or empty to clear it) on the new Country card of the platform Settings page; it is audited in both the customer's and the platform's chain, and the platform's own tenant is refused. When a patient files a data request, the system looks for a SIGNED, unexpired, unsuspended country pack for that customer's country and, if one gives a data-request deadline, stamps `due_at` (now plus that many days) and `due_basis` (`pack@revision`) on the request. If several packs for the country disagree, the strictest (smallest) wins. The patient sees "The clinic should answer by <date>"; clinic staff see the date, "overdue" once it passes, and which pack it came from. Packs live in Zoiko's platform tenant, so the lookup reads them there before the customer's own transaction opens (switching tenants inside one would lose the first context).
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the due date is fixed at filing and never recomputed, so a later pack revision, suspension or expiry changes only NEW requests (this follows GHE-12 WF-12, no implicit rebind); "one country per customer" (a customer with several countries, or a patient in another country than the clinic, is not handled); "strictest wins" for overlapping packs (the spec says overlapping rules resolve to deny).
- **Not built, stated plainly:** nothing reads any other pack setting (emergency number, language, time zone, which features are on) and nothing reminds or escalates when a request becomes overdue (the workflow engine, DEV-132, is not yet used by data requests); an overdue request is labelled and, since DEV-140, appears as late work in the clinic's queue. No legal deadline is checked: the number is whatever a signed pack says, and a pack is "approved on record" by two of our own staff, not a lawyer (DEV-128). A customer with no country, or a country with no signed pack, behaves exactly as before.
- **Checked:** 25 new tests in `test_country_deadline.py` (who may set it, the code format, clearing, platform tenant and unknown customer refused, audit in both chains without repeats, no deadline without a country or pack or while unsigned, the deadline and its source on patient and staff views, another country's pack ignored, strictest wins, a suspended or expired pack gives none, an earlier deadline never changes, the lookup itself) plus 158 neighbouring rights, pack, platform, onboarding, grant and key-rotation tests, 183 passed; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the platform, patient and staff apps type-check and robots 6, 10, 13 and 14 pass.

### DEV-134: the patient and platform apps say when what they show is old (FIX, from the audit's GHE-15 item)

- **What was done:** GHE-15 section 05 says a screen must not show stale data as current. The staff and doctor apps already kept the last good data on a failed refresh and said "The information below is old: it was last updated at <time>". The patient app (which refreshes every minute) and the platform app (which refreshes when opened or on "Refresh") did not: on a failed refresh they showed only the error. Both now remember when they last loaded successfully, keep showing that data, and add the same sentence with the time to the error. The error is announced to screen readers as an alert.
- **Not changed:** a screen that has never loaded successfully has nothing to call old, so it shows only the error. The patient app's individual pages (messages, family, referrals) load their own data and still show only their own errors.
- **Checked:** new browser robot 15 (the patient app, with the backend made unreachable after a successful load and a minute passing on a controlled clock, and the platform app, with its overview blocked and "Refresh" pressed, each show the old-information sentence and keep the old page) passed twice; both apps type-check; the every-page and accessibility robots pass on them.

### DEV-135: the workflow engine is checked against the specification's 60 acceptance tests, and three gaps closed (FIX)

- **What was done:** GHE-12 section 12 lists 60 executable acceptance tests (WF-T01 to WF-T60).
