They cannot be run as written (they need partners, country packs and regions that do not exist), so each was read against the engine and its own tests and recorded in `docs/WORKFLOW-TRACE.md`: **18 met, 18 partly, 24 not built.** The table says for every row why, and which of our tests proves it. It is the developer's reading, not an automatic result, and nobody else has reviewed it. Reading them found three clear gaps in the engine, now fixed:
  - **Rebuilding from history (WF-T36, WF-14):** the engine can rebuild an instance's state, health and version from its history alone and compare. Every step changes the version by exactly one, so the version must equal the number of steps. The history page shows the result.
  - **Freezing on disagreement (WF-T32, WF-T33):** a workflow whose state, health, version or history no longer agrees with itself is refused every change with "temporarily blocked" (HTTP 423) until a person looks. Before this it would have carried on over evidence nobody could trust.
  - **No attesting on someone's behalf (WF-T53):** an administrator could previously move a workflow into "reviewed by a clinician" or "next party accepted" for the responsible person. Those two moves now need the accountable owner. An administrator who needs to act must first reassign the workflow to themselves, which is a visible, recorded step. (This is stricter than the earlier rule that an administrator may move anything.)
- **Still open, from the table:** the 24 "not built" rows are mostly things the engine cannot invent: results and corrections from partners, patient matching, country packs applied at run time, regions and failover, locale text, retention holds and the release gate (GHE-19). The "partly" rows are listed with what is missing.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_orchestration.py` (replay agrees; a tampered state, health or version is found and every move, health change and reassignment is refused with 423; an administrator cannot enter a review or a handoff for the owner but can after reassigning) and the whole file passes, 99 tests; ruff and mypy are clean. The other suites were not rerun for this change, which touches only the workflow engine.

### DEV-136: the external-API and webhook work is checked against the specification's 64 acceptance cases (FIX)

- **What was done:** GHE-13 section 15 lists 64 executable cases (T-01 to T-64). They cannot be run as written (they need partners, certificates, sandboxes and regions that do not exist), so each was read against what exists (outbound webhooks DEV-127, partner credentials and signed inbound messages DEV-131, request measurements DEV-130, country packs DEV-128) and recorded in `docs/API-TRACE.md`: **9 met, 19 partly, 36 not built.** Each row says why and which of our tests proves it. It is the developer's reading, not an automatic result, and nobody else has reviewed it.
- **One claim made provable:** the table first read "a redirect is never followed" as met, but no test covered it, so it was marked partly until a test was written: a small local server answers with a redirect and the sender stops and reports it (`test_a_redirect_is_never_followed`). That row is now met.
- **What the table shows:** the signed-message mechanics (signature, timestamp, delivery id, repeats, retries, parking, suspension, private-network and redirect refusal) are largely met. The 36 not-built cases are mostly healthcare interoperability (FHIR, HL7, imaging, terminology), the published OpenAPI and AsyncAPI documents and SDKs, OAuth, and the partner onboarding, sandbox, certification and recertification lifecycle. None of those can be built without a partner, a standards decision and a named approver.
- **Checked:** the new redirect test passes with the rest of the webhook tests (38); ruff is clean. No application code changed.

### DEV-137: a go-live readiness register that records and evaluates, and never goes live (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-20 describes gates G3 (production readiness) and G4 (country go-live), a release tuple, signed and expiring evidence bound to that tuple, vetoes, and a decision of NO_GO, G3_READY, G4_AUTHORIZED, then pilot and live. New module `modules/readiness` and a platform page "Go-live readiness". A release is a fixed tuple (name, build, cell, country, capabilities) with a SHA-256 fingerprint; a different tuple is a different release, so an approval can never be carried to another version or market. Each of six G3 requirements (signed build, security, privacy, requirements coverage, recovery exercised, operations staffed) and four G4 requirements (legal authority, clinical partner, clinical safety, operating entity) needs one signed attestation for that exact fingerprint, with an artefact id and an expiry date within a year; it stops counting when it expires. An eleventh G4 requirement, the country pack, is signed by nobody: it is checked against the signed country packs (DEV-128), and passes only when a signed, unexpired pack for the country switches on every capability of the release. Evidence and every change of decision are append-only (the database refuses UPDATE and DELETE). The decision is recomputed on read; a button records it (a decision row is added only when the decision or its blockers change).
- **Independence rules (proposed):** nobody signs a release they made; one person signs at most one requirement of a release; clinical requirements (clinical partner, clinical safety) need a safety officer (R12), the rest a support officer (R11), standing in for the security lead, privacy lead, SRE, legal and the operating entity until those roles exist. Any support or safety officer may veto a requirement; a veto cannot be undone and the answer is a new release. There are no waivers (the specification allows narrow ones with compensating controls; they are not modelled).
- **Not built, stated plainly:** there is no pilot or live state and nothing deploys, switches anything on, or stops a deployment: "authorised" is a recorded decision, not a licence and not an enforcement. No cohort control, rollback, canary or halt on a safety signal. Attestations are references to artefacts held elsewhere; nothing here checks a signature, a build digest, the requirements matrix (GHE-19) or a recovery drill. No dossier export, no immutable vault, no real approver roles or credentials.
- **Checked:** 41 new tests in `test_readiness.py` (tuple rules and the fingerprint, who may create, the right officer per requirement, author and one-person-one-requirement rules, artefact and expiry rules, G3 then G4 then pack, pack must cover every capability, other-country, unsigned and suspended packs ignored, vetoes standing, expiry and recording the decision, evidence for another fingerprint ignored, one release's signatures never counting for another, history only on change, append-only evidence and decisions, audit without free text) plus 93 neighbouring pack, grant and policy tests, 134 passed; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the platform app type-checks and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on it twice.

### DEV-138: a requirements matrix that records readings and will not call something met without a test (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-19 calls for a traceability matrix from requirements to the tests and evidence that cover them. New module `modules/traceability` and a platform page "Requirements matrix". Each requirement has its specification, the specification's own id, a title, a standing (met, partly, not built) and evidence text. One rule keeps it honest: a requirement cannot be marked **met** unless its evidence names an automated test file (`test_...py`); partly and not built need only a reason. A support officer (R11) can add, import or update requirements (an import adds and updates, never deletes, and is all-or-nothing); platform staff (R11, R12) can read. A change is audited. The page shows, per specification, how many requirements are met, partly and not built, and lists them with filters. The 124 readings I wrote for the workflow engine (GHE-12, 60) and the external APIs (GHE-13, 64) are loaded from `modules/traceability/seed/` with `python manage.py load_traceability` or the page's button (safe to repeat).
- **Not built, stated plainly:** the matrix does not run any test or read any test result: "met" means someone named a test, not that it passed today. GHE-04, 06, 08, 12, 13, 16 and 18 are loaded (DEV-141, DEV-142); the other specifications' requirements are not extracted. The seed data and `docs/WORKFLOW-TRACE.md` and `docs/API-TRACE.md` come from the same generator and must be regenerated together. Nothing links the matrix to the go-live register's "requirements covered by tests" requirement (DEV-137): that stays a signed attestation. There is no hazard-to-requirement link and no per-country view.
- **Checked:** 22 new tests in `test_traceability.py` (met needs a named test, malformed rows, all-or-nothing batches, importing again, updates and audit only on a change, nothing deleted, who may write and read, summary and filters, the 124 recorded readings load and obey the same rule, the command is safe to repeat); ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the platform app type-checks and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on it twice.

### DEV-139: a clinic's fees, invoices and credit notes, as records only (TEMPORARY, built on a proposal)

- **What was done:** GHE-17 describes a commercial layer and says plainly to buy funds, card and payout infrastructure from licensed partners and to build only the orchestration, financial evidence and reconciliation. New module `modules/billing`, a staff "Billing" page and a patient "Bills & Payments" page cover the small safe core of it. **Prices:** a clinic administrator (R13) sets the price of each service from a date (today or up to a year ahead, whole minor units, a three-letter currency); a new price is a new entry, so an invoice keeps the price it was made with. **Invoices:** staff (R04, R13) make an invoice for a patient of the clinic with one to ten lines, each a service of the clinic and a quantity up to 20; prices come only from the schedule (nobody types a price), there is no free-text line, so no clinical content can reach a bill, and an invoice has one currency. It starts as a draft, is issued, then marked paid with the reference of a payment the clinic took elsewhere, or cancelled. Only the administrator cancels (with a reason from a fixed list) and only an invoice with no credit note can be cancelled. **Credit notes:** only the administrator writes them, on an issued or paid invoice, with a reason from a fixed list, and never beyond what is left; a fully credited invoice cannot be marked paid. Numbers are per customer (INV-000001, CN-000001). Every step is one chained, append-only event and credit notes cannot be changed or removed (the database refuses UPDATE and DELETE). A patient sees their own issued, paid and cancelled invoices and never drafts; a guardian sees none (billing is a "records" class no guardian has been granted).
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the roles (staff make and take payment, the administrator sets prices, cancels and credits), the fixed reason lists, the limits above, the 14-day default due period, and that "paid" is a clinic's own statement.
- **Not built, stated plainly:** no online payment of any kind, no card or bank detail, no payment provider, no receipts from one, no refunds executed (a credit note only records the reduction), no tax or VAT, no invoice PDF or number format per country, no insurer, payer or employer billing, no claims or remittance, no entitlements, no marketplace payouts, no disputes, no three-way reconciliation, no finance approval matrix, and nothing from the country pack. Money amounts are not in the audit trail; the amounts live on the invoice.
- **Checked:** 50 new tests in `test_billing.py` (who may set a price and how a price is validated, a future price shown as upcoming and not used, numbering, prices only from the schedule and no typed price, line and quantity rules, one currency, only staff and only for the clinic's own patients, a price change never touching an invoice already made, the draft to issued to paid path with harmless repeats, payment reference rules, cancelling by reason and by the administrator only, credit notes never below zero and not on a draft or cancelled invoice, a patient seeing only their own non-draft bills, separation between customers, the ledger chain, append-only events and credit notes, audit without amounts or references) plus the neighbouring policy and grant tests; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; both apps type-check. **A bug found by these tests and fixed:** the staff screens looked patient names up (an access check) inside an open transaction, which the access check refuses; the lookups were moved out. Browser robot 16 passes (an administrator sets a price, staff make, issue and mark an invoice as paid, and the patient sees it as paid in their own app), and the every-page and accessibility robots pass on the staff and patient apps.

### DEV-140: an overdue data request now shows up as late work in the clinic's queue (FIX, closes a gap noted in DEV-133)

- **What was done:** DEV-133 stamped a due date on each data request but said only a label shows when it passes, because data requests were not in the staff work queue. They are now. Filing a data request, withdrawing it and closing it (completed or declined) each announce themselves with an event that carries only a reference to the request (`rights.requested.v1`, `rights.withdrawn.v1`, `rights.closed.v1`, written in the same transaction as the change). The work queue listens: a new request opens one "Answer a data request" item for the clinic, due on the date the request was given (from the signed country pack for the customer's country, DEV-133) or, when no pack gave one, on the clinic's own response target; withdrawing cancels the item and closing finishes it; starting the request or checking identity leaves it open. Once its date passes the queue marks it late, like the other kinds of work. The queue's ten-minute sync job also opens an item for any open data request that has none (an event lost, or a request that existed before this), so the queue cannot disagree with the requests. The item carries no word of the patient's request. The screen's "Open data requests" button goes to the Data requests page.
- **Also (same entry):** data requests past their date are now counted in the dashboard's "late" numbers and named, as a count only, in the daily email to verified clinic operations staff ("1 data request past the date to answer"). A request with no pack date is late after the clinic's own response target. 5 more tests cover this (the dashboard count, the no-date rule, ended or not-yet-due requests not counted, and the email holding a count only).
- **Not built:** no escalation, reminder to the patient or regulator notice when a request goes late; the email and the queue label are the only consequences. What a late request should trigger is a question for your lead (lead note, question 9).
- **Checked:** 12 new tests in `test_work_data_requests.py` (one item per request even if delivered twice, due on the clinic's own target without a pack and on the pack's date with one, the event carrying a reference only and the item none of the patient's words, withdrawing cancels, completing and declining finish, starting and checking identity leave it open, late once its date has passed, the sync job opens a missing item and ignores ended requests, another customer's staff and patients see none) plus the neighbouring work-queue, digest, attention and data-request tests, 54 passed for the last group and 127 for the first; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and the page-load, accessibility and data-request robots pass.

### DEV-141: the country-pack and cloud-operations work is checked against the specifications' acceptance cases (FIX)

- **What was done:** as for the workflow engine (DEV-135) and the external APIs (DEV-136), each acceptance case of GHE-04 (country packs, 36 cases) and GHE-18 (cloud foundation and operations, 80 cases) was read against what exists and recorded, in `docs/PACKS-TRACE.md` and `docs/OPERATIONS-TRACE.md`: **GHE-04: 5 met, 18 partly, 13 not built; GHE-18: 5 met, 18 partly, 57 not built.** Each row says why and which of our tests proves it. It is the developer's reading, not a run of those cases, and nobody else has reviewed it. All 116 readings are loaded into the requirements matrix (DEV-138), which now holds 240 (GHE-04 36, GHE-12 60, GHE-13 64, GHE-18 80) and still refuses to call anything "met" without naming a test file.
- **What the tables show:** packs are recorded, reviewed, approved on record, expiring, vetoable and applied to one setting, but there is no signed manifest, cell staging, preflight, run-time resolver or transfer control. The cloud-operations cases are mostly about infrastructure that does not exist (regional cells, infrastructure-as-code, a key service, backups and disaster recovery, a release pipeline, on-call and incident tooling), which needs a hosting decision and an operations team, not code.
- **Checked:** the matrix tests were updated for the larger set and pass (22); the seed data obeys the matrix's own rule; ruff is clean. No application code changed.

### DEV-142: the messages, identity and clinical-safety work is checked against the specifications' acceptance cases (FIX)

- **What was done:** as for DEV-135, DEV-136 and DEV-141, each acceptance case of GHE-16 (secure communications, 64), GHE-06 (identity, patient matching and access, 40) and GHE-08 (clinical safety, 54) was read against what exists and recorded in `docs/COMMS-TRACE.md`, `docs/IDENTITY-TRACE.md` and `docs/SAFETY-TRACE.md`: **GHE-16: 1 met, 28 partly, 35 not built; GHE-06: 7 met, 13 partly, 20 not built; GHE-08: 8 met, 19 partly, 27 not built.** Each row says why and which of our tests proves it; I checked the tests behind every "met" row exists and says what the row claims. It is the developer's reading, not a run of those cases, and nobody else has reviewed it. All 158 readings are loaded into the requirements matrix (DEV-138), which now holds 398 across seven specifications (GHE-04, 06, 08, 12, 13, 16, 18).
- **What the tables show:** identity and access is the best covered (guardian scopes, doctors tied to a site, staff reaching only their own site's patients, two-person suspension, role prohibitions); clinical safety is covered where it is about evidence rules (a delivery or a read receipt is never a clinical fact, a step cannot go back, AI cannot run without two-person approval, no one signs their own release, a veto stands). Communications is mostly not built because it needs real channels. Not built across all three: patient matching and merging, break-glass emergency access, single sign-in and licence checks, SMS, push and phone, message templates and approvals, languages and right-to-left, critical-result routing, result matching and correction handling, incident notices and postmarket follow-up. Those need an identity provider, a channel provider, a clinical owner and regulatory advice.
- **Checked:** the matrix tests were updated for the larger set and pass (22); the seed data obeys the matrix's rule (a "met" reading must name a test file); ruff is clean. No application code changed.

### DEV-143: an unresolved hazard holds a release back (FIX, from the clinical-safety comparison, GHE-08 AT42)

- **What was done:** DEV-142 found the hazard register and the go-live register (DEV-137) were not connected: a release could be "ready" while hazards were still open. The go-live register now also checks the hazard register itself, as a system-checked requirement beside the country pack (signed by nobody). It passes only when the register holds hazards and every one is accepted or retired; a hazard that is open, verified but not accepted, or rejected holds the release at "not ready", and an empty register is not a clean one. The requirement shows how many hazards are resolved ("12 of 36 resolved") and it cannot be signed or vetoed by hand. The platform page labels it.
- **Effect to know about:** a fresh install seeds 36 hazards (H01 to H20 and HAI-01 to HAI-16), all open, so no release can reach "ready" until each has been assessed, verified by a different person and accepted or retired. That is the intent (the specification: an unaccepted residual risk vetoes a live release) and is a deliberate burden, not a fault.
- **Not built:** this still records a decision and stops no real release; hazards are not linked to the build digest, controls or test runs; and which hazards must be resolved for which release or country is not modelled (all of them, always).
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `test_readiness.py` (an empty register holds a release back with everything signed; each of open, verified and rejected holds it back; accepted and retired let it through and resolving the last one releases it; the requirement cannot be signed or vetoed), the earlier readiness tests now run against a settled register, plus the hazard tests, 67 passed; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the platform app type-checks and its page-load and accessibility robots pass. The comparison row for GHE-08 AT42 was updated.

### DEV-144: staff must say what proof they saw before approving a guardian (FIX, from the identity comparison, GHE-06 AT-15)

- **What was done:** approving a guardian already needed a tick saying staff had checked proof of identity and authority, but nothing recorded what that proof was. Approval now needs a kind of proof from a fixed list (birth record, adoption record, court order, power of attorney, carer registration, the patient's written consent, another document) and a short reference to where the clinic keeps it (4 to 64 plain characters, never the document). Both are stored on the grant and shown to staff on the approved link ("Proof seen: Court order (file-1042)"). The audit trail records the approval; the guardian sees the kind of proof but not the clinic's file reference. A rejected request records none. The staff screen asks for both before the Approve button works.
- **Proposed, not in the spec:** the list of kinds, and that any kind may be used with any relationship: matching a kind of proof to a relationship (for example a court order for a legal guardian) would be a legal rule that nobody here has set.
- **Not built:** nothing verifies the document, its date or its issuer; the reference is the clinic's word; and age thresholds for minors under a market rule (GHE-06 AT-16) are still not modelled.
- **Checked:** 11 new tests in `test_family.py` (approval refused without a kind or a reference, or with a made-up kind or a malformed reference, and nothing approved; the proof stored and visible to staff; the guardian sees the kind and not the file reference; the audit trail does not hold the reference; a rejected request records none) plus the neighbouring guardian-scope, grant-state and attention tests, 65 passed; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and its page-load and accessibility robots pass. The comparison row for GHE-06 AT-15 was updated.

### DEV-145: starting a workflow is idempotent, and a webhook that gave up is counted on the dashboard (FIX, from the acceptance-case comparisons)

- **What was done:** two small gaps found by the comparisons (DEV-135, DEV-136, DEV-142). **Starting a workflow** takes an optional idempotency key (8 to 64 plain characters). Asking again with the same key and the same request gives the first workflow and the same answer (marked `replayed`, status 200 instead of 201) and creates nothing; the same key with a different request (another subject or owner) is a conflict and creates nothing; without a key the old rule stands (one live workflow per subject). A fingerprint of the request is stored with the key. **Webhook deliveries that gave up:** the administrator's staff dashboard now carries a number for them and links to the Integrations page (a number for clinic administrators only; others see 0), so a partner that stopped receiving cannot go unnoticed.
- **Effect on the comparisons:** GHE-12 cases WF-T13 and WF-T14 move from partly to **met**, so the workflow engine stands at **20 met, 16 partly, 24 not built** (of 60). GHE-13 T-23 and GHE-16 TC43 stay partly with updated notes (no owner, policy or evidence is recorded on a given-up delivery, and no threshold raises an alert). The requirements matrix was reloaded.
- **Not built:** a staff screen that starts a workflow (the API does it); an alert when given-up deliveries pass a threshold.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_orchestration.py` (same key twice gives one workflow and the same answer; the same key with a different request is a conflict; a malformed key is refused and without a key the old rule holds; two keys for one subject cannot make two workflows; one customer's key does not collide with another's) and 1 in `test_webhooks.py` (the dashboard number, administrators only, back to zero after a delivery is sent again), plus the attention tests updated for the new number, 148 passed across the three files; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and its page-load and accessibility robots pass.

### DEV-146: a webhook delivery that gave up keeps its owner, the rules in force and a fingerprint of the message (FIX, GHE-13 T-23)

- **What was done:** the comparison (DEV-136) found that a delivery in the "gave up" list recorded neither who answers for it nor which access rules applied, which the specification asks for. Each delivery now stores, when it is queued: its owner (the person who registered the address), the fingerprint of the access rules in force (the policy digest) and a SHA-256 fingerprint of the exact message. They stay unchanged when a delivery gives up and when it is sent again. The Integrations page shows the owner's name and the start of the message fingerprint; the administrator's dashboard already counts given-up deliveries (DEV-145).
- **Effect on the comparison:** GHE-13 T-23 moves from partly to **met**; the external-API work stands at **10 met, 18 partly, 36 not built** (of 64).
- **Not built:** the full attempt-by-attempt history of a delivery (only the number of tries, the last answer and its error class are kept) and an alert when too many have given up.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_webhooks.py` (a delivery carries its owner, the policy digest and the right message fingerprint; all three are unchanged after it gives up and after it is sent again), plus the other webhook, attention and grant tests, 76 passed before the last fix and the two new tests rerun green; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean; the staff app type-checks and its page-load and accessibility robots pass. The requirements matrix was reloaded.

### DEV-147: an exception on a workflow cannot be cleared without evidence (FIX, GHE-12 WF-T54)

- **What was done:** the workflow comparison (DEV-135) found that clearing an "exception" or "reconciling" health state needed only a written reason, so an operator could dismiss a doubt (for example a patient-match question) on their own word. Clearing either now also needs evidence of the kind `exception_resolution` (a short reference to what resolved it); a missing, empty or wrong-kind evidence is refused and nothing changes. The evidence is stored on the history step. Clearing "at risk" (which a timer sets) and raising any health state need no evidence, as before.
- **Effect on the comparison:** WF-T54 moves from partly to **met**; the workflow engine stands at **21 met, 15 partly, 24 not built** (of 60).
- **Not built:** nothing checks that the referenced resolution is real or that someone other than the operator produced it; which kinds of exception need which evidence is not modelled.
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `test_orchestration.py` (an exception and a reconciliation each refuse a clear on a reason alone, on the wrong kind and on empty evidence, and keep their state; with the right evidence it clears and the history shows what was given; "at risk" needs no evidence either way, nor does raising an exception) and one existing test updated to give evidence, all 108 tests in the file pass; ruff, mypy and the import contracts are clean. The requirements matrix was reloaded.

### DEV-148: sending a message twice posts it once; narrow screens reflow (FIX, GHE-16 TC17/TC18/TC33)

- **What was done:** starting a conversation and replying now accept an optional `Idempotency-Key`; the same key with the same words returns the same thread (200 instead of 201), the same key with different words is refused (409), a malformed key is refused (422), and no key behaves as before. The patient app sends one key per message and keeps it across a retry. Separately, a 320 pixel reflow pass: the patient menu and layout, topbars, tables (now keyboard-focusable scroll regions with a label), selects and checkboxes (at least 24 pixels) were fixed in all four apps.
- **Checked:** `test_message_idempotency.py` (9 tests) and browser robot 17, which opens every page of the four apps at 320 pixels and checks for sideways scrolling and the automated accessibility rules; a person on a real phone or magnified screen must still try it.
- **Not built:** keys are not shared across devices; the robot finds mechanical failures only.

### DEV-149: an unsupported API version gets a safe, guiding answer (GHE-13 T-05)

- **What was done:** any request under `/api/<version>/` that no route answers now returns the standard not-found body; for an unknown major version it also carries `X-Supported-Api-Versions: v1` and a `successor-version` link to `/api/v1/`. Nothing is echoed back, nothing redirects, and a known version with an unknown path stays an ordinary not-found.
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-05 moves from not built to **partly**; external API now 11 met, 18 partly, 35 not built (of 64).
- **Not built:** deprecation and sunset headers and a published version policy (only v1 exists).
- **Checked:** `test_api_versions.py` (5 tests).

### DEV-150: an OpenAPI 3.1 description of the live API, with a drift check (GHE-13 T-01, T-04, T-08)

- **What was done:** `api/openapi.py` builds the document from the real routes (so it cannot list one that does not exist) and `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` serves it without a login. `endpoint` now records its methods for this. A checked-in snapshot (`api/openapi.snapshot.json`) and a test fail when the API changes without the snapshot being refreshed (`ZOIKO_WRITE_OPENAPI=1`).
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-01 and T-04 move from not built to **partly**, T-08 to **met**; external API now 12 met, 20 partly, 32 not built (of 64).
- **Not built:** field-by-field request and response schemas (the views read plain JSON), examples, an external OpenAPI validator, telling a breaking change from an additive one, blocking a release.
- **Checked:** `test_openapi.py` (7 tests), ruff and mypy.

### DEV-151: a catalogue of published events and an AsyncAPI document (GHE-13 T-02)

- **What was done:** `foundation/event_catalog.py` lists the 18 events the product publishes (plus the verification ping sent straight to a partner), what each is about and which reference fields it carries, with a schema hash each. `GET /api/v1/asyncapi.json` serves the AsyncAPI 3.0 document built from it, with no login. A test reads the source and fails if code publishes an event that is not listed or the list names one nobody publishes; a checked-in snapshot shows any change (refresh with `ZOIKO_WRITE_OPENAPI=1`, which now refreshes both documents).
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-02 moves from not built to **partly**; external API now 12 met, 21 partly, 31 not built (of 64).
- **Not built:** an approved topic review, an external AsyncAPI validator, field types beyond "string", examples, and a check of the real payload against the listed fields at publish time.
- **Checked:** `test_event_catalog.py` (6 tests), ruff and mypy.

### DEV-152: a published event is checked against its catalogue entry (GHE-13 T-02, T-04 groundwork)

- **What was done:** `emit()` now refuses (OutboxError) an event whose subject, required fields or extra fields differ from its entry in `foundation/event_catalog.py`, so the AsyncAPI document cannot disagree with what is really sent. An event type the catalogue does not know is still allowed here; the source scan in `test_event_catalog.py` finds those.
- **Effect on the comparison:** none on the counts; it closes the "payload not checked at publish time" gap listed in DEV-151.
- **Checked:** the 15 suites that publish events (291 tests, run with `--ds=config.settings.test`) all pass, plus 2 new tests of the check itself.

### DEV-153: each workflow step publishes an event in its own transaction (GHE-12 WF-T43)

- **What was done:** every step of the workflow engine (move, overlay, owner, review, handoff, timers) now emits `workflow.changed.v1` through the outbox inside the same transaction, carrying only the instance reference, the kind of step, the state and the overlay, ordered per instance. The event is in the catalogue (DEV-151) and in the AsyncAPI snapshot.
- **Effect on the comparison:** WF-T43 moves from not built to **met**; the workflow engine stands at **23 met, 14 partly, 23 not built** (of 60).
- **Not built:** nothing consumes the event yet; partners cannot subscribe to it (it is not in a webhook family).
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_orchestration.py` (one event per step with references only; a refused step leaves none), the whole of `test_orchestration.py` (117 with the catalogue tests) passes, ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-154: a workflow whose owner has left is flagged, not silently stuck (GHE-12 WF-T42)

- **What was done:** `trust.inactive_actors()` lists a customer's suspended or ended logins. The workflow list marks a live workflow whose owner is one of them (`owner_inactive`), the exception workbench includes it, and the staff dashboard counts it (`workflows_without_owner`, with a line on the dashboard and a note on the workflow card). An administrator reassigns it with the existing reassign step.
- **Effect on the comparison:** WF-T42 moves from not built to **partly**; the workflow engine stands at **23 met, 15 partly, 22 not built** (of 60).
- **Not built:** automatic reassignment, an on-call rota, and a safety-relevant flag on a workflow (the specification names a designated on-call owner).
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_orchestration.py` (flag, workbench, dashboard count, reassign clears it), the two whole-dashboard comparisons in `test_attention.py` updated for the new number, both files pass, ruff, mypy and the operations type-check clean.

### DEV-155: the patient inbox announces new messages to a screen reader (GHE-16 TC33, TC34)

- **What was done:** the patient Messages page has a visually hidden polite live region ("You have 2 new messages." / "No new messages.") that follows the unread count, and its error line is now an alert. Browser robot 18 checks the region; robot 17 (DEV-148) already covers the inbox at 320 pixels and the keyboard menu.
- **Effect on the comparison:** TC33 stays **partly** (its proof is a browser robot and the matrix accepts Met only with a named backend test), its evidence now says robot 17 covers the inbox at 320 pixels; TC34 moves from not built to **partly**; secure communications now 3 met, 27 partly, 34 not built (of 64).
- **Not built:** a push or other notification channel, a real screen-reader test, and the same announcement in the staff and doctor inboxes.
- **Checked:** robot 18 passes; the patient type-check is clean.

### DEV-156: a workflow definition can require country-pack capabilities (TEMPORARY, GHE-12 WF-T01, WF-T03)

- **What was done:** a definition has `required_capabilities` (known country-pack capability names, each once; part of its fingerprint only when set; carried into a new version; changeable while a draft). Starting a workflow on such a definition needs every one of them switched on by a signed, unexpired, unsuspended pack for the customer's country (`jurisdiction.enabled_capabilities`, read in the platform tenant outside the customer's transaction, like the data-request deadline); otherwise the start is refused with 403 JURISDICTION_INACTIVE and no workflow is made. No answer means no. Running workflows are not stopped. A country in the request body is ignored. Migration `orchestration/0003_definition_capabilities`.
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification says a definition is bound to a country pack, but not how. This proposal (a list of required capabilities, a union over the signed packs of the country) is ours, for the lead to confirm: whether several packs add up or the strictest wins, and whether a running workflow should be paused when its pack is suspended (we leave it running).
- **Effect on the comparison:** WF-T01 and WF-T03 move to **met**; the workflow engine stands at **25 met, 15 partly, 20 not built** (of 60).
- **Not built:** pausing running workflows when a pack is suspended, a per-definition pack revision pin, and gating the other engine steps (only the start is gated).
- **Checked:** `test_workflow_packs.py` (17 tests), ruff, mypy, import-linter and `makemigrations --check` clean; the neighbouring suites (orchestration, OpenAPI, catalogue, packs, readiness) are rerun once.

### DEV-157: the staff inbox announces waiting conversations to a screen reader (GHE-16 TC34)

- **What was done:** the staff Messages page has the same hidden polite live region as the patient inbox (DEV-155) ("2 conversations with new messages." / "No conversations waiting."), and its error line is an alert. Robot 18 now checks both inboxes.
- **Effect on the comparison:** none on the counts (TC34 was already partly); it closes the "not in the staff inbox" gap named in DEV-155. The doctor app has no inbox yet.
- **Checked:** robot 18 (2 tests) passes; the operations type-check is clean.

### DEV-158: a partner app for the lab, pharmacy, home-care, payer and employer roles (TEMPORARY)

- **What was done:** the specification names 13 roles; R05, R06, R07, R08 and R09 had rules in the policy table but nowhere to sign in. A new app, `frontend/partners` (port 5177), serves them: sign-in (anyone whose account belongs elsewhere is pointed to their own app and given no session), an **Overview** that names the role, lists what it may reach (read from the same rules table the server enforces, `policy.role_capabilities`, never a second copy), what it never does (the specification's prohibitions), what is connected today and what waits for a decision; and, for R05, R06 and R09, **My services** (add a service, ask for a listing). R07 (payer) and R08 (employer) get the overview only. Behind it: `GET /api/v1/portal/overview`; Zoiko support can now onboard a customer of each kind (`kind`: provider, lab, pharmacy, homecare, payer, employer, developer), whose first user holds the one role of that kind and gets an invitation that opens the right app (`PARTNER_APP_URL`); the platform app's add-customer form has the choice; `scripts/seed_partners.py` makes one test customer per role. R10 (partner developer) already signs in to the staff console, where Integrations is the developer console: onboarding as `developer` gives that role.
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification names the portals but not how a partner, payer or employer is admitted, or who reviews a partner's service listing. We let R05, R06 and R09 ask for a listing; reviewing and publishing still need an organisation administrator, and a partner organisation has none, so its listing waits (the screen says so). The lead decides who reviews (Zoiko support, a second partner user, or an administrator role for partners). Update: DEV-159 lets a colleague of the same kind review it.
- **Not built:** anything that needs data that has no model yet: referrals, orders and results for labs and pharmacies (clinical record and exchange services), entitlement checks and claims for payers, programme counts for employers (no cohort or enrolment model; whatever is built must show counts only, GHE-17), team invitations for partner organisations, and the wider partner workspace in GHE-15.
- **Corrected earlier statement:** R10 can sign in (to the staff console); the earlier answer that it cannot was wrong. Seven roles had a place to sign in before this; now twelve do (R01 to R13), with R10 inside the staff console.
- **Checked:** `test_partner_portal.py` (25 tests: onboarding each kind, invitation to the right app and first sign-in, the overview from the real rules, partner services, payer and employer refused), `test_listing_states.py` still passes, ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; browser robot 19 (8 tests) and robot 17 now covers the partner app at 320 pixels; all five apps type-check.

### DEV-159: partner teams, employer programme counts, payer plans and cover checks (TEMPORARY)

- **What was done:** three of the four gaps left by DEV-158 are now real, on temporary proposals.
  - **Team.** A partner, payer or employer user adds colleagues of their own kind to their own organisation, suspends and reinstates them (never themselves) and sends an invitation again (`/portal/team`; `trust.invite_colleague`). The five roles now hold the `staff` right on their own organisation. A colleague of the same kind can review and publish a partner's service listing, never their own request, which also settles the open "who reviews a partner's listing" question for now.
  - **Employer programme.** A new module `modules/sponsor` holds the employer's enrolled people by an opaque reference (3 to 40 plain characters, never a name), added or withdrawn in batches of up to 200. The employer only ever reads counts, and a count of 1 up to one less than `SPONSOR_MIN_GROUP` (default 10) is hidden, so a small group cannot point at a person; zero is shown as zero. There is no endpoint that returns the references. A new policy resource class `program` is held only by the employer role; the specification's prohibitions on person, booking, referral and the rest still stand.
  - **Payer.** The same module holds plans (code, name, covered service categories, the patient's share) and members (the payer's own member number, shown shortened, with optional dates). A cover check answers covered, with the patient's share, or exactly why not: no such member, membership ended, outside the dates, plan switched off, or the kind of service is not in the plan. Each check is audited against the member record, without the member number.
  - **Screens** in the partner app: My team (everyone), Programme (employer), Plans and members with a Check cover form (payer). The overview now lists what is connected and says plainly what still is not. Migration `sponsor/0001_initial` (row-level security on all three tables).
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification names these portals but not their data or rules. The reference format, the minimum group size of 10, the plan shape (categories plus a share) and the check outcomes are ours, for the lead and for payer and employer owners to confirm. Counts are about enrolment only.
- **Not built, and why:** (1) **Labs and pharmacies: referrals, orders and results.** These must pass between two separate customers (a clinic and a lab) and no exchange between customers exists; the clinical record and partner-exchange services (GHE-07, GHE-22) are the specification's answer and need the lead's design before anything is built. (2) **Payer claims and a clinic asking the payer.** Same missing exchange. (3) **Employer counts about use of care** (visits, bookings): the same missing link between an employer's programme and clinics, and it must stay counts only.
- **Checked:** `test_sponsor.py` (41 tests: team, counts and suppression, plan and member rules, every check outcome, audit, isolation between customers), `test_partner_portal.py` updated, ruff, mypy, import-linter, `makemigrations --check`; browser robot 19 now has 11 tests, and the partner app type-checks. The dev database needed `manage.py migrate` and a backend restart.

### DEV-160: a running workflow stops when the pack that allowed it lapses (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-62)

- **What was done:** DEV-156 gated the start of a workflow on country-pack capabilities. The same check now applies to every forward step: if a signed, current pack no longer switches the definition's capabilities on (suspended, expired, withdrawn, or the customer's country changed), the step is refused with 403 JURISDICTION_INACTIVE and nothing moves. Cancelling and blocking stay possible, so a person can always close the work down safely, and a workflow that needs no pack is never touched. Overlay changes, reassignment, escalations and reading it are unaffected.
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification says suspension "stops new operations and invokes configured human fallback" but not which steps count as new or what the fallback is. Ours: forward steps stop, cancel and block remain, and the fallback is the existing cancel, block and escalation steps; nothing is reassigned or paused automatically.
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-62 moves from not built to **partly**; external API now 12 met, 22 partly, 30 not built (of 64).
- **Not built:** automatically assigning stopped work to a named person, and marking a stopped workflow as stopped in the list (a person finds out when a step is refused).
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_workflow_packs.py` (suspended pack stops a step and nothing moves; cancel still works; a workflow with no pack need is untouched), the whole file (20 tests) passes, ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-161: a home screen with real numbers for every role, and a real developer console for R10 (TEMPORARY)

- **What the check found:** going role by role through what each login sees first: the clinic roles (R01 to R04, R13), the platform roles (R11, R12) and the doctor already had working home screens. The partner-app roles (R05 to R09) only had a description of their role, with no numbers. R10 was worse: it signed in to the clinic staff console, where the dashboard and every integration page refused it (403), because the rules gave it only a `partner` right and the integration screens asked for `integration` and for the administrator role. DEV-158 said the Integrations page was R10's developer console; in practice it was not usable. That statement was wrong.
- **What was done:**
  - **Numbers.** `GET /portal/dashboard` returns numbers only for each kind of login, shown as cards at the top of the partner app's Overview: partners (services listed, waiting for a review and how many are for you to review, not yet listed, team), payers (plans on, members enrolled and ended), employers (people enrolled and withdrawn, small groups hidden), developers (API keys, webhooks, deliveries that gave up, delivered and retrying), and the team size for all.
  - **Developer console.** R10 now has its own area, `developer` (R10 is no longer in the clinic-console role set; a person who also holds R13 keeps the clinic console). R10 holds the `integration` and `staff` rights on its own organisation and may manage its own keys and webhooks (`MANAGING_ROLES` in the integration module); the integration endpoints accept the developer area. The partner app has API keys (add, suspend, resume, rotate the secret, revoke; the secret is shown once), Webhooks (add, verify, pause, resume, rotate, revoke, and the delivery log), API documents (links to the OpenAPI and AsyncAPI documents), My team, and the Overview. Onboarding as `developer` and the invitation now lead to the partner app.
  - **Fixes found on the way.** The team list and count are now limited to the organisation (they listed every login in the customer); partner event types must start with `partner.`, which the key form now says.
- **TEMPORARY:** what a partner developer may manage on its own, and the choice to give R10 the same key and webhook powers as an administrator, are ours for the lead to confirm.
- **Not built:** a sandbox with synthetic patients, partner certification, and onboarding checks for partners (GHE-13), so R10's console is a working key and webhook console, not the full certification journey.
- **Checked:** `test_partner_portal.py` 32 tests (dashboards for each kind, R10's area and rights, closed to other kinds), the suites that mention R10 or integrations (platform, team, suspensions, releases, incidents, customer sign-up, webhooks, partner events: 282 tests) all pass, ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; browser robot 19 has 20 tests (real numbers for each of the six kinds, the developer's key and secret, documents, webhooks) and robot 17 still passes for the partner app. The test developer login is created by `scripts/seed_partners.py`.

### DEV-162: a doctor reads and answers messages from their own patients (TEMPORARY, GHE-16)

- **What was done:** the doctor app's Messages screen was a "soon" placeholder. Now a doctor sees the conversations of patients they have an active care relationship with, in their own clinic only (`GET /clinician/messages`; each patient is checked by the policy engine, and a colleague with no relationship sees nothing), opens a thread and replies, with an Idempotency-Key so a double tap posts once and a polite live region that tells a screen reader how many conversations have new messages. R03 now counts as the clinic's side of a conversation (`messaging.STAFF_ROLES`), so a patient sees a doctor's reply as the clinic answering; the existing thread and reply endpoints serve doctors with the same per-patient check as before. A doctor cannot reply while following up read only (continuity of care), cannot open or answer a patient they do not care for, and cannot close a conversation (staff only).
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification gives clinicians secure messaging with patients but not whether a doctor answers as themselves or as the clinic, or how unread counts are shared. Ours: a doctor's reply is the clinic's side of the thread (the patient is shown "the clinic"), it clears the clinic's shared unread count like any staff reply, and a doctor's own "new" badge clears when they open the thread. Notes, orders and results stay not built.
- **Found on the way:** the first version listed a doctor's conversations through the patient helper (own record plus guardian grants), so a doctor saw none; fixed by using care relationships (`trust.care_patients`).
- **Also fixed (full-suite run):** 15 tests failed in the first full run, 14 because they sent real event names such as `booking.confirmed.v1` with made-up payloads that the publish-time catalogue check (DEV-152) rightly refuses (`test_dispatch`, `test_notifications`, `test_work_history`: now valid payloads) and 2 (`test_late`) because the "late" numbers gained a data-request count earlier today; the OpenAPI snapshot was refreshed for the new route. The product code needed no change for these.
- **Checked:** `test_doctor_messages.py` (10 tests), the messaging suites, OpenAPI and catalogue tests; the full backend suite before this change: 2,175 passed, 16 failed (explained above), now fixed; ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; browser robot 20 (a patient writes, the doctor reads and answers, the patient sees the clinic's reply) and robot 17 for the doctor app; the doctor app type-checks.

### DEV-163: a status exchange between a clinic and its partners, references only (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-57)

- **What was done:** customers are kept strictly apart, so a clinic could not send anything to a lab, pharmacy or home-care partner. A new module `modules/exchange` holds the one place they meet, in Zoiko's own platform space: one row per request with who it is from and to, the kind (laboratory or imaging order, pharmacy request, home-care referral), an opaque order number the clinic chooses, a short plain-words description, the urgency and its state. No patient name, no clinical text and no result is stored: a result stays with the partner and only its reference and the fact that it is ready travel. The clinic keeps its own patient link on the row for its own checks and it is never shown to the partner (the partner's view has no patient field). Each side reads only the rows that name it; anything else is "not found".
  - **Steps:** requested, accepted or declined (with a reason), in progress, result available (the partner gives its own reference), then reviewed by a clinician. The clinic may cancel until the result arrives. Repeating a step is harmless; skipping ahead is a conflict. **Delivery is never review:** only a doctor with a care relationship to that patient can mark a result reviewed (clinic staff and administrators cannot, the policy check is `attest:referral` for that patient). Only the partner role that handles the kind (R05 laboratory, R06 pharmacy, R09 home care) may answer it; a payer, an employer or a developer in the same customer may not.
  - **Screens:** the staff console has Partner requests (send, track, cancel); the partner app has Incoming requests (accept, decline with a reason, start, mark the result available) and a New requests number on its home; the doctor app has Partner results (a doctor reviews a ready result for their own patients). Each step is audited in the caller's own space and in the platform's.
  - **Also:** `GET /exchange/partners`, `/exchange/requests`, `/exchange/requests/<id>/<step>`, `/exchange/requests/<id>/history`, `/exchange/incoming` and its steps. Migration `exchange/0001_initial` (row-level security).
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification names partner exchange (GHE-07, GHE-22) but gives no design. This proposal (a broker holding references and status in the platform space, filtered by the caller's own customer) is ours. Your lead decides whether Zoiko may hold even this much about a clinic's requests, how a lab learns who a patient is, and what the patient agrees to share (consent, GHE-05). Nothing here lets a partner learn who a patient is.
- **Not built:** passing patient identity or any clinical content (needed for a lab to do real work), the patient's consent to share, a patient-facing status, payer claims and employer usage counts (they need the same exchange with their own rules), partner certification, and notifications when a step happens.
- **Checked:** `test_exchange.py` (28 tests: who may send, validation, each side sees only its own rows, the partner never gets the patient reference, nothing clinical is stored, ordered steps, role matches kind, only a doctor reviews, history, home numbers), ruff, mypy, import-linter, `makemigrations --check`; browser robot 21 (the whole journey through the staff console, partner app and doctor app) and robot 17 for the three apps with new pages; the three apps type-check. The dev database needed `manage.py migrate`.

### DEV-164: the patient sees where a request made for them stands (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-57)

- **What was done:** DEV-163 let the clinic, a doctor and the partner see a request's status; the patient could not. The patient app has **My Tests & Requests** (`GET /exchange/mine`): for the patient, or a guardian for the person they act for, what was asked, of whom and how far it has got (sent, accepted, in progress, result ready, reviewed by your doctor, not possible, cancelled). The words never overstate: a ready result is "ready, your doctor has not reviewed it yet, so please do not take it as medical advice", and only a doctor's review changes it to "reviewed". A patient is shown no order number, no partner reference and no reason text, and no result; staff, doctors and partners calling it get nothing back. Each request is shown only when the role table lets that patient read that kind of thing (laboratory orders as diagnostics, pharmacy requests as medication, home care as referral).
- **TEMPORARY:** what a patient may be told, and the wording, are ours for the lead and clinical safety to confirm (including whether a declined request should give any reason).
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-57 stays **partly** (the clinical hand-off acknowledgement is not built); its evidence now includes the patient-facing status.
- **Not built:** a notification to the patient when a request moves, showing the result itself, and the patient's consent to share.
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (the patient sees progress, never an order number, reference or reason, nobody else sees it, another clinic's patient sees nothing); the whole exchange file passes; ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; browser robot 21 now includes the patient seeing "Result ready" then "Reviewed by your doctor"; the patient app type-checks and passes the 320 pixel check.

### DEV-165: the clinic's dashboard counts partner results waiting for a doctor (TEMPORARY)

- **What was done:** a result a partner has marked available, and no doctor has reviewed yet, used to sit unnoticed until someone opened Partner requests. The staff dashboard now has an attention line, "N results from a partner waiting for a doctor to review" (`partner_results`), that links to Partner requests and goes back to zero when a doctor reviews. It reads the count from the exchange outside the clinic's own transaction and shows zero when there is no platform space.
- **TEMPORARY:** that clinic staff (not only doctors) see the count, and that it counts every waiting result in the clinic rather than a doctor's own patients, are our choices for the lead.
- **Not built:** the same number in the doctor app's menu or an email or push notice.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_exchange.py` (0, still 0 until delivered, 1 when ready, 0 after review), the four whole-dashboard expectations in `test_attention.py` updated for the new number, both files pass; ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; the staff console type-checks; browser robot 21 still passes.

### DEV-166: the patient is told when a doctor has reviewed a result (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-57, GHE-16)

- **What was done:** a patient used to find out only by opening My Tests & Requests. When a doctor marks a partner's result as reviewed, the clinic's own space now publishes `exchange.reviewed.v1` (request, patient and kind references only, now in the event catalogue and the AsyncAPI snapshot) and the patient gets an in-app notification, "Your doctor reviewed a result", plus the usual email headline when they have turned email notices on ("Your doctor reviewed a result.", no detail). The wording comes from the event alone, so it says nothing about what the result was, and repeating the review never tells them twice. Nothing is announced when the review is refused, and a result merely being ready tells the patient nothing: delivery is not review.
- **TEMPORARY:** that only the review, not the result being ready, notifies the patient, and the wording, are ours for the lead and clinical safety to confirm.
- **Not built:** notifying the patient when a request is declined or cancelled, and a push or text notice.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (told once on review, not before, not on a refused review, no label or order number in the notice), the exchange, event-catalogue and notification suites pass together (52 tests, plus the refreshed snapshot); ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; browser robot 21 now ends with the patient seeing the notification.

### DEV-167: every login opens on its own home screen, with one-click links and doctor menu counts (TEMPORARY)

- **What was done:** a check across all logins found each kind lands on a working home screen. Robot 22 signs in as 12 logins (patient, doctor, receptionist, clinic administrator, regional support, safety officer, lab, pharmacy, home care, payer, employer, developer) and fails if a home screen is missing or shows an error; it keeps a picture of each in `frontend/e2e/screens/` (not committed). `docs/LOGIN-LINKS.md` lists one-click sign-in links for the same 12 (test accounts only; the apps already accept `dev_org`, `dev_login` and `dev_pw` in development and clean the address at once), each checked to work. A receptionist test login (R04, `e2e.reception@example.org`) was missing and is now made by `scripts/seed_e2e.py`. The doctor app's menu now shows how many conversations have new messages and how many partner results are ready for review beside Messages and Partner results.
- **Not covered:** R02 (guardian), which needs a guardian login a clinic has approved for a patient, so it has no ready-made link.
- **Checked:** robot 22 (12 logins) passes; all 12 links signed in; the doctor app type-checks.

### DEV-168: a contract check between the five apps and the API (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-03)

- **What was done:** `scripts/consumer_contract.py` reads the five front-end apps' own source and lists every API call each makes (method and route) in `backend/api/consumer_contract.json`. `test_consumer_contract.py` fails if the API stops serving any of them, passes when routes are only added, and fails if the contract file is out of date with the apps (skipped where the front-end source is not visible, such as inside the container).
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification asks for consumer-driven contract tests but not their form. Ours covers our own apps, routes and methods only; which outside partners' contracts to include is for the lead.
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-03 moves from not built to **partly** (now 12 met, 24 partly, 28 not built).
- **Not built:** response field checks (the OpenAPI document does not describe bodies), calls built from a variable path, and partner contracts.
- **Checked:** the new test file (2 passed, 1 skipped in the container; the freshness check passes on the host), ruff clean; the full backend suite before this change passed (2236 passed, 1 skipped).

### DEV-169: open partner requests are held when a customer is suspended (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-39)

- **What was done:** when support approves suspending a customer, every request still open (sent, accepted or in progress) between it and another customer is put on hold, inside the same step. The other side sees it: the clinic gets an "on hold" tag, an explanation and a number on its home screen (staff dashboard `partner_on_hold`); the partner sees "on hold" and its Accept/Start/Result buttons go away, and the server refuses them. The clinic can cancel a held request; reinstating the customer releases the hold. Delivered, declined and cancelled requests are left alone. Each hold and release is on the request's history and in the audit chain. The wording never tells the other side that a suspension happened.
- **TEMPORARY:** the specification wants a person assigned to reconcile (T-39, T-55); here the clinic's staff see the count and decide. Whether a named owner, a time limit or an automatic cancel is wanted is for your lead. A held request that is already delivered still waits for a doctor's review as before.
- **Effect on the comparison:** T-39 moves from not built to **partly** (now 12 met, 25 partly, 27 not built).
- **Not built:** a named owner for each held request, disconnecting one connector (only whole customers can be suspended), and the same for webhooks and orders.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (partner suspended, clinic suspended, cancel, reinstate, partner cannot move a held one, only open ones held and not twice); ruff, import-linter, `makemigrations` (new `exchange/0002_request_on_hold`); the staff and partner apps type-check.

### DEV-170: a held partner request has a named owner (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-39, T-55)

- **What was done:** DEV-169 held open requests when a customer is suspended but nobody was named. Now, when the partner is the one suspended, each held request is assigned to the person at the clinic who sent it. The request list shows "This one is yours to decide" or "<name> sent it and decides"; the staff home screen has a separate "yours to decide" number beside the clinic-wide one. Releasing the hold clears the owner. When the clinic itself is suspended there is no owner (nobody there can sign in).
- **TEMPORARY:** that the sender is the owner is ours; your lead may want a role (such as the administrator) or a rota instead, a time limit, or an escalation if the sender has left. T-39 stays **partly**.
- **Not built:** reassigning an owner, escalation, and a limit on how long a hold may wait.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_exchange.py` (owner, colleague view, counts, release); with `test_attention.py` 47 passed; ruff clean; the staff app type-checks; new migration `exchange/0003_request_hold_owner`.

### DEV-171: cursor paging that re-checks rights on every page (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-07)

- **What was done:** a small helper (`foundation/pagination.py`) pages any list newest first with a signed cursor. The cursor is only a position (time and public id of the last row); it is signed so it cannot be forged or edited, tied to the login it was given to and to one list, and expires after 15 minutes. It carries no permission: each page is read afresh with the caller's rights checked then, so if a doctor's care link ends between two pages the next page comes back empty. Used first by the clinic's partner-request list: `GET /exchange/requests?limit=&cursor=` (limit 1 to 100) returns `requests` and `next_cursor`; with neither parameter the list behaves as before.
- **TEMPORARY:** the page size, the 15 minute life and the choice of lists are ours. T-07 moves from not built to **partly** (now 12 met, 26 partly, 26 not built).
- **Not built:** the other lists (they are still capped at a fixed number), paging in the front-end screens, and a stable result if rows are added during paging beyond newest-first order.
- **Checked:** `test_pagination.py` (6 tests: every row once in order, old shape unchanged, bad limit or forged cursor refused, another login's cursor refused, expiry, right taken away between pages); the OpenAPI snapshot is unchanged; ruff, mypy and import-linter clean.

### DEV-172: the partner's request list pages too (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-07)

- **What was done:** `GET /exchange/incoming?limit=&cursor=` now pages the partner's list the same way as DEV-171, with its own cursor (a cursor from the clinic's list is refused here). Without the parameters it behaves as before. T-07 stays **partly**: the other lists are still capped.
- **Checked:** one new test in `test_pagination.py` (7 in all) plus the OpenAPI test; 14 passed; ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-173: a test that public ids never carry a patient identifier (GHE-13 T-06)

- **What was done:** no product change, a proof. `test_public_ids.py` checks that every public id column (more than 50 tables) uses only the random generator and cannot be edited, that the generator gives distinct, unrelated 22-character ids, and that after creating a patient with a distinctive identifier and login, no stored public id of that customer contains any part of it. T-06 moves from partly to **met** (now 13 met, 25 partly, 26 not built).
- **Limit:** it scans one customer's rows and the id columns; the order number a clinic types for a partner request is the clinic's own choice, not an id we generate.
- **Checked:** 3 tests passed; ruff clean.

### DEV-174: a partner has no secret until Zoiko has recorded three checks (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-43)

- **What was done:** a partner added by a clinic administrator or partner developer now starts **pending** with no secret. A Zoiko support person (R11) records an identity check, a legal check and a security check, each with a note of 3 to 300 characters (new platform page "Partner checks"). Only when all three are in can the clinic issue the secret (`issue-secret`), which is shown once and makes the partner active. A pending partner cannot be resumed, suspended into life or given a new secret; it can only be revoked. Support's checks are recorded in the customer's own tenant and audit chain (`integration.partner_check_*`, `integration.partner_secret_issued`). Partners that already existed keep the state they had.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that one support person may record each check, that the three checks are these three, and that the note is free text. Whether two people must agree (as for suspension, DEV-080) and what proof counts are open.
- **Not built:** no proof is attached or verified, no expiry or re-check on a change of profile (T-48), and no sandbox, certified or live stages (T-41, T-47).
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_partner_events.py` (80 in that file and the portal tests passed with the helper that now issues a secret), neighbouring openapi, exchange, sponsor, suspension and contract tests pass (141 passed, 1 skipped), ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean, three apps type-check, robot 19 now goes through the checks, robots 6 and 10 pass with the new page. T-43 moves from not built to **partly** (now 13 met, 26 partly, 25 not built).

### DEV-175: the clinical switch is checked while a workflow runs, and deadlines are proven DST-safe (GHE-12 WF-T07, WF-T40)

- **What was done:** `WORKFLOW_CLINICAL_ENABLED` was only checked when a clinical definition was published. The engine now checks it on starting a workflow and on every forward step (`_clinical_must_be_on`); turning it off later stops new clinical workflows and stops existing ones moving forward, while cancelling and blocking stay possible so nothing is stranded. Ordinary workflows are untouched. A new test proves timers are an exact number of minutes added to a UTC instant at the four 2027 US and European clock changes.
- **Not built:** nothing checks a named approval, only the switch; calendar, working-day and per-country deadline rules are still not supported.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_orchestration.py` (115 in the file passed); ruff and mypy clean. WF-T07 and WF-T40 move from not built to **met** (workflow engine now 27 met, 15 partly, 18 not built of 60).

### DEV-176: an incident of unknown severity stays open until a safety officer assesses it (TEMPORARY, GHE-20 AT38)

- **What was done:** an incident can be logged with severity "unknown". It cannot be closed (422) until a safety officer (R12) gives it a level, SEV-0 to SEV-3, with a note of 3 to 300 characters. This can happen once, only while the incident is open and unknown. Who assessed it and the note are kept, and `incident.assess` is audited. The platform Incidents page offers "Assess this incident" to safety officers and hides "Close" until then. Migration `trust 0022`.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that a safety officer is the "clinician" the specification means, that one person can assess, and that nothing follows (no notice to affected providers, no time limit).
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_incidents.py` (59 in the file passed); ruff, mypy, migrations clean; the platform app type-checks; the OpenAPI snapshot was refreshed. AT38 moves from not built to **partly** (safety now 8 met, 20 partly, 26 not built of 54).

### DEV-177: two revisions of one country pack can never both be signed (GHE-16 AT-16)

- **What was done:** the database now refuses a second signed revision of the same pack (a partial unique rule on customer and pack name where the status is signed, migration `jurisdiction 0002`). The normal path already retired the old revision when a suspended pack came back as a new draft, so this is the guard for two requests that race. The migration retires older signed duplicates in any old database, keeping the newest.
- **Not built:** there is still no separate activation step, and two different packs for one country are not checked against each other (for deadlines the strictest still wins).
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_packs.py` (43 in the file); ruff, mypy and migrations clean. AT-16 moves from not built to **partly**.

### DEV-178: a read-only preflight for a country pack (GHE-16 AT-14)

- **What was done:** `GET /platform/packs/<id>/preflight` (support and safety staff) lists what the pack still lacks, the capabilities signing would switch on, those already on today and those that would be new. It writes nothing; the only record is the platform's normal log of the access itself.
- **Not built:** it is a report, not a rehearsal of a real activation, because there is no activation step.
- **Checked:** 1 new test proving no pack, approval or pack audit row changes. AT-14 moves from not built to **partly** (country packs now 6 met, 19 partly, 11 not built of 36).

### DEV-179: report days on the clinic's own clock (FIX, closes the UTC note of DEV-091)

- **What was done:** the staff **Reports** page now counts days from midnight to midnight on the clinic's own clock instead of UTC. The clock is the time zone the clinic's places share (`catalog.organization_zone`); when its places use different zones, or it has none yet, there is no single clock and the report uses UTC and says why (`zone`, `zone_source`: places, mixed or none). "Bookings made each day" groups by that clock, the period is whole local days ending with the clinic's today, and the other blocks (referrals, waiting list, messages, attendance) use the same period. Week-day tables already used each booking's own clock and are unchanged.
- **Choice (differs from the plan we first wrote down):** no new setting and no migration. We first planned a time zone setting per clinic; the clinic's places already carry a zone, so we use that. A clinic that wants a different clock changes its place's zone. This also means there is nothing for the lead to configure.
- **Temporary rule to confirm (add to docs/LEAD-NOTE.md questions):** a clinic whose places are in different zones reports in UTC. If the lead wants a single reporting zone chosen by the clinic (or one report per place), that needs a setting after all.
- **Not built:** a per-clinic reporting zone setting; a report per place.
- **Checked:** new tests in `tests/test_reports_zone.py` (the zone and its source, the period is whole local days, a booking just after and just before the clinic's midnight lands on the right day, nothing older than the period, same-zone places, mixed zones and no places fall back to UTC, a UTC clinic as before, the other blocks still present, who may read, bad day counts); the existing report tests; typecheck of the operations app.

### DEV-180: duplicate matching, adjudication and identity proofing (ADD, GHE-06, TEMPORARY rule)

- **What was done:** records that might be one person are found without decrypting anything (keyed fingerprints of birth date and phone, per customer and per purpose: `foundation/crypto.blind_index`) and held as a **match case** (`MatchCase`, chained append-only `MatchDecision`). Nothing is merged automatically, and a confirmed link merges nothing. Rule: a name and birth date match, or a birth date and phone match, or a name and phone match. Slips (swapped date digits), twins (same birth date and family name) and shared phones are flagged. A case is **critical** (two different reviewers must agree) when flagged as twins or a slipped date, or when a patient has a linked doctor or guardian. A reviewer can ask for a recheck, confirm "same person", mark "different people" (remembered until the details change) or send a link back (split). Identity is **verified only by a recorded staff check** of an official document (`ProofingRecord`: type, outcome, validity, who and where; never the document or number); it lapses when the name or birth date change, on expiry or on withdrawal, and cannot be recorded while a case is open. Screens: staff **Duplicates**, an **Identity** card on each patient, and the patient's own profile (read only, never shows "under review"). Backend: `modules/trust/identity_*.py`, `api/identity.py`, migration `0021`, command `rebuild_match_keys`.
- **Temporary rules to confirm (add to docs/LEAD-NOTE.md questions):** which documents count as proof; the similarity thresholds; whether guardian or doctor links should always need two reviewers; who may confirm a merge; the three-year default validity of a check.
- **Not built:** an actual merge or unmerge of records (only the decision is recorded); matching across clinics beyond the shared fingerprints; fingerprint rotation when keys rotate beyond the rebuild command.
- **Checked:** 86 backend tests (rules and flows, including denials: patients, other clinics, self-confirmation); typecheck and build of the operations and patient apps.

### DEV-181: quiet hours for routine reminder emails (TEMPORARY, GHE-18 TC30)

- **What was done:** a patient can set quiet hours (a start, an end and a time zone) next to the email switch on the Notifications page. A routine reminder email queued inside the window is held until the window ends (`QueuedEmail.next_attempt_at`). The end is a wall-clock time in the patient's zone, so a clock change cannot put it on the wrong side. Start and end must differ; a window may cross midnight; they can be cleared; every change is audited. `GET/POST /my/email-notices` carries the new `quiet` field. Migration `comms 0003`.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that only the appointment reminder counts as routine, and that cancellations, moves, confirmations, clinic messages and reviewed results always go at once; and that the patient's own zone, not the clinic's or the country's rule, decides the window.
- **Not built:** SMS and push (only email exists), country quiet-hour rules, emergency-priority override rules beyond "not routine".
- **Checked:** 8 new tests in `test_email_notices.py` (set, read and clear; seven bad windows refused; only patients; the end across daylight-saving changes in March and October; a reminder held while a cancellation is not), 27 in the file passed; ruff, mypy, migrations clean; the patient app type-checks. TC30 moves from not built to **partly** (comms now 3 met, 28 partly, 33 not built of 64).

### DEV-182: turning emails off cancels update emails already waiting (GHE-18 TC37)

- **What was done:** when a patient switches email updates off, any `patient_update` email still queued for their login address is cancelled before the worker sends it: its state becomes **cancelled**, its text is wiped and its address masked, as after a send, and `comms.queued_emails_cancelled` is audited. Only emails still waiting are touched; one already being sent cannot be taken back, mail of other kinds (such as address confirmation) is kept, and other people's mail is untouched. New mail state `cancelled` (migration `foundation 0005`), `foundation.mail.cancel_queued`, and an internal `trust.login_email_of_actor` (never sent to a screen).
- **Not built:** there is only one channel (email), so switching between channels is not possible; a switch-off does not recall an email that has already been handed to the mail server.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_email_notices.py` (waiting mail cancelled and masked, other kinds and other people kept, nothing waiting is harmless); ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean. TC37 moves from not built to **partly** (comms now 3 met, 29 partly, 32 not built of 64).

### DEV-183: an open serious or unassessed incident holds a release back (TEMPORARY, GHE-20 readiness)

- **What was done:** the go-live readiness gate (G3) has a third requirement that nobody signs, checked by the system like the country pack and the hazard register: **no open SEV-0 or SEV-1 incident, and none of unknown severity** (DEV-176).
