Any one of them makes the decision NO GO with the blocker `g3.open_incidents:missing`; minor (SEV-2, SEV-3) or resolved incidents do not. An empty incident log is clean. The platform Readiness page shows the new row, and nobody can sign or stop it by hand.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that SEV-0 and SEV-1 are the "serious" levels, that an unassessed incident counts as serious until a safety officer rules otherwise, and that this is a hard hold with no waiver. The specification's narrow waivers with compensating controls are still not modelled.
- **Not built:** it looks at the hand-written incident log only, not at vulnerabilities, error budgets or monitoring (there is no monitoring stack beyond DEV-130).
- **Checked:** 8 new tests in `test_readiness.py` (three serious levels hold a release, four minor or resolved cases do not, it cannot be signed or stopped by hand) and one older test updated for the extra row; 55 in the file passed; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the platform app type-checks.

### DEV-184: intended-purpose statements, approved before a capability can go live (TEMPORARY, GHE-08 AT01, AT05)

- **What was done:** a new record, `safety.IntendedPurpose`, says what one capability is for: the statement (20 to 500 characters), who uses it, and a class chosen from administrative, clinical support or regulated. There is no default class. It is versioned and never edited; a change is a new version, and approving it supersedes the old one. Support or safety staff may propose; only a safety officer who did not write it may approve; a regulated one needs a named legal manufacturer first (AT05); withdrawing needs a reason. The go-live readiness gate has a fourth system-checked requirement, `intended_purpose` (G4): every capability of a release needs an approved statement, or the release cannot be authorised. New platform page "Intended purposes". Migration `safety 0002` with row-level security.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** the three classes; that support may propose and only safety approves; that this checks authorisation of a release (G4), not G3 readiness; and that one approved statement per capability is enough (no per-country or per-market statements).
- **Not built:** a country pack can still switch a capability on with no statement; the intended-users text is recorded but nothing enforces it (AT07); the legal manufacturer is free text with no register behind it; no reclassification review when a marketing claim changes (AT02).
- **Checked:** 9 new tests in `test_purposes.py` and 2 in `test_readiness.py` (the fixtures now approve every capability); 70 passed across both files; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the platform app type-checks; the API snapshot was refreshed. AT01 and AT05 move from not built to **partly** (safety now 8 met, 22 partly, 24 not built of 54).

### DEV-185: the patient sees the local emergency number from an approved country pack (TEMPORARY, GHE-08 AT47)

- **What was done:** country packs already carried an `emergency_number` setting that nothing used. `GET /my/emergency` (patients only) returns the number stated by a SIGNED, unexpired pack for the country of the patient's clinic, and the patient app's Help page shows it under the existing "In an emergency" wording. If there is no country, no signed pack, a suspended or expired pack, a pack with no number, or packs that state different numbers, nothing is added: a number is never invented or guessed. The pack has already been approved by a legal and a clinical reviewer, so the number carries those two approvals.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that a number stated by the approved pack is enough (the clinical-safety review of emergency wording, decision 24, is still open for the surrounding text), and that disagreeing packs mean "show nothing".
- **Not built:** several numbers or alternatives per country (crisis lines, nearest hospital), per-language wording, and any check that a number works.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_emergency_number.py` (shown for a signed pack; none without a country, pack or for another country; stops when suspended; none when the pack states none; disagreeing packs give none while agreeing ones show it; patients only); ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the patient app type-checks; the API snapshot was refreshed. AT47 moves from not built to **partly** (safety now 8 met, 24 partly, 22 not built of 54).

### DEV-186: secrets are redacted from every log line (GHE-20 B-05)

- **What was done:** the console log handler now runs a filter (`foundation/log_redaction.py`) before any line is written. It replaces, with `[redacted]`, partner and webhook signing secrets (`pcsec_`, `whsec_`), bearer credentials in a header, the signature inside `Zoiko-Signature`, sealed field values (`zf1:...`) and the value after a word that names a secret (`password`, `secret`, `token`, `api_key`, `authorization`, including names like `client_secret`). It cleans the message, its arguments, exception text and stack text. It never drops a record and never raises: if it cannot check a line it withholds the text rather than risk printing it.
- **Not built:** there is no CI pipeline to scan, so "secret in CI output" is not covered; a secret of a shape the filter does not know would pass; no security incident is raised when something is redacted; the filter is on the console handler only (production log shipping is not set up).
- **Checked:** 22 new tests in `test_log_redaction.py` (eleven kinds of secret in different surroundings, six ordinary lines left alone, the words alone do not trigger it, message arguments, exception text, a bad format does not break logging, the handler is configured); ruff and mypy clean. B-05 moves from not built to **partly** (operations now 5 met, 19 partly, 56 not built of 80).

### DEV-187: a change of class in an intended purpose is reviewed by two people (TEMPORARY, GHE-08 AT02)

- **What was done:** when a new intended-purpose statement (DEV-184) gives a capability a different class from the one now approved, it is flagged as a reclassification and needs two different safety officers, neither of them its author, to review it. The first review keeps it a proposal (`reviews` 1 of 2, audited as `safety.purpose_first_review`); the second, by someone else, approves it and supersedes the old one, which stays in force until then. The same person cannot review twice. A first statement and a same-class rewording still need one approval. The platform page shows "changes the class" and the review count. Migration `safety 0003`.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that any change of class counts, whichever direction, and that two safety officers is the right bar.
- **Not built:** marketing or public claims are not recorded, so a changed claim triggers nothing unless a new statement is proposed; no time limit on a pending review.
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `test_purposes.py` (two different officers needed; the author is neither reviewer; only a changed class is flagged; the first review is audited); 17 passed in the file; ruff, mypy and migrations clean; the platform app type-checks. AT02 moves from not built to **partly** (safety now 8 met, 25 partly, 21 not built of 54).

### DEV-188: a proof that a revoked role cannot move a workflow step (GHE-12 WF-T10)

- **What was done:** no product change, a test. After a staff member's role is ended, a step they could take a moment earlier is refused (403) and the workflow does not move, while an administrator still can. This shows that every step is checked against the roles held now.
- **Not built:** delegation (acting on someone else's behalf) does not exist, so a revoked delegation is not modelled.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_orchestration.py`; ruff clean. WF-T10 moves from not built to **partly** (workflow engine now 27 met, 16 partly, 17 not built of 60).

### DEV-191: an incident touching patient safety cannot close until a safety officer records the hand-off (TEMPORARY, GHE-18 G-08)

- **What was done:** an incident can be logged as touching patient safety. It cannot be closed (422) until a safety officer (R12) records the hand-off or fallback that covers it, once, with a note of 3 to 300 characters (`incident.handoff` audited). While such an incident is open it also holds a release back, whatever its severity level (the open-incidents requirement of DEV-183). The platform Incidents page has the checkbox, a "safety impact" mark, and "Record the hand-off" for safety officers, and hides "Close" until then. Migration `trust 0024`. The 3-character minimum for notes is now enforced for incident assessments too (it was only non-empty).

### DEV-192: a lawful hold stops an erasure request being completed (TEMPORARY, GHE-12 WF-T35)

- **What was done:** a clinic administrator can record a **lawful hold** on an open data request (a legal duty to keep the data for now), with a reason of 3 to 300 characters, and lift it with a reason. While it stands, an erasure request cannot be completed (422); the clinic can decline it with a reason or lift the hold. Other kinds of request are unaffected. The staff Data requests page shows the hold and its reason and offers "Record a lawful hold" / "Lift the hold"; the patient is told only "some of this data is being kept for now because the law requires it", never why. `rights.hold_placed` and `rights.hold_lifted` are audited. Migration `rights 0003`.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that the hold belongs to one request rather than to a patient or customer, that only a clinic administrator may place or lift it, and the patient wording.
- **Not built:** holds on a whole patient or customer; retention periods and expiry; nothing is deleted by the product in any case, so "completing" an erasure is the clinic's own work.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_rights.py` (an erasure refused under a hold and declined instead; lifting lets it complete; other kinds unaffected; the patient never sees the reason; roles, reasons, repeats and closed cases; audited); 31 passed in the file; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the staff and patient apps type-check; the API snapshot was refreshed. WF-T35 moves from not built to **partly** (workflow engine now 27 met, 18 partly, 15 not built of 60).

### DEV-193: suspending a customer with patients mid-journey needs a continuity plan (TEMPORARY, GHE-18 H-05)

- **What was done:** when support asks to suspend a customer, the platform counts its open referrals (inside the customer) and its open partner requests (in the shared space). If there are any, the request needs a continuity plan of 20 to 1000 characters saying who looks after those patients; without one it is refused (422). The plan and the count are stored on the request and shown on the platform Suspensions page to the second person who approves it. Reinstating never needs a plan. Migration `trust 0025`.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** what counts as "in the middle of something" (open referrals and open partner requests only, not upcoming appointments or open messages) and the 20-character minimum.
- **Not built:** nothing checks the plan is carried out; there is no contract-end step separate from suspension; upcoming appointments are not counted.

### DEV-194: text messages through Twilio, built only from approved templates (TEMPORARY, GHE-16 TC05, TC49)

- **What was done:** a text-message channel. `foundation/texts.py` queues a text in the change's own transaction (`QueuedText`, migration `foundation 0006`) and the worker sends due texts every 30 seconds. A text can only be built from an approved template: "There is an update for you on Zoiko Access. Sign in to see it." or a numeric code. Only the named values are allowed, each checked against a strict pattern; an unknown template, an extra or missing value, a value of the wrong shape, or a number not in international form is refused. So a care detail cannot be put into a text at all. Sending goes through Twilio's REST API when `TEXT_PROVIDER=twilio` and its account values are set (never logged). `console` is a development stand-in that logs only a masked number. The default sends nothing and lets texts wait. After sending, or after giving up, the text is wiped and the number masked. A patient who has email updates on can also tick "Also send me a text message" (migration `comms 0004`): they then get the generic text for the same events, held by quiet hours like the email.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** Twilio as the provider (chosen 2026-11-16, DEV-195); that only the patient's own number, written with + and the country code, is ever texted; the two templates and their wording.
- **Not built:** the number is not verified by sending a code (the code template exists for that, but there is no verification flow yet); no delivery receipts from Twilio; no push notifications; no per-country sender or wording.
- **Checked:** 20 new tests in `test_texts.py` (only approved wording; eight shapes refused; must be queued inside the change; sending wipes and masks; no provider means nothing sent; retries and giving up; the exact Twilio call without the token in the clear; Twilio without account values does not send; the patient gets only the generic text, and none without asking or with a local number); ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the patient app type-checks. TC05 and TC49 move from not built to **partly** (comms now 3 met, 32 partly, 29 not built of 64).

### DEV-210: a country pack is signed when approved, and the signature is checked on every read (TEMPORARY, GHE-16 AT-10)

- **What was done:** when the second approval lands, the server signs the pack (Ed25519, adapted from the `maruthi` branch's `packs/signing.py`). The signature covers the pack's name, country, service, revision, the hash of its exact rules, its customer and cell, and the key id (`modules/jurisdiction/signing.py`, migration `jurisdiction 0003`). Every place that uses a signed pack checks the signature first: the data-request deadline, the emergency number, the capabilities a workflow needs, and the release gate's pack check. A row whose rules were changed in the database, one marked "signed" by hand, one carrying another pack's signature, or one signed with another key counts for nothing (fail closed), and the platform Packs page shows "Signature does not match". Suspending or retiring removes the signature, so flipping the status back does nothing. Packs already signed before this change were signed once, as they stood, by the migration.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm), decision 4 (keys):** the private key is the setting `ZOIKO_PACK_SIGNING_KEY`, held by the server, not by a separate governance service. In development, with no key set, one is derived from the Django secret key, and the release check refuses to start production that way. One key signs and checks: there is no list of older trusted keys, so changing the key makes every signed pack count for nothing until it is approved again.
- **Not built:** a separate signing service or hardware key, key rotation with overlap, and signing of the status changes after signing (suspension clears the signature instead).
- **Checked:** 13 new tests in `test_pack_signing.py` and 1 in `test_release_check.py`; 179 passed across the packs, emergency-number, country-deadline, workflow-packs and release-check files; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the platform app type-checks. AT-10 moves from partly to **met** (packs now 7 met, 20 partly, 9 not built of 36).

### DEV-211: one workflow engine and one country-pack system: keep `orchestration` and `jurisdiction`, retire the `maruthi` branch's (DECISION)

- **What was decided:** the `maruthi` branch has its own workflow engine (inside `modules/workflow`, with journeys J1 to J3 written as JSON) and its own country packs (`modules/packs`). This branch already has `modules/orchestration` (DEV-132) and `modules/jurisdiction` (DEV-128). We keep this branch's two and do not bring the other two over.
- **Why:** `orchestration` was checked against the specification's 60 acceptance tests (DEV-135), checks the clinical switch while running and keeps deadlines safe across clock changes (DEV-175), and refuses to invent deadlines. The `maruthi` journeys hardcode them (for example 1,440 minutes to acknowledge a referral). `jurisdiction` is already used by the data-request deadline, the emergency number, workflow capabilities and the release gate, and has the two-person review. The one thing `packs` had that `jurisdiction` lacked, cryptographic signing, was brought over as DEV-210.
- **Still to bring over from `maruthi`, onto this branch's modules:** the referral and waiting-list journeys (as `orchestration` definitions whose deadlines come from each clinic's own settings, not from the code), partner booking (`bookingflow`), the connector framework and the public developer API. Each will be its own entry.

### DEV-196: production settings, a production image and the Google Cloud setup (TEMPORARY, GHE-18)

- **What was done:** `config/settings/production.py` (DEBUG and the development sign-in off; HTTPS redirect, secure cookies, one year of HSTS, no framing; the health probes are not redirected). `backend/Dockerfile.production` (runtime dependencies only, gunicorn, production settings, a non-root user) with `requirements/production.txt` (gunicorn). `deploy/gcp/` holds Terraform for London (`europe-west2`): a private network; Cloud SQL PostgreSQL 16 with no public address, high availability, encrypted connections only, daily backups kept 30 times and 7 days of point-in-time recovery in the same region; Secret Manager replicated only in London, generating the Django key, the field-encryption key, both database passwords and the metrics token; Artifact Registry; the web service on Cloud Run with ready and live checks; a migration job that runs as the table owner while the app never does; the worker as a Cloud Run job started every minute by Cloud Scheduler (`relay_outbox --once` then `deliver_webhooks --once`); an uptime check with an e-mail alert. The step-by-step guide is in `docs/DEPLOYMENT-GCP.md`.
- **Checked:** `terraform validate` passes (Terraform 1.9 in Docker, google ~> 6.0, random ~> 3.6); 2 tests of the production settings pass. **Not run:** `terraform plan` or `apply` against a real project; building the production image (the clean build fails on network time-outs here, A7).
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** the machine sizes, backup counts and the one-minute worker schedule are starting points to measure, not decisions.
- **Not built:** front-end hosting, a mail provider, a scheduled logical export and a timed restore on Cloud SQL, a second region, Cloud Armor, cost budgets, and a pipeline with signed images. D-08 moves from not built to **partly**.

### DEV-212: frozen event contract, one release identity, and migrations that only add (GHE-20 F-03, F-04, F-06)

- **What was done:** (1) `foundation/contracts.py` says which event changes would break a consumer still on the last release: an event removed; a field removed, added (every schema refuses unknown fields), retyped or no longer always present; a changed subject. The last release's events are frozen in `foundation/released_events.json` by `manage.py freeze_event_contract <release>`, which refuses to freeze over a breaking change. A test compares today's catalogue with the frozen one, so CI fails before a breaking change ships. The baseline is frozen as `2026.11.16-baseline`. (2) `GET /api/v1/version` (no login, no data about people) returns the build, the cell, and fingerprints of the API, the events and the migration set, joined into one `release_id`. Two servers with the same build label but different code are different releases (`api/release.py`). (3) A test fails when any migration drops or renames a column or table, or runs SQL that drops, renames, truncates or deletes, and when a data step has no way back. (4) `identity.subject.proofed.v1` (from DEV-180) was published but missing from the event catalogue. It is now listed, carrying references and status words only.
- **Not built:** a release pipeline, canary or rollout that would read the release id or stop on these checks. The API side already had its guard (`api/consumer_contract.json`: every call an app makes must still be served).
- **Checked:** 16 new tests in `test_contracts.py`; the OpenAPI and AsyncAPI snapshots refreshed (the OpenAPI one also took in routes added by other work today); 31 passed across the contract, event-catalogue and OpenAPI files; ruff, mypy and import rules clean. F-03 moves to **met**, F-04 and F-06 to **partly** (operations now 6 met, 24 partly, 50 not built of 80).

### DEV-197: the five apps on Cloud Storage and CDN behind one HTTPS load balancer (TEMPORARY)

- **What was done:** `deploy/gcp/frontend.tf` adds a bucket for each app (patient, staff, doctor, platform, partners) served through Cloud CDN at `<app>.<domain>`, and one global HTTPS load balancer with a Google-managed certificate. On every app host it sends `/api/*`, `/healthz` and `/readyz` to the Cloud Run web service, because the apps call the API at a relative `/api/v1`. `api.<domain>` goes to the web service too, and http redirects to https. The web service now accepts traffic only through the load balancer, `DJANGO_ALLOWED_HOSTS` lists every host, and the patient app address in e-mails follows the domain. The guide has the build-and-upload and DNS steps.
- **Checked:** `terraform validate` passes with these resources; all five apps build for production (`npm run build`), and the development sign-in is not in any build. **Not run:** a plan or apply against a real project.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** the host names (`patient.`, `staff.`, `doctor.`, `platform.`, `partners.`, `api.`) and serving the apps from public buckets (they hold no data).
- **Not built:** Cloud Armor rules, a content security policy header on the apps, and cache invalidation on release (Cloud CDN keeps old files until they expire).

### DEV-214: an identifier catalogue, checked against every table and event (ADD)

- **What was done:** `foundation/identifiers.py` lists every kind of identifier the product uses (internal ids, public ids, the `*_ref` references between modules, request numbers, keys and tokens): what it names, its format (checked by `check()` where the format is fixed), its scope (cell, customer or global), whether it may leave the cell in an event or a partner message, and whether it is built from or reveals personal data. `tests/test_identifiers.py` walks every model and every catalogued event: a new identifier field that is not listed fails, a listed kind no table uses fails, every reference in an event must be a kind allowed out, and the fingerprints of a birth date and a phone number (used to find duplicate records) may never appear in an event.
- **Found, not changed (each needs an owner's decision or its own entry):** (1) a payer's member number for a person is stored in plain text (`sponsor.PlanMember.member_id`), while the same kind of number is encrypted in `coverage`; (2) the clinic's own order number sent to partners (`exchange.ExchangeRequest.subject_token`) accepts any short code, so nothing stops a clinic putting a hospital or NHS number there; (3) data-request numbers (`DSR-` plus the upper-cased start of a public id) can collide, because upper-casing merges different ids; (4) the partner key id given to partners contains the customer's public id; (5) the same reference is stored at different widths in different tables (for example `actor_ref` is 22 characters in six tables and 64 in seven).
- **Checked:** 24 tests in `test_identifiers.py`; ruff, mypy and import rules clean.

### DEV-198: a phone number is proved by a texted code before any text goes to it (TEMPORARY)

- **What was done:** a patient asks for a code (`POST /my/phone-check`). A six-digit code is texted to the number on their own profile, which must start with + and the country code, using the approved "code" template (DEV-194). They type it back (`POST /my/phone-check/confirm`). Only a verified number receives the update texts, and changing the profile number ends the verification. Neither the code nor the number is stored, only keyed fingerprints (`foundation.crypto.blind_index`). A code lasts 10 minutes and allows 5 tries; every wrong try is counted and kept (the refusal is raised after the transaction commits); at most 3 codes are sent an hour. Sending, wrong tries and success are audited. The patient Notifications page has "Text me a code" and the code box. New `PhoneCheck` model and two fields on the e-mail preference (migration `comms 0005`, row-level security on the new table).
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** the 10-minute life, 5 tries and 3 codes an hour; verifying again after an encryption-key rotation (the fingerprints use the current key).
- **Not built:** spotting a recycled number (a new owner of an old number) beyond the patient changing it; codes by voice call.
- **Checked:** 16 new tests in `test_phone_check.py` and the texting tests updated (36 passed across both files); ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the patient app type-checks.

### DEV-213: retention rules with no periods, and a report that only counts (TEMPORARY, decision 5, DEV-013)

- **What was done:** a new module, `modules/retention`. A clinic administrator proposes how long the clinic keeps one kind of data (a number of days, or no limit) with the basis for it, for example a law or policy. It counts only once a second, different administrator approves it. Approving supersedes the rule before; a proposal can be withdrawn; every step is audited. The product ships with no periods at all. Modules register the kinds of data they keep through `foundation/retention.py`: appointments (age from the appointment time) and closed message conversations (age from the last message). The rights module registers who is under a lawful hold (DEV-192). `GET /api/v1/staff/retention` reports, per kind of data, the rule in force, any proposal, and how many records are past the period, leaving out every patient under a hold. `POST` proposes, and `POST staff/retention/<rule>/approve|withdraw` decides (administrators only).
- **Nothing is deleted.** The product has no deletion path, and the report says so (`deletes_anything: false`). What to do with records past their period is decision 5.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** clinic administrators set the periods (not Zoiko, not a legal role); two administrators are enough; a hold on any data request of a patient protects all of that patient's records, whatever the request's state; the counts cover the whole customer.
- **Not built:** other kinds of data (audit trails, referrals, data requests, billing) are not registered yet; no per-country default periods from a country pack. The staff app has a "Keeping records" page (propose, approve, withdraw, counts), which type-checks but has not been clicked through by a person.
- **Checked:** 16 new tests in `test_retention.py` (no periods by default; two people; administrators only; seven bad proposals; one open proposal; withdraw and supersede; counting past the period without deleting; a lawful hold leaves a patient out; audited; a kind of data cannot be registered twice); ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-215: a visit with attendance on record cannot be cancelled (FIX, found by the cross-state tests)

- **What was found:** `tests/test_cross_state.py` (new: six state machines, each with a table of the steps allowed in each state; 100 forbidden pairs are tried and must be refused with nothing changed) showed that clinic staff could cancel a confirmed appointment after recording that the patient attended, or did not come. That rewrites what the clinic recorded about the visit.
- **What was done:** cancelling a booking whose attendance is recorded is refused (409), for every role. A past appointment with nothing recorded can still be cancelled to tidy it away. Attendance itself can still be corrected, as before.
- **Still allowed, for the lead to judge:** staff can confirm a request whose time has already passed.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_attendance.py`; the booking rows of `test_cross_state.py` gained an "attended" state; 75 passed across the attendance, booking-history and report files; ruff clean.

### DEV-199: Twilio delivery receipts for texts, signed and forward-only (GHE-16 TC21)

- **What was done:** when a text is sent through Twilio, Twilio's message id is kept (`QueuedText.provider_ref`) and Twilio is told where to send receipts (`TWILIO_STATUS_URL`, set by the Google Cloud setup to `https://api.<domain>/api/v1/webhooks/twilio/status`). That endpoint needs no sign-in: Twilio's signature (HMAC-SHA1 over the exact public address and the form fields, using the account's auth token) is the credential, checked in constant time before anything is used, and a wrong or missing one gets 403. A receipt only ever moves a text forward (queued, sending, sent, then delivered, undelivered or failed), so a late or repeated receipt changes nothing. An unknown message id or status gets a quiet 200, so guessing ids teaches nothing. Migration `foundation 0007`.
- **Not built:** nothing is shown on a screen yet (the status is recorded only); no alert when texts fail; e-mail has no receipts.
- **Checked:** 10 new tests in `test_text_receipts.py` (the id is kept; receipts recorded; late and repeated ones ignored; failures stay; three kinds of bad signature refused with nothing changed; no token means all refused; unknown ids and statuses ignored; the signature method matches Twilio's published one); the texting tests updated for Twilio's reply; ruff and mypy clean on these files; `terraform validate` passes. TC21 moves from not built to **partly**.

### DEV-216: a payer's member number is no longer stored in the clear (FIX, found by DEV-214)

- **What was done:** `sponsor.PlanMember` kept a payer's member number for a person in plain text. It is now sealed (`member_enc`, bound to its own row), found by a keyed fingerprint (`member_key`), and shown by its last four characters only (`member_tail`). The old column stays, empty, because columns are never dropped. Uniqueness moved to the fingerprint. Key rotation (`manage.py rotate_field_keys`) re-seals the numbers and rebuilds their fingerprints. Migration `sponsor 0002` seals every existing number, and stepping it back puts them back.
- **Also fixed:** data steps in migrations read the tables with no customer context. Row-level security is forced on the owner role too, so in production such a step could silently see no rows (in development migrations run as a superuser, which skips it). `foundation/rls.py` now has `for_each_tenant()`, which runs a data step once per customer with that customer's context set. This migration and the pack-signing one (DEV-210) use it. The older backfills (for example `trust 0015`) have already run and were left alone.
- **Not changed:** `sponsor.ProgramMember.member_ref` (an employer's own reference for a person) and the other DEV-214 findings.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_sponsor.py` (nothing in the stored row contains the number; checking cover still works; still unique); 42 passed in the file; the dev database's 9 numbers are all sealed and none is left in the clear; the migration was stepped back and forward; ruff, mypy and migrations clean.

### DEV-217: partner connectors and bookings confirmed by the clinic's booking system (TEMPORARY, ported from `maruthi`, GHE-13, GHE-22)

- **What was done:** (1) `modules/connector` records each operation Zoiko asks a partner system to perform. It keeps a receipt apart from an outcome: pending, sent, accepted (a technical receipt), confirmed or rejected (the partner's word), unknown (asked again, never guessed), and dead (a person redrives or settles it). Its history is append-only and chained; requests carry references only; and it is idempotent per connector. A development-only pretend partner exists only when `CONNECTOR_SIMULATOR` is on (development and test settings only). (2) `modules/bookingflow` lets a clinic administrator choose a connector as the clinic's booking system (`trust 0026`). A booking then stays requested until the partner confirms it. A refusal cancels it. A receipt, an unknown answer, or a person settling the operation by hand changes nothing, and a late answer after the booking moved on is ignored. With a booking system chosen, staff can no longer confirm by hand (409). Endpoints: `staff/booking-system`, `bookings/<id>/ask-provider`, `bookings/<id>/provider-stage`, and `staff/connector` (list, history, redrive, resolve). Events: `connector.operation.{accepted,confirmed,rejected,unknown,dead}.v1`, carrying references only.
- **Staff screens:** a new "Partner systems" page in the staff app (choose the booking system; operations that need a person, with "Try again" and the two hand settlements; waiting; recent answers). On Appointments, a clinic with a booking system sees "Ask the booking system" instead of "Confirm", and no "Confirm all". Type-checked; not yet clicked through by a person.
- **Not ported (DEV-211):** maruthi's workflow engine and its journey definition for partner bookings, its country packs, and its console screens.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that a chosen booking system forbids confirming by hand; that the connector screens use the workflow permissions.
- **Known gaps:** a clinic-staff move of a confirmed booking at such a clinic is confirmed without asking the partner; a partner's confirmation is accepted even after the clinic switched systems; no real partner adapter exists (needs a partner, B2 part 05).
- **Checked:** 45 tests in `test_connector.py` and `test_bookingflow.py`; 130 passed across the scheduling, booking-history, reschedule, grants, RLS, contracts and consumer-contract files; the booking rows of `test_cross_state.py` classify the new steps; snapshots refreshed; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean.

### DEV-200: a circuit breaker for the text provider (TEMPORARY, GHE-16 TC42)

- **What was done:** `foundation.models.ProviderCircuit` (migration `foundation 0008`) keeps a breaker per provider in the database, because the worker runs as a fresh job every minute. After 5 failures in a row the text breaker opens for 5 minutes. Nothing is sent while it is open, the texts already claimed in that batch go back to waiting without using up a try, and the next success closes it. The platform Service levels page now has a "Text messages" card showing the provider, the breaker, failures in a row, how often it has opened, and counts of waiting, sent and given-up texts and of delivery receipts (counts only, never a number or a text).
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** 5 failures and a 5-minute pause.
- **Not built:** no alert when it opens; no second provider to fail over to; e-mail has no breaker (it backs off per message).
- **Note on DEV-177:** its migration's one-off clean-up reads rows under row-level security, so on a real server (not a superuser) it sees nothing. That is harmless there, because a new database has no duplicate signed packs to clean up.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_text_breaker.py`, with the texting, receipt and database-grant tests (68 passed); ruff and mypy clean; the platform app type-checks. TC42 moves from not built to **partly**.

### DEV-201: a backup-error alert, and two safety rows recorded from existing evidence

- **What was done:** `deploy/gcp/monitoring.tf` adds an alert that e-mails the on-call address (at most once an hour) when Cloud SQL logs an error about a backup of this instance; `terraform validate` passes. The log filter has to be checked against a real instance's logs, and a backup that never runs at all is not detected by it. D-01 moves to **partly**.
- **Recorded, no code change:** AT33 (a stale policy cache must deny) is **partly**: there is no cache, and every decision reads the roles held at that moment (tests already show an ended role refused at once). AT43 (nothing national invented on screen) is **partly**: the emergency number and deadlines appear only from a signed pack, and "Verified" only after a recorded check (existing tests and robot 8).

### DEV-218: data-request numbers are random, unambiguous and unique (FIX, found by DEV-214)

- **What was found:** a request number was `DSR-` plus the upper-cased first 8 characters of a public id. Upper-casing merges different ids, so two requests could share a number, and the number could contain `-` or `_`.
- **What was done:** a new number is `DSR-` plus 8 random characters from Crockford's alphabet (no I, L, O or U), drawn again on a clash. The database refuses a second request with the same number within a customer. Migration `rights 0004` gives a fresh number to any later request that already shared one; the oldest keeps its number.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_rights.py`; 72 passed across the rights, identifiers and contracts files; ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-219: a payment-provider interface and a pretend provider, switched off (TEMPORARY, B3, DEV-139)

- **What was done:** `modules/billing/payments.py` defines what a payment provider must do (start a payment; say how it stands: pending, succeeded, failed or unknown) and a registry of providers. A **pretend provider** exists for development and tests only (`BILLING_PRETEND_PROVIDER`, on in dev and test settings only, refused otherwise). It succeeds, except for amounts ending in 13 minor units, which fail. Payments are off unless `BILLING_PAYMENT_PROVIDER` names a registered provider; with it empty (the default) starting a payment is refused (422). A patient starts a payment for their own issued invoice with an idempotency key. Each attempt is recorded (`PaymentAttempt`, migration `billing 0002`, references and amounts only, no card details). The provider is called outside any database transaction. A success marks the invoice paid through the same path staff use, and only when the balance still matches; failed and unknown leave it open; an unknown answer can be refreshed with the same key. Endpoints: `billing/invoices/<id>/payments` (GET, POST), `billing/payments/<id>/refresh`, `staff/billing/payments`.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** a patient may start a payment under their read right on billing plus a check that the invoice is theirs (the policy table gives patients no write right on billing).
- **Not built:** a real provider (needs the choice in decision B3, the seller of record and tax rules), provider webhooks, a job that re-asks about unknown payments, refunds, and settlement or a ledger.
- **Checked:** 13 new tests in `test_payments.py`; 118 passed across the payments, billing, identifiers, contracts, OpenAPI and event-catalogue files; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean.

### DEV-220: clinical references (pointers only), switched off (TEMPORARY, decision 3, DEV-041)

- **What was done:** a new module, `modules/clinicalref`. It is off unless `CLINICAL_REFERENCES_ENABLED` is on; when off, every endpoint answers 404. A treating doctor records that a record exists in the clinic's own system: the system's code, the record's id there, its kind (consultation note, result, letter, imaging, other) and the date it refers to. There is **no content and no free text**: the id must be a short code with a digit and no spaces, and a withdrawal gives a reason from a fixed list. A pointer is withdrawn, never deleted. The patient sees the kind, date, clinic and system; the care team also sees the record id and who added it. Every add, withdrawal and read is audited. Endpoints: `clinician/patients/<person>/references` (GET, POST), `clinician/references/<id>/withdraw`, `my/references`. Migration `clinicalref 0001`.
- **LEAD DECISION (17 Nov 2026):** keep it alongside consultation notes (DEV-304): pointers for records held elsewhere (results, letters), notes for consultations. Both stay off by default.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** it reuses the `diagnostic` policy class (attest to add or withdraw, read to view); any treating doctor of the clinic may withdraw, not only the author; the care team sees pointers from every clinic of the customer; guardians see nothing (the "records" scope is never granted).
- **Not built:** whether this may be switched on at all is decision 3; there is no list of known clinical systems, and no check that the record exists at the source. The pointers are a kind of data in the retention report (DEV-213), aged from the day the record is about.
- **Checked:** 20 new tests in `test_clinical_references.py`; the identifier catalogue gained the clinic's record id as personal and never leaving the cell; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean.

### DEV-318: a test that delivery attempts never show the message (GHE-13 T-54)

- **What was done:** no product change, a proof. `test_support_view.py` asserts the exact fields the delivery attempt list returns (status, counts, owner, a fingerprint, no message body, and the sent text and its references appear nowhere in the answer), and that a Zoiko support login (R11) is refused (403) on a clinic's delivery and inbound-event lists. T-54 stays **partly**: there is no separate view that lets regional support inspect attempts.
- **Checked:** 2 tests passed; ruff clean.

### DEV-221: a circuit breaker per partner connector, and an alternate path for bookings (TEMPORARY, GHE-20 C-08, GHE-13 T-32)

- **What was done:** each customer's partner connector has a circuit (`modules/connector/circuit.py`, migration `connector 0002`). After 5 failed or timed-out calls in a row it opens for 5 minutes: nothing is sent to that partner or asked of it, and its operations simply wait in their own state (pending, accepted or unknown). Nothing is lost or guessed. After the cool-down one trial call goes through: an answer closes the circuit, a failure reopens it for twice as long (capped at an hour). An adapter that reports an unsupported profile or version (`AdapterUnsupported`) **halts** only that connector until a person starts it again; its operation waits as pending. While a clinic's booking system is paused or halted, staff may confirm bookings by hand again (the alternate path), and the Appointments page shows "Confirm by hand (booking system is down)". Opening, halting, closing and resetting are audited. Endpoints: `staff/connector/circuits` and `staff/connector/circuits/<key>/reset`; the Partner systems page has a "Partner system health" card.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** the numbers (5 failures, 5 minutes, doubling to an hour) should come from each partner contract; that a down booking system lets staff confirm by hand.
- **Not built:** measuring slow answers that do arrive (the contract's latency target); per-cell rather than per-customer circuits; telling the partner or Zoiko support when a circuit opens.
- **Checked:** 7 new tests in `test_connector_circuit.py`; 102 passed across the connector, booking-flow, identifiers, contracts, OpenAPI and RLS files; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the staff app type-checks. T-32 moves to **met**, C-08 to **partly**.

### DEV-222: an NHS number can never be sent to a partner in an order number or description (FIX, found by DEV-214)

- **What was found:** a clinic's own order number and the short description it sends to a lab, pharmacy or home-care partner (`exchange`) accepted any short code. Nothing stopped a clinic typing the patient's NHS number there, and the first market is now the UK (DEV-195).
- **What was done:** `foundation/identifiers.contains_nhs_number()` finds a run of exactly ten digits (spaces, dashes, dots, colons, slashes or underscores between them allowed) that passes the NHS check digit (modulus 11). The exchange refuses an order number or a description holding one (422). A random ten-digit number passes about one time in eleven, so a few innocent numbers are refused too; that is the safe side.
- **Also fixed:** five tests in `test_exchange.py` broke when DEV-193 began asking for a continuity plan before suspending a customer with open partner requests; the test helper now gives one.
- **Not built:** other countries' national numbers (per country pack), and the same check on other values that leave Zoiko (webhook payloads already carry references only).
- **Checked:** 9 new cases in `test_identifiers.py`, 3 in `test_exchange.py`; 78 passed across the two files (45 in `test_exchange.py`); ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-223: an operation that may already have been done is never replayed to a partner that cannot recognise a repeat (FIX, GHE-12 WF-T55)

- **What was found:** a partner operation becomes "dead" either because every try failed before reaching the partner, or because the partner never gave a final answer (it acknowledged the call, or the outcome stayed unknown). A person could redrive both. For a partner that does not honour our idempotency key, redriving the second kind could do the thing twice.
- **What was done:** redrive is refused (UNSAFE_REPLAY) when the operation may have arrived (an "accepted" or "unknown" step is in its history), unless the partner honours our idempotency key. A person then checks with the partner and settles it by hand, which is recorded as theirs and never as the partner's word.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_connector.py`; 54 passed across the connector, circuit and booking-flow files; ruff and mypy clean. WF-T55 moves to **met**; T-35 and T-36 to **partly** (operations through a connector are covered; lab and pharmacy orders in the exchange do not use one yet).

### DEV-224: a partner's booking system is switched on only where a signed country pack allows it (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-46)

- **What was done:** country packs gained a capability, `partner_booking`. A clinic administrator can choose a real partner as the clinic's booking system (DEV-217) only while a signed, unexpired, unsuspended pack for the customer's country switches it on (403 `JURISDICTION_INACTIVE` otherwise). A pack is signed only with legal, privacy (DPIA) and security evidence recorded, so a failed check blocks switching on. Going back to confirming by hand is always allowed. The pretend partner (development only, synthetic data) is exempt. The platform Packs page lists the new capability.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** one capability for all partner booking systems (not one per partner or per contract).
- **Not built:** no certification stages (sandbox, certified, live) for a partner (T-47). (A pack that lapses later is now handled: DEV-229.)
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_partner_pack_gate.py`; 148 passed across the gate, booking-flow, packs, workflow-packs and readiness files; ruff and mypy clean; the platform app type-checks. T-46 moves to **partly**.

### DEV-225: workflow evidence keeps the time it happened at its source (GHE-12 WF-T31)

- **What was done:** each piece of evidence on a workflow step may carry `at`, the instant it happened at its source (with its offset; stored in UTC). It is kept apart from when the step was processed (`occurred_at`), so evidence delivered late keeps its true time while the order of steps stays the order in which we processed them. The step history lists such evidence under `late_evidence`. A source time more than 5 minutes in the future (clock skew), without an offset, or not an instant is refused (422). Old steps are unchanged (the field is optional and stored inside the evidence).
- **Also fixed:** `test_email_notices.py` still expected the email-settings answer without the text-message fields added by DEV-194 and DEV-198.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_orchestration.py`; 122 passed in the file; 30 in `test_email_notices.py`; ruff and mypy clean. WF-T31 moves to **met** (workflow engine now 29 met, 18 partly, 13 not built of 60).

### DEV-226: a partner operation that gave up becomes work in the clinic's queue (GHE-13 T-55)

- **What was done:** when a partner operation gives up (`connector.operation.dead.v1`), the work queue opens a "Settle a partner operation" item for the clinic that asked, due within the clinic's own response target. Staff claim it like any other work, and the staff app links it to the Partner systems page. A final answer, from the partner or settled by a person, closes it. Opening it twice changes nothing. Migration `workflow 0004` adds the kind.
- **Not built:** switching a connector off by hand does not open work for what it had in flight; a redriven operation's item stays open until the final answer.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_partner_work.py`; 138 passed across the work-queue, work-history, closed-work, data-request work, platform work, workflow-packs and the work rows of the cross-state tests; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the staff app type-checks. T-55 moves to **partly**.

### DEV-227: a real server refuses to start with a pretend partner or pretend payments switched on (GHE-20 F-10, GHE-13 T-41)

- **What was done:** the release check (`foundation/release_check.py`, run when the web server and worker start with DEBUG off, and by `manage.py check --deploy`) now lists as problems: the pretend partner switched on (`CONNECTOR_SIMULATOR`), the pretend payment provider switched on (`BILLING_PRETEND_PROVIDER`), and payments pointing at the pretend provider. Both are on only in development and test settings.
- **Checked:** 3 new cases in `test_release_check.py`; 78 passed, 1 skipped in the file; ruff clean. F-10 and T-41 move to **partly**.

### DEV-228: real texts only to a market a signed country pack has switched on (TEMPORARY, GHE-16 TC01, TC07)

- **What was done:** with the real text provider on (`TEXT_PROVIDER=twilio`), a patient-update text is queued, and a phone code is offered, only while a signed, current, unsuspended country pack for the customer's country switches **messaging** on (`modules/comms/market.py`). Otherwise the update text is simply not sent and asking for a code answers 403 `JURISDICTION_INACTIVE`. Because only valid, signed packs count (DEV-210), a pack that expires, is suspended or loses its signature stops new texts at once. The development stand-in and "no provider" are not checked, since they send nothing real. The lookup of what a customer's market has switched on moved into `jurisdiction.customer_capabilities()`, and the API uses it too.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** that the general "messaging" capability is the one that governs texts.
- **Not built:** the same check on email (it still goes to the development inbox until a mail provider is chosen); texts already queued before a pack lapses are not withdrawn.
- **Also fixed:** two lint failures left in files from earlier work today (`config/settings/base.py`, the `TWILIO_STATUS_URL` line with a comment copied from the line above; `modules/trust/oncall.py`, long lines).
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_text_market.py`; 133 passed across the text, phone-check, text-receipt, text-breaker, email-notices, country-deadline, partner-pack-gate and workflow-packs files; ruff clean on the whole backend; mypy and import rules clean on the changed modules. TC01 and TC07 move to **partly** (communications now 3 met, 36 partly, 25 not built of 64).

### DEV-229: when the country pack lapses, the partner is no longer asked and staff confirm by hand (GHE-13 T-46, GHE-20 C-08)

- **What was done:** a clinic's partner booking system is used only while the customer's market still allows it, not only when it was switched on (DEV-224). After the pack is suspended, expires or loses its signature, asking the partner is refused (403 `JURISDICTION_INACTIVE`) and staff may confirm by hand: the same alternate path as when the system is down (DEV-221). One rule decides both (`bookingflow.partner_decides`), and `staff/booking-system` returns it so the Appointments page shows "Ask the booking system" or "Confirm by hand (booking system unavailable)" accordingly.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_partner_pack_gate.py`; 31 passed across the gate, booking-flow and circuit files; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the staff app type-checks. T-46 stays **partly** (no certification stages).

### DEV-300: the patient agrees, order by order, to tell a partner their name and birth date (LEAD DECISION, GHE-13 T-57, GHE-05)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** a partner may learn the minimum about a patient (name and birth date only), only after the patient agrees for that one order; the agreement is per order and expires; and the patient can take it back. Until now the exchange held references only (DEV-163) and a partner could never learn who a patient was.
- **What was done:** each request starts with the agreement **pending**. The patient (or an approved guardian) sees it in **My Tests & Requests** and chooses *I agree to share my name and date of birth*, *No, cancel this request*, or later *Take my agreement back*.
  - **Agree:** the patient's name and birth date are read from their own profile at that moment and kept on the request **encrypted** (bound to this request, so they cannot be moved to another row). The partner then sees only those two values, marked as shared with the patient's agreement for this request only. It lasts **30 days**; after that the partner sees nothing and cannot continue until the patient agrees again.
  - **No:** nothing is shared and the request is cancelled. It is not reversed quietly.
  - **Take back:** what was shared is erased at once, and work the partner has not finished is cancelled.
  - **Until the patient agrees,** the partner sees the request but is told nothing about the patient, and the server refuses Accept, Start and Deliver (Decline is still allowed). The patient needs a name on their profile before agreeing.
  - The clinic sees the agreement state on each request (waiting, said no, took it back). Each answer is a step in the request's history (side: patient) and is audited in the patient's customer chain and the platform chain. Staff, doctors and partners cannot answer for the patient.
- **Not changed:** the clinic's patient reference is still never shown to the partner, and nothing clinical (notes, results) travels yet. Results still stay with the partner.
- **Needs confirming:** the 30 day length, the wording shown to the patient, and whether a guardian may agree on a dependant's behalf (it follows the existing proxy rules today).
- **Not built:** sharing anything beyond name and birth date, a reminder to a patient whose agreement is pending. Clearing the shared name when it expires is done in DEV-301.
- **Checked:** 9 new tests and 3 changed in `test_exchange.py` (nothing shared and no progress without agreement; only name and birth date, only for that order; stored encrypted; decline; withdraw; 30 day expiry and re-agree; only the patient can answer; a name must be on file; both sides see the state); with the identifier, contract, OpenAPI and attention tests 114 passed; the patient, partner and staff apps type-check; browser robot 21 passes through all three apps. New migration `exchange/0004_request_consent`; the new field is catalogued in `foundation/identifiers.py`; the OpenAPI snapshot and consumer contract are refreshed.

### DEV-301: a shared name is erased when its agreement runs out (follows DEV-300)

- **What was done:** an expired agreement already hid the patient's name from the partner at once (DEV-300). Now the worker also erases the stored, encrypted copy: once an hour it looks in the platform space for agreements past their 30 days and blanks the name and birth date, recording an `identity_erased` step in the request's history and in the audit chain. Running it again does nothing. An agreement that is still valid is untouched, and the patient can agree again afterwards.
- **Needs confirming:** the hourly timing. Finished or cancelled requests are not erased early; only expiry or the patient taking the agreement back does it.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_exchange.py` (an expired one is erased and a fresh one kept, the second run finds nothing, the history shows the step); mypy, ruff and import-linter clean.

### DEV-302: the patient is told when a request is waiting for their agreement (follows DEV-300)

- **What was done:** when a clinic sends a request, a new event `exchange.consent_needed.v1` (references only, in the event catalogue) tells the patient: a notification "A request needs your agreement", and a short email if they asked for emails. It is worded from the event alone: only the kind of thing (test or scan, pharmacy request, home-care request), never the partner's name, an order number or what was asked. It points them to My Tests & Requests. The worker running again does not tell them twice.
- **Needs confirming:** the wording, and that a patient who agrees at once (the clinic and patient in the same room) still gets the notice. No second reminder is sent if they ignore it.
- **Not built:** a repeat reminder after some days, and a reminder by text message.
- **Checked:** 1 new test and 1 changed in `test_exchange.py`; with the notifications, event-catalogue and pagination tests 81 passed; mypy, ruff and import-linter clean; the event snapshot is refreshed.

### DEV-303: a held partner request is escalated to the administrator after 3 days (LEAD DECISION, GHE-13 T-39, T-55)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** after 3 days the clinic administrator is told, and becomes responsible, if the sender has not acted.
- **What was done:** once an hour the worker looks for requests that have been on hold (DEV-169) for 3 days and are still open, and escalates each one once. The administrator's home screen shows a separate number, "held partner requests that have waited over 3 days: please decide", which other staff do not see; the request list says the administrator has been asked to decide. The sender can still cancel it. A request that is released or cancelled stops counting. Each escalation is a `hold_escalated` step in the request's history and is audited.
- **Needs confirming:** the hour-by-hour check, and that the administrator is told by the home screen number only (no email or text yet).
- **Not built:** a message to the administrator, assigning it to a named administrator, and a second escalation after a longer wait.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_exchange.py` (too early does nothing, escalated after 3 days once only, only the administrator's number changes, history, release clears it); with the attention, identifier, contract and OpenAPI tests 118 passed; mypy, ruff and import-linter clean. New migration `exchange/0005_hold_escalation`.

### DEV-230: a partner's interface is certified and put live by three different people before any clinic may use it (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-47, T-48)

- **What was done:** each partner system's interface goes sandbox, certified, live (`modules/connector/certification.py`, migration `connector 0003`, kept in Zoiko's platform space like country packs). A support officer (R11) enrols it; a safety officer (R12) who did not enrol it certifies it; a support officer who neither enrolled nor certified it puts it live. Either kind may suspend it with a reason, and every step is audited. A certification belongs to one **profile version** of the interface: when the adapter reports a version nobody certified, the partner counts as not live (T-48) and the new version starts again in the sandbox. A clinic can choose a real partner only when it is live and its country pack allows partner bookings (DEV-224), and is asked only while both still hold; otherwise staff confirm by hand (DEV-229). The pretend partner needs no certificate. Endpoints: `platform/connectors` and `platform/connectors/<key>/enrol|certify|live|suspend`; the platform app has a "Partner systems" page.
- **LEAD DECISION (17 Nov 2026):** the lead confirmed the rule as built: three different people (support enrols, a different safety officer certifies, a third support officer puts it live). Still open: an approval is a record, not a cryptographic signature.
- **Not built:** an automated compatibility test before certifying; detecting a change of the partner's authentication; a per-customer or per-contract certification.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_connector_certification.py`; the pack-gate tests now certify their partner; 62 passed across the certification, pack-gate, booking-flow and identifier files; OpenAPI snapshot and consumer contract refreshed; ruff, mypy and import rules clean; the platform app type-checks. T-47 and T-48 move to **partly**.

### DEV-304: consultation notes, encrypted, signed and never edited (LEAD DECISION, GHE-05, GHE-09; supersedes DEV-041 on whether Zoiko holds note text)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** Zoiko may hold the text of a clinical note, encrypted, in the clinic's own space; a signed note is never edited and a correction is a new version that replaces it; the first slice is consultation notes. This replaces the earlier position (DEV-041, decision 3) that Zoiko would keep only pointers and no text. DEV-041's pointers still exist and are unchanged.
- **What was done:** a new module `modules/clinicalnote` (migration with row-level security). A doctor with a care relationship to the patient writes a **draft** for a consultation day, may edit it, and **signs** it. From then on it cannot change. To correct a signed note the author **starts a correction**: a new draft, one version higher, with the old text copied in and a fixed reason (entered in error, clarification, new information). When the correction is signed the original becomes *replaced* in the same step; its text is kept exactly as signed. Only one correction can be open at a time, and a draft or an already-replaced note cannot be corrected.
  - **Encrypted:** the text is stored only as ciphertext, bound to that note so it cannot be moved to another row. Lists show everything except the text.
  - **Who:** the policy gets a new resource class `note` (read, write, sign) for doctors (R03) through a care relationship only. Patients, clinic staff, administrators and every partner are refused; the employer and Zoiko support are hard-blocked. A doctor who stops treating the patient loses access at once. A colleague with a care relationship may read a signed note but not change it, and a **draft is its author's alone**.
  - **Recorded:** every draft, edit, signature, correction start and **every read** is audited, with no text in the trail.
  - **Doctor app:** the Notes screen (patient, notes list, open, write, edit, sign, correct). The "Notes & results" placeholder is replaced.
  - **Endpoints:** `GET/POST /clinician/notes`, `GET /clinician/notes/<id>`, `POST /clinician/notes/<id>/edit|sign|amend`.
- **Switch:** it is **off** unless `ZOIKO_CLINICAL_NOTES=true` is set, like the clinical pointers; with it off every route answers not-found. Turn it on only where the legal and clinical-safety reviews are done.
- **Needs confirming:** 20,000 characters as the longest note; that only the author may correct a note; the reason list; and how long notes are kept (no retention rule yet).
- **Not built:** patient access to their own notes (a separate decision), orders, results for review, structured fields (diagnosis codes, medicines), attachments, an addendum that does not replace the note, and a clinic administrator's break-glass read.
- **Checked:** `test_clinical_notes.py` (16 tests: switch off, patient and staff refused, no relationship refused, access lost when the relationship ends, list never shows text, stored only as ciphertext, bad input, draft private to its author, signed never edited and signing twice harmless, colleague reads but cannot change, correction as new version with the original kept, one open correction and fixed reasons, draft and replaced notes cannot be corrected, every read audited without text); with the policy, identifier, contract and OpenAPI tests 95 passed; ruff, mypy and import-linter clean; the doctor app type-checks. Browser check not run (needs the switch on in the running backend).

### DEV-305: a doctor places an order from the doctor app (follows DEV-163 and DEV-300)

- **What was done:** the doctor app's *Partner results* page is now **Orders & results**. A doctor picks one of their own patients, a laboratory, pharmacy or home-care service, what is asked (a short plain description), and the urgency, and sends it. It uses the exchange that already existed (the server refuses a patient the doctor is not treating), and an order number is made for them. After sending, the screen says the patient has been asked to agree to share their name and that nothing can start until they do (DEV-300, DEV-302). The page still lists each order and where it stands, and still lets the doctor mark a delivered result as reviewed.
- **Not built:** structured orders (test codes, medicines and doses), a result summary the doctor can open, and order sets.
- **Checked:** no backend change. Browser robot 23 (a doctor places an order and sees it waiting) and robot 21 (the whole journey, now through the renamed page) pass; the doctor app type-checks; the consumer contract is refreshed.

### DEV-306: a patient reads their own signed notes, 24 hours after signing (LEAD DECISION, follows DEV-304)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** yes, signed notes only, after 24 hours.
- **What was done:** `GET /my/notes` (the list, no text) and `GET /my/notes/<id>` (one note with its text) for the patient's own record. A **draft is never shown**; a signed note appears **24 hours after it was signed**, so the doctor can follow up first. A version that was later corrected is shown too, marked *corrected*, once its own 24 hours have passed. Every read by the patient is recorded (`clinicalnote.read_by_patient`). The policy gives patients (R01) read of their own notes only; **guardians get none** (the "records" part of a guardian's grant is still not granted), and a doctor or staff login using the patient route sees nothing. Behind the same switch as DEV-304.
- **Needs confirming:** the 24 hours, and whether a guardian should ever see a child's notes.
- **Patient app:** a **My Notes** page (list, read, "corrected later" mark); it type-checks, passes the 320 pixel accessibility sweep, and says "not available yet" while the switch is off.
- **Not built:** a notice to the patient that a note is available, and a patient's request to correct a note.
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `test_clinical_notes.py` (draft and the first 24 hours hidden, visible afterwards, corrected shown, read recorded, other patient and doctor refused, off with the switch); the file's 20 tests passed; ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-307: the lab delivers a short summary and a rating; a treating doctor can read it (LEAD DECISION, GHE-13 T-57, T-59)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** a short text summary from the lab with a rating (normal, abnormal, critical); a critical result always stands out to the ordering doctor; stored encrypted like a note.
- **What was done:** when a partner delivers a result it may add a summary (up to 2,000 characters) and **must** give a rating with it (a rating alone is allowed). The summary is stored **encrypted**; every list shows only the rating and whether a summary exists. A **treating doctor of that patient** opens it from the doctor app's Orders & results ("Read the lab's summary"); clinic staff, other doctors, the patient and the partner are refused, and every read is recorded in the clinic's trail and the platform's, never with the text. A **critical** rating shows as a red "CRITICAL RESULT" on the doctor's list. New route `GET /exchange/requests/<id>/result`; migration `exchange/0006_result_summary`; the lab's delivery form has a summary box and a rating.
- **Needs confirming:** 2,000 characters; that the text is held in the platform space, encrypted with the platform key (the lab never sees the patient's name unless they agreed, DEV-300).
- **Not built:** an email or text alert for a critical result (today it is the red mark on the doctor's page), the patient seeing results, structured values, attachments.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (a treating doctor reads it; staff, other doctor, patient and partner refused; stored only as ciphertext and absent from every other view; rating required and length checked; reads recorded; nothing to open before delivery); browser robot 21 now includes a critical summary read by the doctor; partner and doctor apps type-check.

### DEV-308: claims from a clinic to a payer, showing the payer only member id, service, amount and date (LEAD DECISION, GHE-13)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** the payer sees the member id it gave us, the service, the amount and the date, and nothing else: no diagnosis, no notes, no patient name.
- **What was done:** a new module `modules/claim` in Zoiko's platform space (migration `claim/0001`, row-level security), the same broker pattern as the exchange (DEV-163). Clinic billing staff send a claim for a patient the clinic has seen: payer, the payer's member id, the kind of service as the payer's plans list it, a short plain description, the amount (in the smallest unit), the currency and the service date. The clinic keeps its own patient link on the row; **the payer's view has no patient field**. The member id is stored **sealed** (encrypted) and only its last four characters show on the clinic's side. A clinic can withdraw a claim until the payer decides. The payer can **check cover** against its own plans and members (the existing cover check, with a reason), then **approve**, **decline with one of five fixed reasons** (not covered, member not found, duplicate, information missing, other; no free text) or **mark paid** with a payment reference. A second open claim for the same patient, service and day is refused. Repeating a step is harmless; skipping ahead is a conflict. Each step is on the claim's history (a side, not a name) and is audited in both parties' trails.
  - **Who:** only billing staff and administrators of the clinic (doctors and patients are refused); only payer logins (R07) on the payer side; another payer, a partner or another clinic gets "not found". New routes under `/claims/*`; the staff app has a Claims page, the payer app has a Claims page.
- **Needs confirming:** **whether the patient must agree before a claim is sent** (you decided what the payer sees, not whether the patient consents; the member id came from the patient's insurance); the amount limit (1,000,000.00); that the member id may sit in the platform space (sealed).
- **Not built:** attachments, remittance advice, partial approval, resubmitting a declined claim as a correction, payer-side bulk handling, and a claim total on the payer's home screen.
- **Checked:** `test_claims.py` (31 tests: what each side sees, sealed member id, approve then pay, fixed decline reasons, payment reference, cover check, withdraw, duplicates, 15 bad inputs, unknown payer and patient, who may do what, other payer and clinic see nothing, history); with the identifier, contract, policy, OpenAPI and event tests all passing; browser robot 24 (clinic sends, payer approves and pays, clinic sees it paid); the staff and payer apps type-check; the OpenAPI snapshot and consumer contract are refreshed.

### DEV-309: an addendum on a signed note, without replacing it (follows DEV-304)

- **What was done:** the author of a signed note can add a short dated text (up to 5,000 characters) to it **without** replacing it. The note keeps its text and version; the addendum is stored **encrypted**, can never be edited or deleted, and several can be added (shown oldest first). Only the author, only on the **current signed** version (not a draft, not a replaced one). A doctor sees the addenda when opening the note; a patient sees an addendum 24 hours after it was added (the same delay as notes). Each addition is recorded. New route step `POST /clinician/notes/<id>/addendum`; migration `clinicalnote/0002_note_addendum` (row-level security).
- **Needs confirming:** 5,000 characters; that only the author may add one.
- **Not built:** the doctor-app and patient-app screens for addenda (the data is returned with each note), and a notice to the patient.
- **Checked:** 6 new tests in `test_clinical_notes.py` (author adds, note untouched; stored encrypted and absent from the list; only the author and only the current signed note; bad input refused; a patient sees it a day later; recorded); the file's 26 tests passed; mypy clean.

### DEV-310: email alerts for a critical result and for a request held too long (LEAD DECISION, GHE-13 T-59)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** a critical result always alerts the ordering doctor; the clinic administrator is told when a held request escalates. The first build is **email**.
- **What was done:** when a partner delivers a result rated **critical**, the person who placed the order gets one email (to an address they have **confirmed**; none if they have not). Once an hour a worker finds clinics with a partner request held for more than 3 days (DEV-303) and emails the clinic's administrators (R13), at most once a day per clinic. **The emails hold no patient, no result and no partner**: only that something needs a person, and links to the apps. A failure to send never undoes a delivery (it is logged by class only). A repeated delivery does not send a second email. New: `comms/exchange_alerts.py`, `trust.verified_login_email_of_actor`, `exchange.critical_target`; deliveries now report whether they changed anything.
- **Needs confirming:** that the alert goes to the person who placed the order (usually a doctor; if clinic staff placed it, they get it); the once-a-day limit for administrators.
- **Not built:** **text messages** (the text sending is being changed in another piece of work, so I left it alone), an alert to a covering doctor when the orderer is away, and a reminder if a critical result stays unreviewed.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (critical emails the orderer once and names nothing; none for a normal result or an unconfirmed address; administrator emailed once a day only after escalation); with the related exchange tests 10 passed; ruff, mypy and import-linter clean.

### DEV-311: a patient agrees to each claim before the payer sees it (LEAD DECISION, follows DEV-308)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** yes, per claim, like orders.
- **What was done:** a claim now starts as *waiting for the patient*. **The payer sees nothing of it, and every payer step, including the cover check and the history, answers "not found", until the patient agrees.** The patient (or a guardian acting for them) sees each claim in the patient app's **My Claims**: the payer, the service, the amount and the date (what the payer would be told), never the member number. They **agree**, **decline** (the claim is cancelled, and the clinic may ask again) or **take their agreement back** (the claim is cancelled unless the payer has already decided it; after that the server refuses). The clinic sees each claim's agreement state. Each answer is a step in the claim's history (side: patient) and is audited. New routes `GET /claims/mine`, `POST /claims/mine/<id>/agree|decline|withdraw`; migration `claim/0002_claim_consent`.
- **Needs confirming:** that an agreement has no expiry (a claim is short-lived); whether the patient should also be notified when a claim needs them (not built).
- **Not built:** a notice to the patient that a claim is waiting for them.
- **Checked:** 5 new tests and a helper change in `test_claims.py` (the payer sees nothing until the patient agrees; the patient sees what would be sent, never the member id; declining cancels; withdrawing before and after a decision; only the patient can answer); 35 of 36 passed, the one failure was a stale expectation of the history (now corrected, not yet re-run); the patient app type-checks.

### DEV-312: an employer sees monthly visit totals for people who join with a one-time code (LEAD DECISION, GHE-15)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** counts per programme only, groups of 10 or more, visits and bookings by month for its enrolled people; people are linked by a one-time code from the employer.
- **What was done:** a new module `modules/programme` in Zoiko's platform space (migration `programme/0001`, row-level security). The employer makes a **one-time code** for an enrolled member (a reference of its own; shown **once**; only a keyed fingerprint is stored; valid 30 days; a new code replaces an unused one). The patient types it into the patient app (**Employer Programme**) and **agrees to be counted**; they can leave at any time, and the employer can revoke. **Every wrong, used, expired or revoked code gets the same "not found"**, so a code cannot be guessed. The employer sees **only totals by month**: appointments booked and appointments attended, and **a month is shown only when at least the minimum group (10 by default) of different people contributed**; otherwise it is hidden, never a small number. The employer never sees a person, a clinic, a service or the code. The platform counts bookings in each clinic's own space for the linked patients at the moment the employer asks; nothing per person is stored.
- **TEMPORARY:** the spec names the employer portal but gives no design; the one-time code, the 30 days and counting on request are ours.
- **Needs confirming:** that a month needs 10 different people (not 10 appointments); no minimum on the total linked shown (it is hidden below 10); how often an employer may ask.
- **Not built:** counts by kind of service (declined for now), a limit on how many times a code can be tried, and a monthly email.
- **Checked:** `test_programme.py` (13 tests: one-time code and fingerprint only, any bad code gets the same answer, used once, no double link, expiry, leave and revoke, totals hidden below the group size and shown at it, visits count only attended, leaving stops counting, only linked patients count, who may do what, another employer sees nothing); the partner (employer) and patient apps type-check.

### DEV-313: the patient is told when a claim needs their agreement (follows DEV-311)

- **What was done:** when a clinic sends a claim, a new event `claim.consent_needed.v1` (references only, in the event catalogue) tells the patient: a notification "A claim needs your agreement" and a short email if they asked for emails. It is worded from the event alone: **no insurer, no amount and no service**. It points them to My Claims. The worker running again does not tell them twice.
- **Not built:** a reminder if the patient ignores it, and a notice when a note or an addendum becomes readable.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_claims.py` (told once, names nothing); lint clean; the combined run that includes the event snapshot refresh follows.

### DEV-314: the two claim lists page with a cursor that re-checks rights (follows DEV-171)

- **What was done:** `GET /claims/sent` (the clinic's claims) and `GET /claims/incoming` (the payer's) accept `?limit=&cursor=` (limit 1 to 100) and return `next_cursor`, the same signed cursor as DEV-171: only a position, tied to one login and one list, expiring after 15 minutes, with rights checked afresh on every page. The payer's list pages only claims the patient has agreed to. Without the parameters the lists behave as before. T-07 stays **partly**: other lists are still capped.
