- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_claims.py` (every claim once and in order on both lists; the old shape unchanged; a cursor from one list refused on the other; a bad limit or forged cursor refused; only agreed claims paged); the whole claims file passed (39 tests with the DEV-313 notice test); mypy clean on the claim, comms and views code.

### DEV-315: a strict FHIR R4 checker, the first step of the FHIR plan (TEMPORARY, GHE-13 T-25; see docs/FHIR-DESIGN.md)

- **Decided by the project lead (17 Nov 2026):** FHIR only, one pinned version, one country first. This is step 1 of `docs/FHIR-DESIGN.md`.
- **What was done:** `foundation/fhir.py`, a pure checker (no database, no network) for the two resources the first slice uses, **ServiceRequest** and **DiagnosticReport**, pinned to **R4 (4.0.1)**. It checks the base specification only: required elements, the fixed value sets (status, intent, priority, report status), relative references like `Patient/abc` only (no web address, no display name), codes that always carry both a system and a code, complete dates with a time zone, a report that answers exactly one ServiceRequest, short identifiers, plain-text conclusions of at most 2,000 characters, and size and nesting limits. It is **narrower than the standard on purpose**: an element this slice does not use is refused, not ignored, and so are the places personal data could hide (`contained`, the narrative `text`, `extension`, `meta`). It reports every problem it finds, never only the first.
- **TEMPORARY:** no **country profile** is applied, because the country is not chosen yet (the design note asks). Nothing is stored, received or sent yet: no quarantine table, no endpoint, no mapping to the exchange.
- **Not built:** steps 2 to 5 of the design (quarantine with its staff screen, receiving a report as "result available", sending a request, code mapping, corrections). T-25 therefore stays **not built**.
- **Checked:** `test_fhir_validator.py` (28 tests: good resources accepted, required elements, fixed value sets, hiding places refused, unknown elements refused, reference rules, code rules, dates, one answered request, conclusion limits, identifier limits, wrong kinds of input, size and depth, all problems reported); ruff, mypy and import-linter clean.

### DEV-202: who is on call for an incident, escalation when nobody answers, and the restoration notice (TEMPORARY, GHE-18 G-01, G-10)

- **History:** started by an earlier session on 17 Nov (the file `modules/trust/oncall.py` only, with no fields, migration or wiring, so it failed the type check). Finished at the lead's request the same day.
- **What was done:** an incident gets an **owner** and a **deputy**, two different support or safety staff, named by a support officer (re-naming starts the clock again). The owner or the deputy acknowledges it, once. A serious open incident (SEV-0, SEV-1, unknown severity, or touching patient safety) that nobody acknowledges within 15 minutes is escalated to the deputy **once** by the worker (every minute): a timeline entry and a short email with no incident details, sent only to a verified address. Every step (recorded, assigned, acknowledged, escalated, assessed, hand-off, resolved) is written to the incident's **timeline**, which the database refuses to change (`trust_incidentevent`, append-only, migration `trust 0027`). A SEV-0 or SEV-1 incident cannot be closed without a **restoration notice**: what customers and partners were told (G-10). The platform Incidents page names who is on call, has "I have it", shows the timeline, and asks for the notice when closing a serious incident.
- **TEMPORARY (please confirm):** the 15 minutes; one escalation, to the deputy only; support names the on-call pair.
- **Not built:** an on-call rota, paging or phone calls; sending the restoration notice or a public status page.
- **Checked:** 8 new tests in `test_incident_oncall.py`; 1 updated in `test_incidents.py`; 134 passed across the on-call, incidents, database-grants and identifier files; ruff, mypy, import rules and migrations clean; the platform app type-checks. G-01 and G-10 move to **partly**.

### DEV-316: lead decisions of 18 Nov 2026 (scope confirmed)

- **What was decided:** the team lead confirmed: first market, buyer and journey = **one UK clinic, book and attend** (DEV-011); Zoiko's role = **coordination platform** (DEV-012); hosting = **GCP with managed PostgreSQL in the market region and managed KMS**, recovery copy held by Security/CTO (DEV-013); clinical notes **stay switched off until a clinical-safety review** (DEV-041); FHIR first country profile = **UK Core**, pinned to R4 (`docs/FHIR-DESIGN.md`); insurers, claims and billing are **out of the first release**; **all AI features stay off**; doctor verification stays with **one clinic administrator** (DEV-095, temporary).
- **What it unblocks:** UK Core profile checks in the FHIR validator (step 4 of the design), a signed release scope for the first slice, and production setup design for GCP/KMS. These answers do not turn any switch on by themselves.
- **Still needed:** named approvers and reviewers, a real contracted partner and provider contracts, retention periods and key-rotation rules, and clinical-safety sign-off before notes can be enabled.

### DEV-317: the "critical results" tripwire becomes the real rule (follows DEV-307)

- **What was done:** `tests/test_prohibitions.py` test 3 was a tripwire that failed as soon as any route about results existed, so that the real rule would be written then. Results now exist (DEV-307), so it now states the rule: the only route that mentions a result is `GET /exchange/requests/<id>/result`, which only **opens** the lab's summary. A new test in `test_exchange.py` proves that a delivered message, a **critical** rating and opening the summary (twice) never mark a result reviewed; clinic staff, another doctor, the patient and the partner are refused the review step; only a treating doctor's own review changes it. Any new route about results or critical findings fails test 3 until it is added on purpose.
- **Checked:** the prohibition, identifier, exchange and notes tests re-run: 41 passed. The final full backend suite before this change: 2,924 passed, 2 skipped, 2 failed (this tripwire and the `claim_ref` identifier, both now fixed).

### DEV-319: UK Core checks in the FHIR checker (LEAD DECISION, follows DEV-315 and DEV-316)

- **Decided by the lead (18 Nov 2026, DEV-316):** the first country profile is UK Core, pinned to FHIR R4 (4.0.1).
- **What was done:** `foundation.fhir.validate(resource, profile="uk-core")` adds the UK Core rules **we are sure of** on top of the base checks: the request or report code must include a **SNOMED CT** coding (`http://snomed.info/sct`); every SNOMED CT code, including a report's conclusion codes, must be a real-looking **concept identifier** (6 to 18 digits, no leading zero, the **Verhoeff** check digit, partition 00 or 10); and **an NHS number is never accepted anywhere in a message**, whether as an identifier with the NHS number system or written in any text (Zoiko's own rule, DEV-222: a partner learns who a patient is only through the patient's agreement, DEV-300). Without the profile the base check is unchanged; an unknown profile is refused. The UK Core profile addresses are recorded for later use.
- **TEMPORARY:** these are not the whole UK Core profile. The full rules (cardinalities, value sets, the UK Core Patient and Organization references) must be checked against the **published UK Core package** before anything relies on them; nothing here downloads or reads it.
- **Not built:** the intake (`modules/integration/fhir.py`, another piece of work) still calls the base check only; switching it to `profile="uk-core"` is one line but changes what it accepts, so it is left to that work's owner with its own tests. Code mapping and the review queue for unmapped codes (design step 4) are not built.
- **Checked:** 13 new tests in `test_fhir_validator.py` (41 in all): SNOMED-coded resources accepted; LOINC-only accepted by the base check but refused by UK Core; bad SNOMED identifiers refused (wrong check digit, leading zero, too short, a description id); the check digit verified on known SNOMED CT concepts; conclusion codes checked; an NHS number refused by system and in free text; an unknown profile refused. ruff, mypy and import-linter clean.

### DEV-320: the notes list and the employer's code list page too (follows DEV-171)

- **What was done:** `GET /clinician/notes?patient=` and `GET /programme/codes` accept `?limit=&cursor=` and return `next_cursor`, with the same signed cursor as DEV-171 (one login, one list, 15 minutes, rights checked on every page). The notes cursor belongs to one patient's list and one doctor: another doctor cannot reuse it, and another doctor's draft is never in any page. Without the parameters both lists behave as before (the notes list had no cap at all; the code list was capped at 500). T-07 stays **partly**: lists in modules other than mine still return a fixed number.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_clinical_notes.py` and 1 in `test_programme.py`; both files passed (41 tests); mypy clean.

### DEV-321: the live FHIR intake applies the UK Core checks (LEAD DECISION, follows DEV-319)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** switch it on now.
- **What was done:** the intake (`modules/integration/fhir.py`) now validates every message with `profile="uk-core"`. A message whose request or report code has **no SNOMED CT code** (for example LOINC only), a SNOMED code that is not a real-looking concept identifier, or an NHS number anywhere, is **held in quarantine** for a person and moves nothing; nothing is lost. The intake's own test payloads now carry a SNOMED CT code next to their LOINC code.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_fhir_intake.py` (a LOINC-only report is held with "code required" and no exchange step is taken); the FHIR intake, API and validator tests: 55 passed.

### DEV-322: partners' local codes are mapped to SNOMED CT by two people, never guessed (LEAD DECISION, GHE-13 T-29; FHIR design step 4)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** Zoiko decides, two people: regional support proposes, a different person, the clinical safety officer, approves.
- **What was done:** a new module `modules/codemap` in Zoiko's platform space (migration `codemap/0001`, row-level security). After a signed FHIR message is taken in (accepted or quarantined), every **local** code it carries next to SNOMED CT (in the request or report code and the conclusion codes) is listed **once**, counting repeats, as *needs review*; a refused message lists nothing, and listing can never break the intake or change the message. Regional support (R11) **proposes** the SNOMED CT concept a code means (checked as a real-looking concept id); a **different** person, the clinical safety officer (R12), **approves** it, or **rejects** it with a fixed reason (no equivalent, wrong match, other), which sends it back for a new proposal. Nobody decides their own proposal. `codemap.lookup` answers only with an **approved** match, never a proposal or a guess. The platform app has a **Code mapping** page. Routes: `GET /platform/codemaps`, `POST /platform/codemaps/<id>/propose|approve|reject`.
- **Not built:** using an approved mapping inside the intake (adding the SNOMED CT code to a stored message), and a count of codes waiting on the platform home screen.
- **Checked:** `test_codemap.py` (9 tests: listed once and SNOMED not listed; support proposes and a different safety officer approves; only support proposes and only a safety officer decides; nobody decides their own proposal; proposals must be real SNOMED ids; reject and re-propose with fixed reasons; reject with no proposal; Zoiko staff only; a taken-in message lists its codes and a refused one nothing); with identifiers and contracts 58 passed; OpenAPI, contract, event, prohibition and FHIR tests 36 passed (snapshot refreshed); the platform app type-checks and its every-page robot passes.

### DEV-323: a coded order goes to the lab's own system when the patient agrees (LEAD DECISION, FHIR design step 3)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** the order is sent when the patient agrees; the lab chooses its order system in the partner app.
- **What was done:**
  - **The lab's choice:** `GET/POST /portal/order-system` lets a lab, pharmacy or home-care partner choose the system that receives its orders, only among systems Zoiko has certified and put live (DEV-230); the pretend system only in development; "" means the partner app only. The partner app's Incoming requests page has the choice.
  - **A coded order:** a clinic (the doctor app's order form) may give a **SNOMED CT** code with an order; a bad code is refused. Only a coded order can go to a lab's system; an uncoded one stays in the partner app. Migration `exchange/0007_order_code`.
  - **Sent on agreement:** when the patient agrees (DEV-300), a coded order to a lab that has chosen a live system is queued on the partner connector (`modules/connector`), which already handles retries, unknown outcomes and the circuit breaker. **The patient's own "I agree" is the authority**: it is the receipt, so the audit and the operation say the patient asked. Asking twice sends once (one key per request and agreement). The queued request is **references only** (request, order number, SNOMED code, kind, urgency, agreement time): no name, birth date or patient reference. The FHIR `ServiceRequest` is built from these at send time by `foundation.fhir.service_request`, which always passes the UK Core checks and names nobody (the subject is a per-order pseudonym). Nothing is sent without a code, a chosen live system or an agreement, and a failure never undoes the agreement.
  - **Connector:** `request_operation` takes one new, backward-compatible flag, `receipt_without_organization`, so a patient's own decision (made for the patient, not at an organisation) is checked exactly; agreed with the connector's owner.
  - **Fix found on the way:** the FHIR intake imported the exchange's tables directly, which broke the import contract; it now uses `exchange.delivery_lookup`. **Open point:** that lookup ran in the clinic's space and could not work under row-level security. **Fixed in DEV-326.**
- **Not built:** a real FHIR adapter for a named lab (only the pretend one exists), the patient's name and birth date in FHIR (a Patient resource), corrections and versions (step 5).
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `test_exchange.py` plus the order-system test (119 exchange, connector and booking-flow tests passed); 2 builder tests in `test_fhir_validator.py`; intake tests 14 passed; OpenAPI, contract, identifier, contract-migration and prohibition tests 63 passed (snapshot refreshed); import contracts kept; mypy clean; the doctor and partner apps type-check; browser robots 21 and 23 pass.

### DEV-324: how long notes are kept, guardians and children's notes, and the production switch (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** keep notes by the NHS rules; a guardian reads a child's notes only while the child is under 16, and a doctor can keep a single note from the guardian; notes stay off in production until the clinical safety review signs them off.
- **What was done:**
  - **Retention (NHS Records Management Code):** adults, 8 years after the last care; children, until their 25th birthday, or 26th if they were 17 at the last care; whichever is later. It is counted from the patient's **last** note, so recent care keeps every older note. With no known birth date nothing is erased (a child's notes must never go early). A daily worker job (`clinicalnote.retention`) erases the **text** of the note and its addenda; the row stays, so the record that a note existed is kept, and the erasure is audited. The doctor and patient apps say a note was erased. The job runs even with the switch off. Migration `clinicalnote/0003`.
  - **Guardians:** a new part of a guardian's grant, `notes`, separate from `records` (which stays closed, DEV-110). The guardian role (R02) may read notes only with that part, and the patient app shows a child's notes only while the child is **under 16** (checked against the birth date on file; none on file means no). Reads are audited as `read_by_guardian`, and the list says whose note it is. The staff app offers the new part when approving a guardian; the patient app's family page names it.
  - **Keeping one note from a guardian:** the author can tick "Keep this note from a parent or guardian" (`POST clinician/notes/{id}/guardian {hidden}`); the patient still sees it. Audited.
  - **The switch:** on by default in development; in production the app refuses to start with notes on unless `ZOIKO_NOTES_SAFETY_SIGNOFF` names the clinical safety review's sign-off.
- **Not built:** reading notes for a person who has died (the NHS rule of 8 years after death needs a recorded date of death); a review step before erasure (the NHS code expects one for records of long-term value); guardians of adults who lack capacity.
- **Checked:** 7 new tests in `test_clinical_notes.py` (39 with the prohibition tests passed); family, guardian-scope, people and policy tests 86 passed; import contracts kept; mypy clean on a fresh cache; the doctor, patient and staff apps type-check.

### DEV-325: corrections and versions of a lab result (FHIR design step 5, T-30)

- **Context:** the FHIR design says a later report that corrects an earlier one is stored as a new version that supersedes it, and the original stays traceable, the same rule as notes. No lead decision was needed; the clinical choices below follow that rule and are safe defaults the lead may change.
- **What was done:**
  - **A new version, the old one kept:** `exchange.correct_result` replaces the current result (reference, summary, rating) and keeps the one it replaces in a new table, `ExchangeResultVersion`, exactly as delivered, with who delivered it and whether it had been reviewed. It is never changed or deleted. The summary stays encrypted, bound to its request. Migration `exchange/0008_result_versions` (row-level security on).
  - **Review again:** a corrected result goes back to "result available", so a clinician must review the new version even if the old one was reviewed. A correction to **critical** emails the person who ordered it, as a first delivery does (DEV-310).
  - **Why it changed:** a fixed choice, the FHIR DiagnosticReport statuses `corrected`, `amended`, `appended`. The same correction sent twice changes nothing. No new agreement from the patient is needed: a correction to a result already given must always reach the doctor.
  - **Partner app:** "Send a correction" on a delivered request (`POST exchange/incoming/{id}/correct`). **Doctor app:** the result shows its version and why it changed, and each earlier version opens below it.
  - **FHIR intake:** a `DiagnosticReport` with status `corrected`, `amended` or `appended` for a result already delivered becomes a new version; before any delivery it is simply delivered. One with status `cancelled` or `entered-in-error` is **held in quarantine for a person**: a withdrawn result is never applied automatically.
- **Not built:** withdrawing a result (the held message is the signal; a person acts on it with the lab); telling the patient about a correction; the FHIR intake still sends no critical-result email itself (only the partner app's delivery does; this was already so before this change).
- **Checked:** 5 new tests in `test_exchange.py` and 3 in `test_fhir_intake.py`; exchange, contract, identifier, OpenAPI and public-id tests 137 passed (snapshot refreshed); FHIR intake 13 passed; import contracts kept; mypy clean; the doctor and partner apps type-check.

### DEV-326: a lab's FHIR report now really reaches the doctor; critical alerts from FHIR (fix)

- **What was wrong:** the FHIR intake looked the request up with an unscoped query, then tried to change it from inside the clinic's space. The exchange lives in Zoiko's platform space, so its own tenant filter never found the row: **a valid `DiagnosticReport` was held in quarantine as `exchange_conflict` instead of delivered**, and under production row-level security even the lookup would have failed. The unit tests mock the exchange, so they never saw it. This was the open point recorded in DEV-323.
- **What was done:** the intake now does all exchange work inside the platform space (the same way the screens do), and `exchange.delivery_lookup` is scoped to the bound tenant, so it works under row-level security. A quarantine row is still written in the clinic's own space. A critical result arriving by FHIR now emails the person who ordered it, once, as the partner app's delivery does (DEV-310).
- **Checked:** 3 new end-to-end tests in `test_exchange.py` that post a signed FHIR message through the real HTTP route: a report delivers the result and a later `corrected` one versions it; a critical report emails the orderer once, even when sent twice; a report for a request that is not the clinic's is held. They failed before the fix. FHIR intake and FHIR API tests 17 passed; import contracts kept; mypy and ruff clean.


### DEV-327: approved code mappings are used by the FHIR intake (FHIR design step 4, finished)

- **What was done:** before a message is checked against UK Core, a `code` that carries no SNOMED CT code of its own but has a local code with an **approved** mapping (DEV-322: proposed by one Zoiko person, approved by another) gets that SNOMED CT code added. A lab that sends only its own codes is then accepted instead of held for that alone. Only approved mappings count; a proposed or rejected one is never used. The partner's own codes stay in the message. Each use is audited (`integration.fhir_code_mapped`, reason `approved_code_mapping`, no content). A lookup failure never fails the intake; the message is then judged as it came.
- **Not built:** re-running messages already held for a missing SNOMED code once their mapping is approved (staff ask the partner to resend, as today); mapping inside `conclusionCode`.
- **Checked:** 2 new end-to-end tests in `test_exchange.py` (a local LOINC-only report is held, then accepted once its mapping is approved; a proposed mapping is never used); FHIR, exchange and codemap tests 34 passed; import contracts kept; mypy and ruff clean.

### DEV-328: regional support's view of delivery attempts (GHE-13 T-54)

- **What was done:** `GET platform/delivery-attempts`, for Zoiko's own staff only (R11, R12, in the platform space). For every customer in this region (this cell) that has any: counts by state (waiting, will be tried again, gave up, delivered), the attempts not yet delivered with **status only** (event type, state, tries, last answer, next try), and how many FHIR messages are held for the customer's staff (a count). Never the message, its fingerprint, the receiving address, who registered it, or any person. Customers with the most give-ups come first. Each look is recorded in the platform's audit chain. Support cannot resend; only the customer can (as before). The Platform app has a "Delivery attempts" page.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_support_view.py` (the exact fields support sees, nothing of the message or the address; only Zoiko staff may look, and each look is recorded); 4 passed; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and contract tests 28 passed; mypy and ruff clean; the Platform app type-checks.

### DEV-329: a partner developer's sandbox with synthetic patients

- **What was done:** `foundation/synthetic.py` holds five fixed, made-up patients ("synthetic-001" to "-005", names starting "Synthetic Testpatient", birth dates in 1900 or 2099), one per case a developer must try: normal, abnormal and critical results, a correction, and a message the intake holds (local code only). For each, the FHIR order Zoiko would send (built with `fhir.service_request`) and an example report back, and whether the intake would accept or hold it. Nothing is stored or read from the database, so no synthetic record can mix with real ones. `GET portal/sandbox` serves them; `POST portal/sandbox/check` checks a developer's own resource against the intake's rules (FHIR R4 + UK Core) and says accepted or held and why, keeping nothing (64 KB at most). Partner developers (R10) only. The Partner app's developer area has a "Sandbox" page.
- **Not built:** a pretend clinic in which a developer places synthetic orders end to end (the pretend partner covers development); sandbox keys separate from live keys.
- **Checked:** `test_sandbox.py`, 3 tests (every synthetic patient is plainly synthetic and its messages behave as promised; only a partner developer gets the sandbox; the checker says accepted or held and why, and refuses a huge message); OpenAPI and contract tests 61 passed (snapshot refreshed); mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the Partner app type-checks.

### DEV-330: measured values of a lab result, for the treating doctor (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** labs may also send individual values (each with unit and normal range); only the treating doctor sees them, encrypted like the summary; the summary and the rating stay.
- **What was done:**
  - **Exchange:** a delivery or a correction may carry up to 50 values: a name (80 characters), an optional SNOMED CT code, a value (a number, or a text of up to 40 characters), and an optional unit, normal range and flag (normal, high, low, abnormal, critical). Nothing else is accepted, so no free text can hide in them; values always come with the lab's overall rating. They are stored **encrypted** (`result_values_enc`, migration `exchange/0009_result_values`) and returned only by the treating doctor's result read (DEV-307 rule), never in a list (lists say only `has_values`). A correction keeps the earlier version's values with it (DEV-325).
  - **FHIR:** the intake accepts a `Bundle` of type `collection` holding exactly one `DiagnosticReport` and the `Observation`s its `result` lists, every one exactly once and about the same subject. An `Observation` holds only status, a SNOMED CT code (UK Core), a quantity or a short text, up to two normal ranges and an interpretation (N, A, H, L, HH, LL, AA, CRIT map to the flags). A single report with no values works as before.
  - **Screens:** the partner app's delivery and correction forms have a "Measured values" table; the doctor app shows the values as a table with the normal range and flag, and each earlier version's values below it.
- **Not built:** showing values to the patient (the lead chose the doctor only); trends across results; approved code mappings inside a Bundle's entries (only a single report's own code is mapped, DEV-327).
- **Checked:** 3 validator tests, 4 exchange tests (only the treating doctor reads values; bad values refused; stored encrypted and kept with an earlier version; a lab's FHIR Bundle delivers its values end to end); exchange, FHIR, sandbox, OpenAPI, contract, identifier and public-id tests 210 passed before DEV-331, then the FHIR and exchange end-to-end tests again after it; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the doctor and partner apps type-check; browser robots 21 and 23 pass.

### DEV-331: Zoiko ids in FHIR messages (fix)

- **What was wrong:** a Zoiko id is URL-safe base64 and may contain "_", which a FHIR id may not. So, at random, about half of all orders had an id the FHIR rules refuse: the order Zoiko sends to a lab's own system (DEV-323) failed to build (silently, since a send failure never undoes the patient's agreement), and a lab's report pointing at such an order was held. The full exchange test run caught it as a test that failed only sometimes.
- **What was done:** `fhir.to_fhir_id` writes "_" as "." (allowed in FHIR, never in our ids) and `fhir.from_fhir_id` turns it back. The order we build uses it, and the intake turns the report's `basedOn` back into our id. A test converts 300 random ids both ways and checks every order built from them passes UK Core.
- **Partners must know:** in FHIR, our request ids appear with "." in place of "_"; a partner echoes them back unchanged.

### DEV-332: a patient sees a result's values a day after the doctor reviewed it (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** patients see their result values only after a doctor has reviewed the result, plus 24 hours (the same delay as notes); a critical result is never shown before review.
- **What was done:** `GET exchange/mine/{id}/values` returns the measured values (DEV-330) of one of the patient's own results once the CURRENT version is reviewed and 24 hours have passed; before that, or for anyone else, it is not found. A correction sends the result back to review, so its values wait again. The lab's summary text is not shown to the patient (it is written for clinicians). Every read is audited (`exchange.values_read_by_patient`). The patient's list says `values_ready`; the patient app shows "See the measured values" with a table of values, units and usual ranges. A guardian sees none (results are a "records" class, never granted, DEV-110).
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (values only a day after review and never before; a correction waits again; nobody else reads them); 22 related tests passed; the patient app type-checks.

### DEV-333: recording that a patient has died (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** a clinic administrator records the date of death with how the clinic knows; it closes the patient's bookings and reminders and starts the 8-year notes clock; audited, correctable by a second person.
- **What was done:** `POST people/{id}/death {died_on, source}` (clinic administrator, R13, for a patient connected to their site): the date (never in the future, not before 1900) and a fixed source (death certificate, hospital notice, family notice, other) are stored on the patient record (migration `trust/0028_person_death`). The patient's future bookings are cancelled with the new clinic reason "The patient has died", which also stops their reminders. **Nothing more is sent to the patient**: no notification, email or text (a family member might read "your appointment was cancelled"). Recording the same thing twice changes nothing; a different date is refused until it is withdrawn. `POST people/{id}/death/withdraw {reason}` (recorded in error, wrong patient) only by a **different** administrator; cancelled bookings are not restored. Both steps are audited. Notes are kept **8 years after death** (NHS rule), whatever the age. The staff app's patient details have a "Death" card.
- **Not built:** stopping the deceased patient's own login (it still signs in; a guardian's access is unchanged); closing waiting-list entries and open partner requests; telling the treating doctors.
- **Note for the other session:** this adds `trust` migration 0028; if you also add one, the numbers need merging.
- **Checked:** `test_death.py`, 5 tests (an administrator records it and the future booking is cancelled; only an administrator, with valid input; only a second administrator withdraws; the 8-year clock; no notice reaches the family); notification tests still pass (15 with these); mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the staff app type-checks.

### DEV-334: a text to the doctor when a result they ordered is critical (LEAD DECISION: text messages next)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** text messages next. Most of texting already existed (DEV-194 Twilio and approved wording, DEV-198 phone codes, DEV-200 breaker, DEV-228 market switch, the `console` stand-in for development; patients already get reminders and updates by text when they turn texts on). The gap was the critical-result alert, which only emailed.
- **What was done:** a clinician (or other clinic staff) can give their own mobile number (`staff/alert-phone`), prove it with the same 6-digit code flow as patients, and turn on "text me when a result I ordered is critical" (only once proved). The number is stored **encrypted**, compared only by its keyed fingerprint, and shown masked. The critical alert (partner app or FHIR, DEV-310, DEV-326) now also queues a text from a new approved template, "A result needs your attention on Zoiko Access. Please sign in now.", never what the result is or whom it is about, and only where texts are switched on for the market (DEV-228). Migration `comms/0006_alert_phone`. The doctor app's "My account" page has an "Urgent alerts by text" card.
- **Not built:** texts for the 3-day hold escalation to administrators; push notifications; a real provider account (Twilio values are still to be supplied).
- **Checked:** `test_alert_phone.py`, 4 tests (prove the number, then the critical alert texts the fixed wording; no text unproved or unasked; stored encrypted, wrong code refused; patients have no alert number); the existing text, phone-check and text-market tests still pass (45 with these); OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and contract, identifier, public-id and prohibition tests 64 passed; no missing migrations; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the doctor app type-checks.

### DEV-335: a deceased patient's own login stops (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** when a death is recorded, the patient's own login can no longer sign in; reversed if the death is withdrawn. (Waiting lists, open lab requests and telling the doctors were not chosen.)
- **What was done:** sign-in, every signed session and the development sign-in refuse a login that is a patient's OWN login while that patient is recorded as having died (`trust.auth.login_of_deceased`), with the same answer as any failed sign-in (no hint why). Nothing about the login is changed, so withdrawing the death (DEV-333) lets it straight back in. Guardians and staff are unaffected.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_death.py` (signed out after the record, staff unaffected, back after the withdrawal); the sign-in tests still pass (44 with the death tests).

### DEV-336: the 3-day hold escalation is also texted to administrators who asked (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (19 Nov 2026):** text too, with the same fixed wording style, once a day, only to administrators who turned it on.
- **What was done:** a clinic administrator (R13) who proved their own number (DEV-334) can turn on "text me once a day when partner requests have been on hold for more than 3 days". The hourly escalation job then queues one text a day per such administrator, from a new approved template, "Something at your clinic needs a decision on Zoiko Access. Please sign in.", never what or whom; only where texts are switched on for the market. The day last texted is kept (migration `comms/0007_hold_texts`), so a missing email address cannot make it repeat hourly. `trust.actors_with_role` lists who holds a role. The staff app's Settings page has the "Urgent alerts by text" card; the switch shows for administrators only.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_alert_phone.py` (administrators only; once a day; never to one who did not ask; the fixed wording); escalation, text and alert tests 34 passed; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and contract, identifier, alert and death tests 67 passed; no missing migrations; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the staff and doctor apps type-check.

### DEV-337: lists that stopped silently at 200 rows now page (follows DEV-171, DEV-314, DEV-320)

- **Why:** many lists returned only their newest 100 or 200 rows, so older invoices, payments, referrals and held messages simply disappeared from the screen with no sign that more existed.
- **What was done:** one shared helper, `foundation.pagination.PageRequest` with `apply()`, so a list function takes an optional page request. Without one it gives exactly what it gave before; with `?limit=` (1 to 100) and/or `?cursor=` it gives one page, newest first, and `next_cursor` (null at the end). The cursor is the same signed one as DEV-171: tied to the login and the list, expires after 15 minutes, carries no permission. Lists now paging: invoices and payments (staff), webhook deliveries, inbound events and held FHIR messages (staff), the doctor's sent referrals and referrals to answer, a patient's referrals, a patient's own lab requests, claims and insurance cover. The staff Billing page now loads 50 invoices at a time with "Show older invoices".
- **Also (20 Nov):** "Show older" buttons on the staff app's held FHIR messages and webhook deliveries.
- **Not built:** "show more" buttons on the remaining screens (they still ask for the whole list, as before); a patient's requests list filters rows the login may not see after the page is read, so a page can be shorter than the limit.
- **Checked:** `test_paging_lists.py`, 3 tests (held FHIR messages page to the end with nothing twice and old screens still get everything; a cursor works only for its own list and login; webhook deliveries page); the existing billing, payments, claims, coverage, referral, FHIR API, webhook and pagination tests; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the staff app type-checks.

### DEV-338: held messages are tried again once a code is approved; mappings inside a Bundle (FHIR design step 4)

- **What was done:** approved code mappings (DEV-327) now apply to every report and value inside a `Bundle` too, not only a single report's own code. A held FHIR message can be **tried again**: by clinic staff ("Try again" in the staff app, `POST staff/fhir/quarantine/{id}/retry`) or by an hourly job (`integration.fhir_retry`) for messages held over a code. A retry takes the message in exactly as if it had just arrived (mappings, UK Core, matching the order, delivery, critical alert); if it passes, the held row becomes **"taken in after a retry"** (`released`, migration `integration/0006`), audited, with the note "approved code mapping"; if not, it stays held with its problems brought up to date. Nobody has to ask the partner to resend.
- **Also fixed:** local codes from the FHIR intake were never added to the two-person review list (only the older partner route did it); now every FHIR and HL7 message lists its local codes, including those inside a Bundle, but not the standard rating codes (N, A, CRIT).
- **Checked:** 3 end-to-end tests in `test_exchange.py` (the hourly retry takes a message in once its code is approved, once only; staff retry, a still-wrong message stays held, a decided one cannot be retried, reception may not; local codes inside a Bundle are mapped).

### DEV-339: a value over time for the treating doctor

- **What was done:** `GET exchange/requests/{id}/trend?key=` gives one measured value (by SNOMED CT code, or by name when it has none) across the patient's results at this clinic, oldest first, at most 100 points; only the current version of a corrected result counts. The same rule as reading a result: a treating doctor only, every look audited. In the doctor app, a test's name in the values table opens its history: a small line with the normal range shaded, and each date, value, flag and whether it was reviewed.
- **Checked:** 2 tests in `test_exchange.py` (oldest first, by code and by name, a correction replaces its point; only a treating doctor, and the key is required).

### DEV-340: HL7 v2 lab results (ORU^R01)

- **What was done:** `POST /api/v1/hl7` takes a signed HL7 v2 ORU^R01 message (the same signature and delivery id as FHIR). `modules/integration/hl7.py` reads it into the FHIR Bundle the intake already takes (a DiagnosticReport and its Observations, DEV-330), and from there it goes through exactly the same steps: UK Core, approved code mappings, matching the order (OBR-2 or ORC-2, our request id), delivery or held for a person, the critical alert, corrections (OBR-25 C), retries. Nothing about the patient is taken from the message: the **PID segment is ignored** (names, numbers, birth dates) and the report is about a per-order pseudonym. Read: OBR-4 the test, OBX-2 value type (NM a number, otherwise text), OBX-3 test (SCT = SNOMED CT, LN = LOINC, anything else a local code for review), OBX-5 value, OBX-6 unit, OBX-7 range "low-high", OBX-8 flag (HH, LL, AA critical), OBX-11 status, the NTE after OBR as the conclusion; the overall rating is the worst flag; HL7 escapes are read back. An unreadable message, or one that is not ORU^R01, is held with the reason. The developer sandbox's checker also reads HL7 v2.
- **Not built:** other HL7 message types (orders out, ADT); MLLP transport (messages come over HTTPS like FHIR); acknowledgements (ACK) in HL7 form (the answer is the same JSON receipt as FHIR).
- **Checked:** `test_hl7.py`, 4 tests (an ORU becomes a valid UK Core Bundle naming nobody, with values, ranges, flags and the worst rating; a correction and local codes; unreadable messages refused clearly; escapes); 2 end-to-end tests in `test_exchange.py` (a signed HL7 result reaches the doctor with its values, summary and critical email; an unreadable message is held and local codes go for review); 1 in `test_sandbox.py`; the exchange file 96 passed; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed; contract and identifier tests passed; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean.
- **Was open, now found and fixed (DEV-341):** a test of DEV-323 failed once in a combined run; the cause was a real bug, not the test.

### DEV-341: an order to a lab with several sites was sent only some of the time (fix)

- **What was wrong:** when a patient agreed, the order went to the lab's chosen system by looking at the lab's "first" organisation. The database returns a customer's organisations in no fixed order, so for a lab with more than one site (as the test lab has) the order was sent only when the site that made the choice happened to come first; otherwise nothing was sent and nothing said so (a send failure never undoes the agreement). It showed up as a test that failed once in a combined run.
- **What was done:** the choice is now read across **all** the lab's organisations: exactly one chosen system sends the order; two sites that chose **different** systems send nothing rather than guess (logged; the lab still has the order in the partner app). The clinic's own organisation on the queued operation is now picked in a fixed order too. A search found no other place that picks "the first organisation" without an order.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (the order is sent whichever site chose the system; two different choices send nothing and the agreement stands); the order tests passed three times in a row; ruff and mypy clean.

### DEV-342: the UK Core rules checked against the published release (fhir.r4.ukcore.stu2 2.0.2)

- **Why:** DEV-319 applied only the UK Core rules we were sure of and said the full rules must be checked against the published package before anything goes live.
- **What was done:** downloaded the official UK Core package `fhir.r4.ukcore.stu2` version **2.0.2** from the public FHIR package registry (checksum sha1 `c18ff745a590bcac42dcbe0041714570ee433c00`, the same as the registry lists) and read what its lab profiles add to plain FHIR: `UKCoreDiagnosticReportLab` 2.0.0, `UKCoreObservationLab` 2.0.0 and `UKCoreServiceRequest` 2.5.0.
  - **Found and fixed:** both lab profiles **require a category** (a report: v2-0074 `LAB`; a value: observation-category `laboratory`), but our checker refused `category` as an unknown element, so **every message from a lab that follows UK Core would have been held**. `category` is now accepted (1 to 5 codes) on ServiceRequest, DiagnosticReport and Observation, and under UK Core a lab report and each lab value must carry the lab category. Our own builders add it: the HL7 v2 reader (DEV-340) and the sandbox examples (DEV-329).
  - **Confirmed:** `subject` is required on the lab report (we already required it); the other profile changes are "must support" or preferred code lists, not rules that refuse a message.
  - **Recorded as Zoiko's own, not UK Core's:** UK Core only *prefers* SNOMED CT for the report, value and request codes; requiring it is the lead's stricter rule (DEV-321), now said so in the code.
- **Not done:** the package itself is not kept in the repository (it is public and versioned; the version and checksum above are the record); a validator that runs the complete StructureDefinitions (slicing, every invariant) instead of our hand-written subset.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_fhir_validator.py` (a report with the lab category is accepted, without it held under UK Core but not plain FHIR, a radiology category held; each value needs the laboratory category); every hand-built test message now carries the category, as a UK Core lab system's would; FHIR, HL7, sandbox and code-mapping tests 81 passed, and the exchange FHIR tests 65 passed; ruff clean.

### DEV-343: when a death is recorded, waiting lists close and open lab requests stop (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (20 Nov 2026):** also close waiting lists and open lab requests (telling the treating doctors and push notifications were not chosen).
- **What was done:** recording a death (DEV-333) now also closes every open waiting-list entry of the patient (reason "the patient has died", migration `waitlist/0002`), and cancels the clinic's partner requests for them that are not yet delivered, erasing any name already shared with the lab; a delivered result stays as it is. Both are audited. The answer and the staff app's Death card say how many appointments, lab requests and waiting lists were closed. If the lab part cannot run (the platform space unreachable), the death record still stands and the failure is logged.
- **Checked:** 1 new test in `test_death.py` (two open requests cancelled with the shared name erased, the delivered one kept, the waiting-list entry closed "died"); the death tests now run with Zoiko's platform space, as a real system has; 7 passed.

### DEV-344: coded diagnoses on a visit note (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (20 Nov 2026):** diagnoses only, coded (SNOMED CT), encrypted, signed like notes; no prescribing.
- **What was done:** a visit note may carry up to 10 diagnoses, each a valid SNOMED CT concept, its words (up to 120 characters) and a status (confirmed, provisional, ruled out); nothing else is accepted. They are stored **encrypted** with the note (migration `clinicalnote/0004`), changed only while it is a draft (an edit without diagnoses keeps them), signed with it, copied into a correction, erased with the text at the end of retention (DEV-324), and never shown in a list. The doctor app's draft has a "Diagnoses" editor and a signed note shows them; the patient sees them with the note after the same 24 hours (DEV-306), in plain words ("not yet confirmed", "ruled out"). Behind the notes switch like notes. No prescribing (drug safety and e-prescribing rules need their own review).
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_clinical_notes.py` (carried, kept on edit, fixed once signed, copied on correction, shown to the patient; stored encrypted and every bad shape refused); 36 passed.

### DEV-345: the lab's PDF report, for the treating doctor, virus-checked (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (20 Nov 2026):** a lab may attach its PDF report to a result; only the treating doctor opens it; encrypted, size-limited, virus-scanned before anyone opens it.
- **What was done:** `POST exchange/incoming/{id}/attachment?filename=` (the lab that handles the request, on a delivered result): a PDF only, checked by its content (`%PDF-` and `%%EOF`), at most 5 MB (read with that cap, not Django's general one) and 5 per result, and refused if it holds active content (scripts, launch or open actions, embedded files, rich media, XFA). Stored **encrypted** in the platform space with its size and SHA-256, tied to the result version (`ResultAttachment`, migration `exchange/0010`, row-level security on). A **virus scan** (`foundation/scanner.py`) runs at once if a scanner is set up and every minute for files still waiting: ClamAV in production (`ATTACHMENT_SCANNER=clamav`, `CLAMAV_HOST`), a development stand-in that finds the EICAR test file; with no scanner, files wait and can never be opened; an unreachable scanner never counts as clean; an infected file's content is deleted. The treating doctor downloads a clean file only (`GET exchange/requests/{id}/attachments/{att}`, sent as a download, `nosniff`, `no-store`), every opening audited; clinic staff, other doctors and the patient cannot. The partner app has "Attach the PDF report" on a delivered request; the doctor app lists the files with "Download" once checked.
- **Not built:** a ClamAV service in the deployment (only the setting and client); PDFs inside a FHIR DiagnosticReport (`presentedForm`) or HL7 (OBX ED); files on notes; the patient seeing the PDF.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (attach and download by the treating doctor only; a virus is never opened and its content not kept; nothing opens before the scan, then the worker scans it; only a safe PDF of a sensible size on a delivered result, a 3 MB file accepted, over 5 MB and the sixth refused); exchange, notes, death, waiting-list, OpenAPI, contract, identifier and public-id tests 222 passed (snapshot refreshed); no missing migrations; the doctor and partner apps type-check.

### DEV-346: lead decisions of 20 Nov 2026 (scope of the first release)

- **Decided by the project lead (20 Nov 2026):**
  - **Guardians of adults** who cannot decide for themselves: only with legal proof, a registered Lasting Power of Attorney for health and welfare or a court-appointed deputy; the clinic checks the document, a second staff member approves, reviewed every year.
  - **Region:** the UK only for now; the design stays ready for more cells.
  - **Claims:** the payer records payment advice (amount paid, date, its reference) on a paid claim; no documents on claims yet.
  - **Lab PDFs:** the treating doctor only, as now; no files on notes.
  - **Prescribing:** no; doctors prescribe in their own certified system.
  - **Imaging:** reports only, through the same order-and-report flow as labs; no images (DICOM) stored.
  - **AI:** none in the first release (stays off).
  - **Billing:** keep it simple: invoices and credit notes, no tax; payments taken elsewhere and recorded.
  - **Marketplace:** a patient books only with their own clinic; no search across clinics yet.
  - **Online payment by patients:** no; clinics record payments taken elsewhere.
  - **Email provider:** SendGrid.
  - **PDFs inside lab messages** (FHIR `presentedForm`, HL7 embedded PDF): yes, with the same rules and virus check as uploads.
- **What it unblocks:** adult proxies, claim payment advice, imaging reports, the SendGrid adapter and PDFs in lab messages are built next (DEV-347 onwards). Everything else in the list above is a decision NOT to build now.

### DEV-347: payment advice on a paid claim (LEAD DECISION, DEV-346)

- **What was done:** when the payer marks a claim paid it may also say **how much it paid** (in minor units; at most the amount claimed, never negative; the full amount when not given) and **on which day** (not in the future; today when not given), next to its payment reference (migration `claim/0003`). The clinic's claims list shows them, saying when less was paid than claimed. The partner app's pay form has the two fields.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_claims.py` (recorded and seen by the clinic; defaults, and every bad value refused); 41 passed.

### DEV-348: imaging reports through the lab flow (LEAD DECISION, DEV-346)

- **What was done:** a "laboratory or imaging" order already went to the "laboratory or imaging" partner role; but the UK Core check (DEV-342) demanded the LAB category, so an **imaging report (category RAD) would have been held**. Now a report may say LAB or RAD and a value "laboratory" or "imaging". The HL7 v2 reader marks a report as imaging when OBR-24 names an imaging section (RAD, CT, MR, US, NM, MG and kin). No images (DICOM) are taken in.
- **Checked:** the validator test updated (RAD accepted, another category held); 1 new HL7 test; 54 passed.

### DEV-349: the lab's PDF inside its FHIR or HL7 message (LEAD DECISION, DEV-346)

- **What was done:** a DiagnosticReport may carry up to 5 PDFs in `presentedForm` (contentType `application/pdf`, base64 data, an optional title; checked to be a PDF of at most 5 MB); an HL7 OBX of type ED (`^application^pdf^Base64^...`) becomes the same thing instead of a value. After the result is delivered, each PDF goes through **exactly the upload path** (DEV-345): safety checks, encryption, virus scan, treating doctor only. A PDF that is refused does not undo the result; the answer says how many were attached and refused, and a refusal is audited. The message size limit is now 7.5 MB so a 5 MB PDF fits (read with that cap, not Django's general one); the checker still holds the rest of the message to 256 KB, measured without the PDF data.
- **Checked:** 1 new validator test, 2 end-to-end tests (a FHIR report with a 3 MB PDF and an unsafe one: one attached, one refused; an HL7 result with its PDF, not counted as a value); FHIR and HL7 suites 83 passed.

### DEV-350: email through SendGrid (LEAD DECISION, DEV-346)

- **What was done:** `foundation/sendgrid_backend.py`, a Django email backend that sends through SendGrid's web API over HTTPS (not SMTP, so it works from Cloud Run). Switched on with `MAIL_PROVIDER=sendgrid` and `SENDGRID_API_KEY` (from Secret Manager; never logged, only in the Authorization header); `SENDGRID_API_HOST` allows the EU host and must be https. Open and click tracking are switched off (no tracking links in clinical mail). The queue, retries, masking and wiping (foundation/mail.py) are unchanged; a failure raises so the queue tries again. Development keeps the local mail inbox.
- **Not built:** SendGrid delivery receipts (its event webhook).
- **Checked:** `test_sendgrid.py`, 2 tests (the exact request, no tracking, the key only in the header; failures raise, no key or a non-https host refused); with the existing email tests 56 passed.

### DEV-351: the virus scanner and SendGrid in the deployment

- **What was done:** **Google Cloud** (`deploy/gcp/scanner.tf`): ClamAV runs on a small private VM in the cell's own network (Cloud Run cannot serve clamd's TCP protocol): Container-Optimised OS with Shielded VM, no public address, a service account with no permissions beyond logging, and a firewall that lets only the app's subnet reach port 3310. Every container gets `ATTACHMENT_SCANNER=clamav` and `CLAMAV_HOST` (the VM's private address); `MAIL_PROVIDER=sendgrid`, and the `sendgrid-api-key` secret (filled in by a person, attached once `manual_secrets_ready` is true). **Locally:** an optional `clamav` service in docker-compose (`--profile scan`); development otherwise keeps the stand-in scanner.
- **Checked:** `terraform fmt -check` and `terraform validate` (official Terraform 1.9.8 image): "The configuration is valid"; `docker compose config` accepts the compose file.

### DEV-352: an adult's attorney or deputy (LEAD DECISION, DEV-346)

- **What was done:** two new relationships for an ADULT (18 or over) who cannot decide for themselves: **attorney** under a registered Lasting Power of Attorney for health and welfare, and **court-appointed deputy**. The clinic approves only with exactly that document (`lpa_health_welfare` or `court_order`); the staff member who marks the document received is recorded (migration `trust/0029`) and **a different** staff member must approve; the link lasts **at most a year** (a year by default) and is renewed by a **yearly review** (`POST staff/family-requests/{id}/review {still_in_force: true}`), also for one that just ran out. `trust.adult_reviews_due` counts those due within 30 days. The patient app offers the two relationships with an explanation; the staff app names the document needed, the two-person rule, and has the review button.
- **Since decided and built (DEV-353):** an adult's attorney or deputy may read the person's notes with the notes part of the grant.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_family.py` (the right document, checked by one and approved by another, at most a year; no skipping the check or running past a year, a deputy with a court order; adults only, yearly review, a parent's link has none); family and guardian-scope tests 53 passed; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed; contract, identifier, public-id, prohibition and sandbox tests 68 passed; no missing migrations; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean; the patient and staff apps type-check.

### DEV-353: an adult's attorney or deputy may read the person's notes (LEAD DECISION)

- **Decided by the project lead (20 Nov 2026):** yes, but only with the "notes" permission ticked when staff approve; the same 24-hour delay; a doctor can still keep a single note from them.
- **What was done:** the patient app's notes now also open to a login acting for an adult as **attorney** (health and welfare LPA) or **court deputy** under an active, unexpired link (`trust.adult_proxy_persons`), when the grant includes "notes" (the policy engine requires that part, DEV-324). Everything else is as for a guardian: signed notes only, after 24 hours, never one its author kept from guardians, every read audited as a guardian read. A **carer** of an adult never reads notes, even with "notes" ticked. The staff and patient apps describe the "notes" permission accordingly.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_clinical_notes.py` (an attorney with "notes" reads them and a hidden note stays hidden; a deputy without "notes", or a carer with it, reads nothing); 6 related passed; ruff and mypy clean; the staff and patient apps type-check.

### DEV-354: SendGrid delivery receipts (follows DEV-350)

- **What was done:** each email now carries our own id to SendGrid (`custom_args`, an id and nothing personal), and `POST /api/v1/webhooks/sendgrid/events` takes SendGrid's Event Webhook back. No sign-in: SendGrid's **ECDSA signature** (P-256, SHA-256, over timestamp and body) is the credential, checked with the account's public key (`SENDGRID_WEBHOOK_PUBLIC_KEY`) before the body is read; no key, a timestamp more than 10 minutes off, or a bad signature is refused (401). Only our id and the outcome are used (delivered, bounced, dropped, deferred; opens and clicks are ignored); addresses and everything else in the post are never stored. The outcome and its time are kept on the queued email (migration `foundation/0009`); a final outcome (delivered, bounced, dropped) is never replaced by a later "deferred". The platform's mail numbers now count receipts. The Google Cloud settings take the public key as a plain variable.
- **Checked:** 3 new tests in `test_sendgrid.py` (a signed receipt is recorded and a final one never undone; unknown events and ids ignored; unsigned, stale, tampered or keyless posts refused; the API call carries our id); with the email tests 29 passed; `terraform validate` valid and `fmt -check` clean.

### DEV-355: "show older" on the doctor's referral lists; the full UK Core validator not built (follows DEV-337, DEV-342)

- **What was done:** the doctor app's two referral lists (referrals sent, referrals to answer) load 50 at a time with "Show older referrals"; the newest page still refreshes every 15 seconds, and older pages already loaded stay, without repeats.
- **Also:** the patient app's "My Tests & Requests" loads 50 at a time with "Show older requests". Left as they are, on purpose: the patient's referrals, insurance and claims (loaded with the app's start, and small for any one person), and the staff payments list (the staff app has no screen that shows it).
- **Decided not to build (engineering):** a second, complete UK Core validator. Reading the published lab profiles (DEV-342) showed that the only rules that can refuse a message are the category (enforced, DEV-342, DEV-348) and the subject (enforced); the rest is "must support" or base FHIR, where our checker is already stricter (a fixed list of allowed elements). The remaining assurance should come from the **official HL7 FHIR validator** run against the first partner lab's real sample messages, during that partner's certification (DEV-230).
- **Checked:** the doctor app type-checks; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and contract, identifier, public-id, paging and referral tests 79 passed; no missing migrations; mypy, ruff and import contracts clean.

### DEV-356: the browser robots, all 89, after two days of changes

- **What was found:** a full run of the 25 robot files (89 tests, one at a time, against the running apps) gave 85 passed, 4 failed. Robots 13 (data rights) and 14 (remember me) passed when run again: they failed only while the whole backend test suite was running at the same time and slowed everything. Robots 05 and 19 failed again, both because **earlier robot runs had left data behind**, not because of a fault in the product:
  - **05 (suspending a customer):** the test clinic had open lab requests left by other robots, so the product rightly asked for a **continuity plan** (who looks after those patients) before a suspension; the robot never filled it in. It now always writes one (accepted, and harmless, when none is needed).
  - **19 (a partner developer's key):** a partner may hold at most **10 live keys**; earlier runs had left exactly 10 test keys, so the robot could not add one. The robot now revokes the key it makes at the end, and the 10 leftover "Robot ERP" test keys in the local development database were revoked.
- **Checked:** robots 05 and 19 re-run: 21 passed; robots 13 and 14 passed on their own.
- **The whole backend suite, once:** 3,097 passed, 2 skipped, 1 failed (2 h 40 min). The one failure, `test_platform_work.py::test_a_closed_incident_is_no_longer_counted`, was a **stale test**: closing a SEV-0 or SEV-1 incident has since needed a restoration notice (DEV-202, with its own test), and this older test never sent one, nor checked that the close succeeded, so the refusal looked like "still open". The test now sends the notice and checks the close worked; its file passes (10). With that, the whole backend suite is green.

### DEV-357: "Book again" and an easier way to pick a time (patient app)

- **What was done:** on My Appointments, each past or cancelled appointment has **"Book again"**, and a waiting-list entry whose time has opened up has **"Book this now"**; both open Find Healthcare with that service already chosen and its free times shown. If the service is no longer offered, the patient is told and can search instead. Find Healthcare shows the first **5 days** of free times, with "Show more days" for the rest. Choosing a time scrolls the confirm box into view and moves focus to it; each time button says its full date, time and doctor to screen readers and whether it is chosen. After a request is sent, a link goes to the patient's reminder settings.
- **Not changed:** booking rules, the API and the waiting list itself; this is screen-only.
- **Checked:** the patient app type-checks.

### DEV-358: the clean production image, built and run end to end (backlog A7)

- **What was done:** the production image (`backend/Dockerfile.production`, DEV-196) was built with `--no-cache --pull` (5.5 minutes, no time-outs this time) and then taken through `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` steps 1 to 5 against a throwaway PostgreSQL 16: `create_db_roles.sql`, every migration as the owner role on an empty database, `db_grants --apply` then `--check` ("exactly as listed"), `check --deploy` with no `zoiko.E` errors, and the image's own gunicorn command as the restricted app role. `/healthz` and `/readyz` answered 200, an unsigned API call 401, and plain HTTP was redirected (301) to HTTPS.
- **Found and fixed:**
  - **No `.dockerignore`:** `COPY . .` put the developer's local Windows `.venv` (197 MB) and tool caches into the image; `/app` was 234 MB. `backend/.dockerignore` now leaves out virtual environments, caches, compiled files, `.env` files and keys; `/app` is 15 MB. Development is unaffected (compose mounts `./backend` over `/app`). No `.env` or key file had been copied.
  - **The frame header was never sent:** production set `X_FRAME_OPTIONS = "DENY"`, but Django's `XFrameOptionsMiddleware` was not in the middleware list, so the setting did nothing (`check --deploy` warning W002). Production now adds it right after `SecurityMiddleware`; responses carry `X-Frame-Options: DENY`. A test keeps it there.
- **Left as they are, on purpose:** `check --deploy` still shows two warnings. W003 (no CSRF middleware) does not apply: the API signs in with bearer tokens only, with no sessions or cookies. W021 (HSTS preload) is a domain owner's choice, not ours.
- **The guards behaved as designed along the way:** the app refused to start with the development secret key, the placeholder database password, only local allowed hosts, and a superuser database login (row-level security would be skipped).
- **Checked:** production-settings tests 3 passed; ruff and mypy clean; the development image (`backend/Dockerfile`) also builds with the new `.dockerignore` (`/app` 15 MB, test tools present).

### DEV-359: the fresh-clone check (backlog A9); the webhook sender now guards its start and waits for the database

- **What was done:** the repository was cloned into an empty folder (with the uncommitted DEV-357 and DEV-358 changes on top) and started exactly as `README.md` says: `cp .env.example .env`, a new field key made with the command in that file, `docker compose up --build` (on other ports, under its own project name, so the developer's own stack was untouched). Every migration ran on the empty database, `/healthz` and `/readyz` answered, and the worker, webhook sender and mail inbox came up.
- **Found and fixed:** on that first start the **webhook sender** (`deliver_webhooks`) crashed with a traceback ("relation trust_tenant does not exist") because it started while the migrations were still running; Docker's restart brought it back later. Looking at why showed a second, more important gap: unlike the web server and the outbox worker (DEV-083), it **never ran the start-up guard**, so a webhook sender started on its own (as `docs/DEPLOYMENT.md` tells a non-Google-Cloud deployment to do) would have run with development settings or a database login that skips row-level security, without refusing. On Google Cloud it was covered only by accident: the job runs it after `relay_outbox --once &&`. It now works like the worker: the same guard before its first pass (a refusal stops it with a clear error), a wait-and-retry while the database is not ready (logging the error class only, never its text), and a clean stop on SIGTERM. A fresh start after the change: no tracebacks, the sender waits, then runs.
- **The apps:** each of the four apps was installed as `frontend/README.md` says (`npm install`), then type-checked and built for production: all four pass. **Lock files added** for the patient, platform and doctor apps (`frontend/{patient,platform,clinician}/package-lock.json`): they had none, so only their direct packages were pinned and every fresh install could pick different versions of the packages underneath (the staff console, partner portal and browser robots already had one). The new files come from that clean install, which built; every package resolves from the public npm registry. A plain `npm ci` now works for all of them.
- **Seen, not changed:** `docker-compose.yml` names the image `zoiko-backend:dev`, so a second checkout built on the same machine replaces the first one's image under that name (harmless while both hold the same code). `mypy .` reports 86 errors, all in test files and all already there before this change (the code outside `tests/` is clean, 447 files); earlier "mypy clean" notes meant the files changed.
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_db_role.py` (the sender refuses when the guard refuses and sends nothing; a "not migrated yet" error is retried and its text is not logged); with the webhook tests 63 passed; ruff clean; import contracts kept; no missing migrations.

### DEV-360: CI now checks the five web apps, and type-checks and migration-checks more of the backend

- **What was done:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` had one job, the backend. It now has a second job, **apps**, that runs for each of the five web apps (staff console, patient, platform, doctor, partner portal): `npm ci` (installs exactly what the lock file says, which works for all five since DEV-359 added the missing lock files), `npm run typecheck`, then a production build (`vite build`). One app failing does not hide the others. Before this, nothing outside a developer's own machine checked that the apps type-check or build.
- **The backend job also:** type-checks `api` and `devconsole` (they were left out; both were already clean; since DEV-361 it type-checks everything, tests included), and fails when a model change has no migration (`makemigrations --check`, with the test settings).
- **Left as it is, on purpose:** the browser robots are not in CI: they need the whole stack and seeded data running.
- **Checked:** `actionlint` 1.7.7 (the GitHub Actions linter) finds nothing; each app installed with `npm ci`, type-checked and built from a clean clone; `mypy foundation modules config api devconsole`: no issues in 446 files; `makemigrations --check` in a Linux container against PostgreSQL 16 set up as in CI: "No changes detected". The workflow has not run on GitHub yet: it runs on the next push.

### DEV-361: the tests type-check too; seven test files that could not run on Python 3.10 fixed

- **What was found:** `mypy .` reported 86 errors, all in `tests/`, which CI never type-checked. Most were harmless (a value that may be empty used without checking first), but **19 were real**: seven test files used `datetime.UTC`, which exists only from Python 3.11. The project deliberately stays compatible with 3.10 (`pyproject.toml`: the developer's own machine runs 3.10.0; Docker and CI run 3.12), so those seven files crashed on import when run locally and passed only inside Docker.
- **What was done:** the seven files now use `foundation.timeutil.UTC`, the project's own 3.10-safe constant that the other tests already use. The rest: a check that a value is there before it is used (`first()`, `re.search`, optional dates), the logins some tests keep on the shared test helpers declared on the helper classes (as the existing ones already were), typed request headers, and two loops over mixed bad inputs given a type. No test changed what it checks. CI now type-checks the whole backend, tests included (`mypy .`).
- **Also:** two tests wrote HL7's encoding characters `^~\&` in a plain string (`test_exchange.py`, `test_sandbox.py`); Python keeps the backslash today but warns (`SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence`), and a later Python will refuse it. Both are now raw strings: the same characters, no warning.
- **Checked:** `mypy .`: no issues in 612 files; ruff clean; import contracts kept; the 27 changed test files run once in the Linux image against PostgreSQL 16 set up as in CI: **778 passed** (39 minutes).

### DEV-362: the browser robots run in CI; each checkout gets its own Docker images; the launcher page takes other ports and has the partner logins

- **Robots in CI:** a third CI job, **robots**, does what `frontend/e2e/README.md` describes by hand: copies `.env.example` with a throwaway field key made for that run, starts the development stack, runs the five seed scripts (demo clinic, administrators, platform logins, E2E clinic, partners), installs and starts the five apps on their usual ports, installs Chromium and runs every robot. On a failure it keeps the screenshots, traces and every app and container log for 7 days. Development settings only; nothing real is used. In CI only, a failed robot is tried once more and, if it then passes, is reported as **flaky**, not passed (`playwright.config.ts`); on a developer's machine a failure stays a failure.
- **Why the retry:** a full local run (95 robot tests, 15 minutes) gave 92 passed and 3 failed: robots 05 (suspension), 13 (data rights) and 15 (stale data). Run again on their own, all three passed (4 of 4). Robot 13 failed the same way under load on 19 November (DEV-356). The first failures' screenshots were replaced by the re-run, so the exact errors were not kept.
- **Docker images per checkout:** `docker-compose.yml` no longer names the image `zoiko-backend:dev`; Compose names it after the checkout (`zoikoaccess-backend`, `-worker`, `-webhooks`), so a second checkout on the same machine can no longer replace the first one's image (seen in DEV-359). The worker and webhook sender build the same Dockerfile; the build cache made all three builds take 20 seconds.
- **The launcher page** (`scripts/dev_links.py`): an app on another port is set with the same variables the robots use (`E2E_STAFF` and the others), for when another project holds 5173; and the partner portal with its six logins (lab, pharmacy, home care, payer, employer, partner developer) is now on it: 17 one-click logins in all.
- **Checked:** `actionlint` finds nothing in the workflow; robot 11 opened all 17 one-click logins, each signed in with the password gone from the address bar (18 passed); the full robot run above; `CI=1 npx playwright test --list` loads the CI configuration (95 tests). The robots job has not yet run on GitHub: it runs on the next push.

### DEV-363: the first dependency audit; Vite 5.4.11 to 6.4.6 in the five apps, Playwright 1.49.1 to 1.65.1 for the robots

- **What was found:** `npm audit`, run for the first time, flagged every app and the robots. All of it is in **development tools**, not in what a user receives (a production build is static files):
  - **Vite 5.4.11 and its esbuild** (all five apps): flaws in the development server, for example a web page open in the same browser could read files from `npm run dev`, and several `server.fs.deny` bypasses, some on Windows only; one high (a launch-editor check bypassable on Windows). The 5.x line was not fully fixed: Vite 5.4.21 still had them (they cover every Vite up to 6.4.3).
  - **Playwright 1.49.1** (robots, high): it downloaded browsers without checking the TLS certificate, a supply-chain risk on every CI run.
- **What was done:** Vite **6.4.6** in all five apps (the oldest line that clears everything; npm's own suggestion, Vite 8, is a larger step than needed), which brings esbuild 0.25.12; the React plugin (4.3.4) already supports Vite 6, and the shared Vite settings use nothing Vite 6 changed. Playwright **1.65.1** for the robots. Exact versions, in `package.json` and the lock files.
- **Checked:** `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities in all six folders; each app type-checks and builds; the five apps started on Vite 6.4.6 and robots 06 (every page of every app, crashes and server errors) and 11 (every one-click login) passed: 22 of 22.
- **Learned on the way:** stopping an app's `npm run dev` from a tool can leave Vite itself running and holding the port, so a new copy refuses to start (`--strictPort` did its job) and anything tested meanwhile reaches the OLD copy. The first robot run after the upgrade did exactly that and was discarded; the old processes were stopped and the run repeated.

### DEV-364: the Python audit; `cryptography` 45.0.7 to 50.0.2, pip in the images to 26.x

- **What was found:** `pip-audit`, run for the first time on the backend image, found two packages with known flaws. **`cryptography` 45.0.7** is a runtime library here: it encrypts patient details at rest (`foundation/crypto.py`), checks SendGrid's signed receipts (DEV-354) and signs country packs. One advisory applies directly: before 46.0.5, loading an elliptic-curve public key did not check that the key is valid, and the SendGrid check loads exactly such a key. The wheels before 48.0.1 also bundled a vulnerable OpenSSL; the rest (certificate-chain checks, PKCS#7, a buffer over-read with unusual buffers) touch code we do not use. **pip 25.0.1** (the installer in the image) had flaws of its own.
- **What was done:** `requirements/base.txt` now asks for `cryptography>=50.0.2,<51` (the requirement was `>=43,<46`, which could not take the fix); 50.x still supports Python 3.9 and later, so the 3.10 machines are fine. Both Dockerfiles upgrade pip (`>=26.2,<27`) before installing anything. The parts we use (AES-GCM, HKDF, EC and Ed25519 keys, signature checks) did not change.
- **Checked:** `pip-audit` on the rebuilt image: no known vulnerabilities (cryptography 50.0.2, pip 26.3). On 50.0.2: encryption, key rotation, SendGrid, pack signing and insurance tests 67 passed. Data encrypted under 45.0.7 in the local development database still opens: robots 06 and 11 (every page of every app, every one-click login) 22 passed, and the backend log shows no decryption error. (A first, wider test run on the new image was stopped at 51 per cent by a time limit while the machine ran three test runs; nothing had failed.)

### DEV-365: merging a confirmed duplicate patient record, and undoing it (ported from the maruthi branch, with additions)

- **What was done:** maruthi's identity merge (`82d4824`) brought onto this branch's modules. After reviewers confirm two records are the same person (DEV-180, two reviewers where critical), clinic staff may **merge** them as a separate step, naming which survives (otherwise the older one). Nothing is deleted: the ended record is kept with a pointer to the survivor, and a journal lists every row that moved, so **unmerge** moves back exactly those rows and the case returns to review. Bookings, waiting-list entries, referrals, message threads, notifications and insurance policies move; the clinic connection moves, and the login if only the ended record had one (two records that both have a login are not merged). Access never moves: guardian, attorney and doctor-care grants stay on the ended record and must be approved again. Nobody merges a case about themselves or someone they act for; a merged link cannot be split until it is undone. Staff screen: "Possible duplicates" gets "Merge the two records" and "Undo the merge", with what moved and what did not. Migration `trust/0030` (row-level security on both new tables).
- **Added on this branch, beyond maruthi:**
  - **Clinical records never move** (TEMPORARY, for clinical safety to confirm): clinical notes and clinical references, like bills, claims, data requests, lab exchanges, programme links and insurance-sharing choices, stay on the ended record and are **counted** ("not moved") in the merge result and on the screen. Whether a doctor's notes should follow a merged patient is a clinical-safety decision, not ours.
  - **A record with a death recorded is not merged** (DEV-333).
  - **Every table with a patient reference must say whether it moves**: a test fails if a new one is not classified, so a merge can never silently skip a table.
  - **A gap closed on the way:** the identity-matching events `patient.link.review_required.v1` and `patient.link.corrected.v1` had been published since DEV-180 but were missing from the frozen event catalogue (the type was built with an f-string, which the catalogue check cannot see). The names are now written out in full, and all four `patient.link.*` events (with `merged` and `unmerged`) are catalogued. Adding events is allowed by the frozen contract.
  - The four new reference fields are in the identifier catalogue; the OpenAPI and AsyncAPI snapshots are refreshed.
- **Not ported from maruthi, on purpose:** its wording and code that depend on its retired engine and packs (DEV-211).
