- **Checked:** `test_identity_merge.py` (maruthi's tests and 4 new ones: death refused, kept rows counted and left, every patient table classified, clinical records never moved) with the identity, contract, identifier, database-rights, death and OpenAPI tests: 205 passed, then the identifier, OpenAPI and event-catalogue tests 48 passed after the last fixes; no missing migrations; ruff, mypy (616 files) and import contracts clean; the staff console type-checks; migration applied to the local database and robot 06 (every staff page) passed.

### DEV-366: waiting-list priority and each clinic's own offer window (ported from the maruthi branch, with a change)

- **What was done:** from maruthi's `2bc904e`, onto this branch's waiting list (which already offered freed-up times and expired unused offers). **Priority:** clinic staff set a waiting patient to routine, priority or urgent (`POST staff/waitlist/{id}/priority`); a freed-up time goes to the most urgent first, then to whoever joined first, and the staff list shows each person's place in that same order. Only staff may do it (a patient or guardian cannot rank themselves), only for an entry still waiting or offered, and every change is audited. **A patient never sees where they are ranked.** **Offer window:** each clinic's administrator sets how many hours (1 to 168) a patient has to book an offered time before it goes to the next person, or goes back to the service standard (`staff/settings`, `waitlist_offer_hours`); unused offers now expire on each clinic's own window. Staff screens: "Set priority" on the Waiting list, and an "offer window" card in Settings next to the response target (one card, reused). Migration `waitlist/0003` (row-level security on the new rule table).
- **Changed from maruthi, on purpose:** the reason for a priority is **a code from a fixed list** (clinical urgency, the clinic cancelled their earlier appointment, a long wait, access needs, other), **not free text**. What staff would naturally type ("chest pain") is health information, and this product keeps that only encrypted (DEV-024) or as codes (as for billing reasons and identity-case notes). Maruthi's priority-change event is left out: nobody outside the clinic needs it, and the audit trail already records each change.
- **Checked:** `test_waitlist_rules.py` (maruthi's tests, adapted to the codes: an urgent person is offered first, equal levels keep their order, the staff list shows offer order, words instead of a code refused, patients and other clinics refused, closed entries refused, repeats harmless, audited and the patient not told; the offer window set, reset, refused for non-administrators and bad values, and used for expiry) with the waiting-list, OpenAPI (snapshot refreshed), identifier, settings, reports, death and merge tests: 132 passed; no missing migrations; ruff, mypy (618 files) and import contracts clean; the staff console type-checks; robots 06 (every page) and 10 (accessibility scan of every page) passed, 9 of 9.

### DEV-367: deadlines count only a clinic's opening hours, everywhere (ported from the maruthi branch, and widened)

- **What was done:** `foundation/business_time.py` from maruthi's `2bc904e` (count a number of OPEN minutes forward, skipping nights, closed days and listed holidays, on the clinic's own clock), plus `subtract_business_minutes` (the same backwards). A clinic administrator sets the clinic's working days, opening and closing times and holidays (`GET/POST staff/business-hours`, other staff may read, patients may not; audited; migration `trust/0031`); Settings has an "Opening hours" card, which warns when the clinic's places use different time zones.
- **Wider than maruthi, on purpose:** maruthi used the hours for referral escalation and its own (retired, DEV-211) journey engine. Here EVERY place that decides "late" now asks one of two functions in `catalog` (which owns the clinic's clock): `response_due` (a deadline) and `late_cutoff` (the line for counting late work in the database), plus `is_late`. They are used by referral escalation, the "waiting too long" flag on bookings, the work queue's due times (both the event subscribers and the catch-up job), the dashboard's late counts and the daily late-work email, so all of a clinic's deadlines agree. With no hours set, everything is exactly as before; hours that can no longer be used fall back to every hour rather than stopping deadlines.
- **Not stretched:** a data request's legal deadline from a signed country pack stays in calendar days.
- **Fixed on the way:** the dashboard's late-bookings count counted the bookings of ALL of a customer's clinics on one clinic's dashboard (its messages and data-requests counts were already per clinic); it is now that clinic's own.
- **Checked:** `test_business_time.py` (maruthi's 32 arithmetic tests and 4 new ones for counting back, including 500 random round trips across a year with both UK clock changes) and `test_business_hours.py` (the screen's rules from maruthi; referral escalation by open hours; the late line agrees with `is_late` either side of it; no hours means no change; the late count follows the clinic's line; unusable stored hours fall back); with the attendance, attention, country-deadline, digest, late, messaging, referral, response-target, settings, staff-API, today, work-queue, OpenAPI and identifier tests: 353 passed, then the two new tests that had wrong assumptions (one about a closed clinic's deadline, one passing a whole slot instead of its id) fixed and passed; ruff, mypy (624 files) and import contracts clean; robots 06 and 10 passed.

### DEV-368: an insurance status page for clinic staff (ported from the maruthi branch, with a fix)

- **What was done:** maruthi's "Billing & Claims overview" (`0629359`) as a staff **Insurance status** page (`GET staff/insurance-status`, `api/insurance.py`; this branch already has its own Billing and Claims pages). For each of the clinic's own patients (DEV-107: the new `trust.people_at_site`, the same rule as `person_at_site`, for many at once): insured, ending within 30 days, no insurance in force, or **not shared**. A patient who has not shared their insurance with staff (DEV-096) shows only that, never whether they have any; member numbers only as their last four characters. An "Ask before the visit" list names the patients with a visit still to come whose insurance is missing, ending soon or not shared.
- **Fixed from maruthi:** it found "a visit still to come" by listing the clinic's bookings, capped at 200 and sorted OLDEST first, so in a busy clinic every upcoming visit could fall outside the list and nobody would be flagged. `scheduling.patients_with_upcoming_visits` now asks the database directly.
- **Checked:** `test_insurance_status.py` (maruthi's 15 tests on the new address, and 1 new one: a past visit is not upcoming) with the insurance tests: 42 passed; the OpenAPI snapshot refreshed, adding only `/staff/insurance-status`; the staff console type-checks; robots 06 (every page, including the new one) and 10 (accessibility) passed.

### DEV-369: an HL7 v2 result with a missing or unknown status is refused, no longer taken as "final" (safety fix)

- **What was found:** the design-document audit (GHE-22 AT-015, GHE-08 H03/T03, GHE-07 I-04) found that `modules/integration/hl7.py` read the report status (OBR-25) and each value's status (OBX-11) with a default of "final": a missing or unrecognised code became a finished result. A preliminary or unknown result could then reach the treating doctor as final.
- **What was done:** a missing or unknown status now raises the reader's own error (`Hl7Error`), so the message is **held** for a person exactly like any other unreadable HL7 message (DEV-340); every known code (F, C, A, P, X, W, R, I, any case) reads as before.
- **Found by the same audit, fixed as DEV-370:** a FHIR report marked preliminary was delivered like a final one.
- **Checked:** `test_hl7.py` 12 passed (new: unknown and missing report status refused, unknown and missing value status refused, every known status still reads); the HL7 and sandbox end-to-end tests 7 passed.

### DEV-370: a preliminary lab result is shown labelled but can never be marked reviewed (TEMPORARY: for the clinical safety lead to confirm)

- **What was found:** the design-document audit (GHE-08 AT14 and H03/T03, GHE-07 I-04) found that a FHIR DiagnosticReport marked `preliminary`, `registered` or `partial` was delivered exactly like a final one, so a doctor could mark it reviewed and, a day later, the patient would see it as a finished result (DEV-332).
- **Decided (by the user, 23 Nov 2026), with the trade-off stated:** holding a preliminary result for a person would be simpler, but a dangerous preliminary value would then wait in a queue instead of alerting the doctor. So a preliminary result is **delivered, labelled, and its critical alert goes out at once**, but it **cannot be marked reviewed** until the lab sends the final one; because patients see values only after a review, it never reaches the patient as finished. To be confirmed by the clinical safety lead.
- **What was done:** `ExchangeRequest.result_preliminary` and `ExchangeResultVersion.preliminary` (migration `exchange/0011`). The intake marks `registered`, `partial` and `preliminary` (FHIR) and, through the HL7 reader, `P`, `R` and `I` as preliminary. The doctor's review step refuses a preliminary result (409, field `preliminary`). The next report replaces a preliminary one as a new version through the correction path (DEV-325): the final one with the new reason "final", a newer preliminary one as "amended", still preliminary; every earlier version is kept with its own preliminary mark. A report with status `unknown` or none is held for a person (as DEV-369 does for HL7). The doctor app shows "PRELIMINARY: not final" and explains why it cannot be reviewed yet.
- **Checked:** 5 new end-to-end tests in `test_exchange.py` (a preliminary report is shown, labelled, cannot be reviewed and is not shown to the patient; partial then preliminary then final become versions 1 to 3 and the final can be reviewed; a preliminary critical value still alerts at once; unknown and missing status held); with the FHIR API, FHIR intake (two exact-argument checks updated) and HL7 tests: 61 passed, then the intake file 13 passed; robots 21 (partner exchange), 23 (doctor order) and 06 passed, 7 of 7; ruff, mypy (625 files) and import contracts clean; no missing migrations; the doctor app type-checks.

### DEV-371: supply-chain checks in CI: secret scan, dependency audits, a software bill of materials

- **Why:** three of the design-document audits (GHE-09 SDLC-01/04/06, GHE-18 B-02 to B-04, GHE-19 V-H02/H03) found no supply-chain checks in CI; the dependency audits of DEV-363 and DEV-364 were done by hand.
- **What was done:** a new CI job, **security**: (1) a **secret scan of every commit** (gitleaks 8.18.4, the whole history, so a secret removed later still counts); (2) **Python dependencies** checked against known flaws (`pip-audit`, all three requirement files); (3) the **browser robots' npm packages** audited; (4) a **software bill of materials** in CycloneDX, kept 90 days with each run: the backend's from a clean install of exactly the production requirements (with versions), and one per app from its lock file (`npm sbom`). The **apps** job now also fails on any npm package with a known high or critical flaw (`npm audit --audit-level=high`). Code scanning for Python was already there: ruff's "S" rules are Bandit's security checks.
- **Five findings, checked by hand and excused one by one:** gitleaks flagged five values, all deliberate fakes in tests (three that must look real for the production start-up check, one for the log-redaction filter, one dummy partner secret). They are listed in `.gitleaksignore` by commit, file, rule and line, not by folder, so a new secret anywhere, tests included, still fails.
- **Not done, needs something outside the code:** signing images and build provenance (needs the container registry of a deployed environment, GO-LIVE-CHECKLIST), and an admission rule that runs only signed images (Cloud Run, once applied).
- **Checked:** gitleaks over all 42 commits: no leaks once the five are listed; the working-tree scan found only the same five and third-party files in a local `.venv` (not in git). In a clean Python 3.12 container: `pip-audit` no known vulnerabilities; the backend SBOM lists 13 installed packages with versions. `npm audit`: 0 for the robots and the apps; the patient app's SBOM lists 122 components. `actionlint` finds nothing. The job runs on GitHub with the next push.

### DEV-372: patient longitudinal result trends and sparkline visualization (follows DEV-330, DEV-332, DEV-339)

- **Why:** `docs/HANDOVER.md` explicitly noted that while doctors could see measured values and historical trends across results (DEV-330, DEV-339), patients could previously view only single-result values with no historical trend context across their care journey.
- **What was done:** `exchange.patient_value_trend` in `modules/exchange/services.py` and endpoint `GET /api/v1/exchange/mine/<request_id>/trend?key=<key>` (`views.exchange_mine_trend`). It returns historical points for a test (matched by SNOMED code or case-insensitive name) across the patient's results at the clinic, oldest first. **Clinical safety & privacy protections:** it strictly queries only results where `_patient_may_see_values` is true (i.e. reviewed by a doctor, 24-hour review embargo has elapsed, and not preliminary); unreviewed, in-embargo, or preliminary results are omitted; audited as `exchange.patient_trend_read`.
- **Frontend (Patient app):** updated `frontend/patient/src/pages/MyRequests.tsx` with an accessible `Sparkline` component and "Trend" toggle buttons on numeric values in the measured values table, showing the date, value, unit, and result label over time with normal range bounds.
- **Checked:** 4 new tests in `backend/tests/test_patient_trend.py` covering embargo filtering, preliminary exclusion, case-insensitive name matching, key validation, and API authentication; OpenAPI snapshot refreshed; consumer contracts verified (52 patient routes); patient frontend type-checks cleanly (`npx tsc --noEmit`).
- **Follow-up (24 Nov, found when the end-to-end tests were run):** (1) the end-to-end test asked for the trend by the name "Haemoglobin" of a value that has a SNOMED CT code, and expected 135 where its data says 128; a value with a code is matched by its code (by name only when it has none, as for the doctor's trend, DEV-339), so the test now asks by code, expects 128, and also checks a value without a code by name. (2) The endpoint accepted a result the patient may not see yet as its starting point (the points were still filtered, so nothing leaked); it now answers "not found" for it, as the patient's own values endpoint does. (3) Eleven style findings (line length, an unused import and variable) that CI would have failed on were fixed. End-to-end trend test and the 4 unit tests pass.

### DEV-373: a doctor's review names the version of the result they read

- **Why:** the design-document audit (GHE-08 AT11, AT18, AT19, AT27) found the review was not tied to what the doctor read: a doctor could open version 1, a correction (version 2) could arrive meanwhile, and their "I have reviewed this" would be recorded against version 2, which they never saw.
- **What was done:** `POST exchange/requests/{id}/review` now requires `{"version": n}`, the version on the doctor's screen (missing or not a whole number above 0: 422); any version but the current one is refused (409, field `version`). The version reviewed is kept on the request (`reviewed_version`, migration `exchange/0012`) and cleared when a correction comes in. The doctor app sends the version it shows (the one opened, else the one in the list) and, on a refusal, says the result changed or is not final yet and to read the current one. The "live credential" part of the audit item was already met: every review re-runs the access policy at that moment (the doctor role and an active care relationship), so a doctor whose access ended cannot review.
- **Not done (needs a decision):** a signed reviewer attestation and a check against a professional register (the licence source is open, DEV-016 and question 25).
- **Checked:** 2 new tests in `test_exchange.py` (a review must name a version, and a wrong one is refused; a correction arriving while the doctor reads cannot be reviewed unseen, and the evidence names version 2); every existing review in the tests now names its version; the exchange, FHIR intake and patient-trend tests: 127 passed, the one failure being DEV-372's test above, then fixed; robots 23 (a doctor orders and reviews in the real app) and 21 passed; ruff, mypy (627 files) and import contracts clean; the doctor app type-checks.

### DEV-374: patient online bill payment and payment history (follows DEV-139)

- **Why:** `docs/HANDOVER.md` and `frontend/patient/src/pages/Bills.tsx` previously stated: *"You cannot pay here: the clinic takes payment and marks the bill as paid."* While the backend payment engine (`modules.billing.payments` and `views.my_invoice_payments`) was built in DEV-139, the patient frontend lacked interactive checkout, payment status refresh, and payment attempt history.
- **What was done:**
  - **Patient App (`frontend/patient/src/pages/Bills.tsx`):**
    - Enabled interactive **"Pay <amount>"** button for issued invoices with an outstanding balance.
    - Added automated client-side idempotency key generation (`pay-<invoice>-<timestamp>`), calling `POST /billing/invoices/<id>/payments`.
    - Integrated real-time payment attempt outcome handling: instantaneous invoice resolution to "Paid" upon payment success, or clear actionable error messaging when declined.
    - Added a collapsible **Payment history** drawer per invoice displaying past attempts (`amount`, `provider`, `status`, and `provider_ref`) with a **Check status** button calling `POST /billing/payments/<id>/refresh` for pending/unknown states.
  - **API Client (`frontend/patient/src/api.ts`):** Exported `PaymentAttempt` interface and methods `getInvoicePayments`, `payInvoice`, and `refreshPayment`.
  - **Settings (`backend/config/settings/dev.py`):** Enabled default `BILLING_PAYMENT_PROVIDER = env("BILLING_PAYMENT_PROVIDER", "pretend")` in development so local dev environments can test online checkout with the deterministic pretend provider without manual configuration.
- **Checked:**
  - Consumer contracts verified: patient routes increased from 52 to 55 (`python scripts/consumer_contract.py --check` clean).
  - TypeScript compilation in `frontend/patient` verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (0 errors).
  - Backend payment test suite verified: all 13 tests in `tests/test_payments.py` passed in Docker with test settings.
  - OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and verified (`pytest backend/tests/test_openapi.py`: 7 passed).

### DEV-376: consultation notes: clinical document and media attachments (follows DEV-304, DEV-345)

- **Why:** `docs/HANDOVER.md` under "What is not built (Clinical)" listed "attachments". Clinicians writing consultation notes need to attach relevant clinical documents (PDF referral letters, imaging summaries) and clinical media (PNG/JPEG ECG traces, wound photos) during visit consultations, sealed upon signing, encrypted at rest, virus-scanned, and made visible to patients after the 24-hour review embargo.
- **What was done:**
  - **Database & Model (`modules/clinicalnote/models.py`, migration `0006_noteattachment`):** Added `NoteAttachment(CellScopedModel)` with `filename`, `content_type`, `size`, `sha256`, `content_enc` (encrypted base64 ciphertext), `scan_state` (`pending`, `clean`, `infected`), `scanned_at`, and `uploaded_by_ref`.
  - **Security, Validation & Virus Scanning (`modules/clinicalnote/services.py`):**
    - File validation (`_detect_type`): validates file magic bytes, allowing only PDF (with `_ACTIVE_PDF` protection against active JS/launch actions), PNG, and JPEG. Size capped at 10 MB (`MAX_ATTACHMENT_BYTES`). Max 5 attachments per note (`MAX_ATTACHMENTS = 5`).
    - Virus scanning (`_apply_scan`): automatically checks uploads using `scanner.scan(data)`. Infected files are immediately stripped of ciphertext (`content_enc = ""`) and blocked from opening.
    - Context-bound envelope encryption: files are encrypted with context `f"note:{note.public_id}:attachment:{a.public_id}"`. Plaintext is never stored in DB or logged.
    - Sealing upon signing: attachments can only be added or removed while the note is in `DRAFT` state. Attempting to add or delete attachments from a signed note returns 409 CONFLICT_VERSION.
    - Preservation across note corrections (`amend`): clean attachments are automatically decrypted and re-sealed into the new correction version.
    - Patient embargo (`patient_open_attachment`): patients and guardians can only download attachments after the 24-hour review embargo has elapsed, and only if clean.
    - NHS retention erasure: all `content_enc` are cleared to `""` when the retention period expires.
  - **Background Worker (`jobs.py`, `apps.py`):** Registered periodic job `clinicalnote.scan_attachments` every 60 seconds to scan any pending attachments.
  - **API Endpoints (`backend/api/views.py`, `backend/api/urls.py`):**
    - `POST /api/v1/clinician/notes/<note_id>/attachments`: doctor uploads attachment to draft note.
    - `GET /api/v1/clinician/notes/<note_id>/attachments/<attachment_id>`: download attachment for clinician (clean scan only).
    - `DELETE /api/v1/clinician/notes/<note_id>/attachments/<attachment_id>`: delete attachment from draft note.
    - `GET /api/v1/my/notes/<note_id>/attachments/<attachment_id>`: patient/guardian downloads attachment after 24h review embargo.
  - **Clinician Frontend (`frontend/clinician`):**
    - `types.ts`: added `NoteAttachment` interface and `attachments?: NoteAttachment[]` on `Note`.
    - `api.ts`: exported `uploadNoteAttachment`, `deleteNoteAttachment`, and `downloadNoteAttachment`.
    - `pages/Notes.tsx`: added `AttachmentsEditor` for drafts (upload/remove) and `AttachmentsList` for signed/view mode (download).
  - **Patient Frontend (`frontend/patient`):**
    - `api.ts`: added `MyNoteAttachment` interface, `attachments?: MyNoteAttachment[]` on `MyNote`, and `downloadMyNoteAttachment`.
    - `pages/MyNotes.tsx`: displays attached documents and media with download buttons once the 24-hour review embargo has elapsed.
- **Checked:**
  - 42 tests in `backend/tests/test_clinical_notes.py` passed in Docker (100% success).
  - Both `frontend/clinician` and `frontend/patient` type-check cleanly (`npx tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors).
  - Consumer contracts verified: clinician routes 35, patient routes 55 (`python scripts/consumer_contract.py --check` clean).
  - OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and verified (`pytest backend/tests/test_openapi.py`: 7 passed).

### DEV-375: clinical notes: structured active medications and dosages (follows DEV-304, DEV-344)

- **Why:** `docs/HANDOVER.md` under "What is not built (Clinical)" listed "diagnosis and medicine fields". Following DEV-344 (which added coded diagnoses), consultation notes needed structured active medications and dosages (`medications`) recorded by doctors during visits, sealed upon signing, encrypted at rest, and made visible to patients after the 24-hour review embargo. Prescribing/e-prescribing is strictly excluded.
- **What was done:**
  - **Database & Model (`modules/clinicalnote/models.py`, migration `0005_medications`):** Added `medications_enc = models.TextField(blank=True, default="")` to `ClinicalNote`.
  - **Security & Clinical Safety Service (`modules/clinicalnote/services.py`):**
    - `MAX_MEDICATIONS = 10`, `MEDICATION_STATUS = ("active", "suspended", "stopped")`.
    - Implemented `_clean_medications(value)`: strictly validates dm+d / SNOMED CT concept IDs using `foundation.fhir.sctid_ok` (Verhoeff check digit), enforces 1-120 char name, 0-120 char dose, fixed status enum, forbids duplicate concept codes and unrecognized properties.
    - Context-bound envelope encryption: `_seal_medications(note, items)` encrypts with context `f"note:{note.public_id}:medications"`. Plaintext is never stored in DB or logged.
    - Sealing upon signing, automatic preservation across note amendments (`amend`), and immutable audit trail.
    - Patient embargo (`_patient_sees`): medications are only returned to the patient after the 24-hour clinician review embargo has passed.
    - Retention expiration (`erase_expired`): clears `medications_enc = ""` along with body and diagnoses once the NHS retention period ends.
  - **API Views (`backend/api/views.py`):** `clinician_notes` (POST draft) and `clinician_note_step` (POST edit) now accept `medications` and forward to `create_draft` and `edit_draft`.
  - **Clinician Frontend (`frontend/clinician`):**
    - `types.ts`: added `Medication` interface (`code`, `name`, `dose`, `status`) and `medications?: Medication[]` on `Note`.
    - `api.ts`: updated `createNote` and `editNote` with optional `medications`.
    - `pages/Notes.tsx`: added `MedicationsEditor` for drafts and `MedicationsList` for signed/view mode.
  - **Patient Frontend (`frontend/patient`):**
    - `api.ts`: added `MyMedication` interface and `medications?: MyMedication[]` on `MyNote`.
    - `pages/MyNotes.tsx`: displays active medications with dosage and status pills (`active`, `paused / on hold`, `stopped`).
- **Checked:**
  - 40 tests in `backend/tests/test_clinical_notes.py` passed with 100% success (including validation of dm+d concept IDs, max 10, status enum, encryption at rest, amendment propagation, patient embargo, and retention erasure).
  - Both `frontend/clinician` and `frontend/patient` type-check cleanly (`npx tsc --noEmit`: 0 errors).
  - Consumer contracts verified (`python scripts/consumer_contract.py --check` clean).
  - OpenAPI snapshot refreshed and verified (`pytest backend/tests/test_openapi.py`: 7 passed).
